Pan's Shenaniganery

Zweihorn

Summary:

Short stories I've written for/about my DnD Character, level 8+ Monk, certified chaos gremlin "Pan".

Honestly, this work has everything except actual explicit content. There's no telling what is actual canon in our home game and what isn't. This is the work where everything is made up and consistency doesn't matter.

The stories are ordered after when I wrote them, so connecting stories might not follow each other (looking at you Equadorius).

Chapter 1: Backstory

Summary:

The (almost) original backstory to Pan, the main source of trouble.

Notes:

21 Oct 2022 - 1185 words

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

Pan is a younger man with bronze skin, deep brown eyes and short brown hair. He tries to be something that isn't much to look at but, despite his scrawny appearance, if you clean him up a bit, he is quite pretty, with clever eyes and smooth skin. And if you then put him in some expensive clothes, he almost would fit well into high society.

Only once one hears his full name you would guess he actually belongs there: Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd, Eleventh Lord of Millguard and second of his name.

First of the name had been his grand-father four times over (the sixth Lord).

Pan never had much interest in the wealth of his parents. He was tired of the banquets, audiences, pretended niceties and empty words. One could only do so much with money.

His parents put a lot of money into his education, physical and mental, to keep him happy and most importantly busy, so he didn’t wreck some part of the mansion in boredom. He most enjoyed the lessons he could use in normal life – so not the ones his parents wanted him to internalize like social etiquette and court protocol. A good amount of lessons were aimed at eating up his never-ending amount of energy, and many masters have taught Pan over the years in different fighting styles, both armed and unarmed. The teachings of the old halfling Monk Xieni Tenspirit were most fulfilling for him, so eventually his parents only kept her on payroll.

On the mental side, his parents tried honing many different skills, but their fight against his tendency towards trickery and chaos was never won. They succeeded in teaching him calligraphy, history and a lot about nature. Their only real downfalls were religion and languages.

 

His parents (Taraxacum-Isteris and Salixia Jadari) are the Duke and Duchess of Millguard, a larger settlement in the Southeast of Algoria. It’s near the ocean and thriving thanks to the local agriculture and the export of crops mostly by ship. The city also harbors many different craftsmen, many of high renown as the Jazrueltehds invested in them moving there. Nevertheless a lot of the people are working the fields, as even that is quite lucrative there. Pan’s parents try to keep the poverty low and supply the villagers when necessary. Most of the taxes travel back to the community and only a small amount goes into the family’s pockets. As the Duchess was a powerful negotiator, she mostly grew the family’s wealth by trading.

The city is rarely involved in conflict, acting peacefully towards others but still capable of defending itself when threatened. Once upon a time the city had been encircled by a wall but with the population growing the wall had eventually been torn down. Now the city stretches east to the smaller town of Millcoast which is defined by its harbor and shipyard.

 

At seventeen Pan left to travel the world. He dislikes showing and using his heritage, only introducing himself as Pan, Thanis or whatever name he came up with. This has two reasons: 1) he doesn’t want to get pulled back into high society and 2) he doesn’t want people to make a connection between him and his family lest he sully their good name.

Only in very rare cases he pulls out the signet ring and his pedigree to get an audience with whatever important people he needs things from, but in these cases he cleans himself up first, making him almost unrecognizable to his daily, adventuring self.

Most of the time his clothing isn’t anything to look at: Cotton pants and shirt, a leather west and a fur cloak making him look twice his size. He carries a simple-looking longsword, the shaft wrapped tightly with cloth looking like it has to be wrapped. Unwrapping the shaft would reveal high quality craftsmanship and the family crest, as well as Pan’s initials obscured by ornaments. The sword is perfectly balanced, never dulls thanks to magic and Pan never distances himself from it more than a few meters, never letting it out of his field of vision.

Furthermore, Pan wears a small key strung onto a necklace. The key is for a box he keeps in a sewed off pocket in his bag. The box itself looks like a worn wood box, but that is only the outer layer. It is actually made of hardened steel and is only barely breakable. Inside of it is his signet ring hidden. His parents have a spare key so in case he ever lost it he only had to write home to get it.

In his bag one can find a set clothes from the finer side, but they were stuffed in the bottom and rarely used. They aren’t really clothes for a noble, but certainly a step up from his usual attire for sure. In the rare occasions he would need something fitting a noble, he would find some to rent out. Then one can find a box with different tinker’s tools: some sewing needles and thread, a whetstone, pliers, and so on. These were used often and replaced just as much. And tucked in a side pocket is a ring with thieves’ tools.

There is another box, bigger, with the format of sheets. The case looks nicely made but worn. The front once had some decorative carving but by now one could only guess the decoration. Inside the case are clean papers, a simple quill – often replaced – a small bottle with ink, and a block of wax to seal letters. If Pan wanted he could mold the wax with the signet ring to create official documents, but he rarely did. Pan’s writing can be incredibly neat like the writing of scribes, and sometimes Pan takes on work as a scribe, copying books and writing official letters, but since he considered it incredibly boring, there were rare occasions.

 

He often gets into trouble for shenanigans which are never too serious, but nevertheless he has spent the odd night in a dungeon. People also sometimes grow suspicious of him when they start looking through his pretense of lower to middle class origin. Once his lie about not having a last name got him into trouble with a ring of bandits who thought he was a spy after seeing him giving up a letter to the Jazrueltehd-family – even if he was just writing his parents at the time.

Pan regularly writes his parents, every other week he tries to send a letter and sometimes some of his loot back to them. A lot of times Pan is stingy on the details, and paints a better picture of what happened than the truth. This way his parents know approximately what he is up to and where. And the same way he knows the most important things happening in Millguard.

 

Pan comes home every winter for the winter solstice (21st) and then his birthday (25th). Since his 16th birthday his parents also host a ball two days after his birthday to find him a wife. It hasn’t worked out yet.

Notes:

Pan started out as a very simple idea "Monk but noble", since normally Monks are of the outlander or hermit background. I originally created him as a level 8 character (since we around that level in our campaign). Mechanically he is a Kensei Monk, definitely good aligned and at heart he will always be chaotic.
Since I created him, he lives rent free in my head, and he is a monster, but I love him to pieces.

Chapter 2: Pan gets recruited

Summary:

Pan gets recruited into the local adventuring guild

Notes:

23 Oct 2022 - 2335 words

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

»You’ll get in here when you bring us the treasure at the bottom of Rosend Depths«, the guy said, thinking he could scare me off. But that only showed that he didn’t know me. And I knew where Rosend Depths-Lake was.

On the way there I put my bag in the inn I was renting a room at. I only took my clothes and my sword, and made my way to the lake.

 

The lake was fairly big on the surface but it was deep. And I was fast, even in the water.

Arriving at the lake, I put down my cloak and within a couple of seconds I was in the middle of it. I took a deep breath like Xieni had taught me and dove down.

It got dark quickly and I didn’t have a light but when the Kraken opened its eye everything was bright as daylight.

I hadn’t known there was a Kraken so my first thought was: »Oh shit!«

My second thought was: »Oh shiny!«, when I saw a golden, mechanical orb sinking to the ground after it had lain on the Kraken previously. Quickly I dove after it and just managed to pocket it before it could vanish into the darkness once again.

But just as I wrapped my fingers around the thing, the Kraken wrapped one of it’s arms around my leg. The orb landed in my pocket and in a smooth motion I drew my sword and ducked under another tentacle. Then I turned around and my sword cut clean through the arm holding me. My next strike managed to hit a sensitive nerve point which stuns the creature. I use the time to dash upwards.

The lake water had gotten muddied by the movement of the giant beast so I couldn’t see the tentacle that suddenly hit me with such force I was catapulted out of the water faster than I could have ever swam. But about as equally quick I hit the water surface again and that hurt.

Again the Kraken wrapped his arm around me, this time around my stomach, and pulled me under. And like the idiot I was, I inhaled a bunch of water.

A scuffle broke out in which I managed to get free but not out of the water despite trying. Eventually it was the Kraken that kicked me out: Instead of it a vertical throw, it was a horizontal one and a second later I crashed against a tree standing a bit further away. The next thing I did was to loose consciousness.

 

I woke in a dimly lit room. I sat up and gathered my bearings: my head hurt like after a night with dwarven spirit but otherwise I seemed unhurt. The orb from the bottom of the lake was still in my pocket but… My sword was gone.

Immediately I jumped up and the lights got brighter. Magic. Great.

The room had a desk in the middle with a chair on either side. Additionally there was the couch I had rested on and next to the door hung my coat on a coat rack

My sword was not with it – or anywhere in the room for that matter.

I tried the door but it was locked. So I sat down on the chair that faced the door, crossed my arms, put my feet on the table and waited. I was sure, the mages would know I was awake and would check on me soon.

 

Sure enough, a few minutes later, the door opened. A woman of around 70 years – human ones – entered. She had white hair tied in a braid that went over her shoulder to the front and down to her hip. Her clothes were of the ‘I’m an adventurer and I’m special’-category and she wore an eye patch.

»You took quite the slamming against that tree«, she said instead of a greeting. From the tone it was clear that she wanted more than just payment for the healing she had done and an explanation of what I had done in the lake in the first place.

»Yeah, the Kraken in the lake didn’t like that I took his toy«, I took my boots from the table.

The woman wasn’t surprised by the mention of the Kraken – what a disappointment.

»You were lucky«, she answered and came towards me.

»And who are you to assume that about me?«, my tone was friendly and I tilted my head to the side a bit.

»I am called Madame Kambera in these parts. And you can thank me for keeping that Kraken fed otherwise you would be his meal right now.«

»Thanks, I guess«, I leaned back on my chair: »And what made you lock me in this room?«

She smiled – a dangerous smile – and sat down across from me.

»You have talent, kid«, she began and suddenly slapped my sword on the table. I didn’t hide my surprise and disapproval.

»So you lock me in and take my stuff?«, I wanted to reach for it but she pulled it back. Her hands were strong and battle-forged.

»I had to make sure you wouldn’t just up and vanish, kid.«

»Pan!«, I barked annoyed, »Since you obviously want something you can at least try and treat me with some respect!«

She made an apologizing hand motion and bowed her head in agreement; »You’re right, Pan. I do have an offer for you.«

I looked at her unconvinced.

»From your fight with the Kraken I can see your potential. I could use someone like you...«

I kept looking unconvinced, my arms crossed again.

»I’m part of a powerful organization dedicated on putting the connection between the planes back in order. You seem like someone who wants to fill his life with interesting experiences. You won’t get better anywhere else. Plus, you’ll have a place to return to; people gathering you when you crash against a tree next time; all the equipment you like; a stronger sword –«

I had to chuckle now. She half ignored it and finished: »How does that sound, Pan?«

She made the mistake of leaning back and leaving the sword on the table. In a quick motion I grabbed it and stood.

»Sounds like bullshit«, I answered, fixing it on my belt again. The cloth was untouched – good. I continued speaking with little to no pause: »Listen, do I look like my life bores me? Do you think that I actually want a group to hold me back? Like I am unhappy with my equipment? Your gang sounds nice and all but you don’t have anything that I would want.«

Kambera now rose too: »Look, kid – Pan. You would be better off with us. And if riches is what you want –«

At that I actually started laughing. »Thanks. But no. It’s not going to happen.«

And then I walked past the table and put on my cloak again: »I have everything I want. Have fun with your group but I am rather free.«

Kambera smiled: »You prefer cheap inn beds and rations over something proper? You don’t know real life, Pan. We can provide that.«

»You don’t know me, woman«, I answered factually and opened the door that was still unlocked. »Great sales pitch. You just got the wrong person.«

And with that I was out of the door and confidently walked down the unfamiliar hallway.

 

I made my way past a couple closed doors before coming to a crossroad. I took the one hall I could see the most open doors in. And as I passed I looked inside the rooms: there was a path to some arena; there was a dining hall and a kitchen.

The last door lead to a blacksmith’s workshop. As I looked inside my gaze was caught by a mechanical wyvern laying sprawled out on a desk. A shoulder of clockwork was exposed and a person was hunched over the creature, fiddling with tiny tools inside of it.

»I told you to keep still, you don’t want to have the gears spring out again, do you?«, the person told the wyvern. As a response the wyvern clicked, somehow sounding annoyed.

The person looked up and seemed surprised to see me. It was a female elf with long, brown hair and a golden, dangling earring, a jeweler's lens covering one eye.

»Oh, sorry, I didn’t hear you come in. If you’re looking for Iron, she is getting some snacks«, she said and took off the magnifying glass.

»Uhm no. I am just talking a look around.«

»I don’t think we have met yet, I’m Arkaniest«, she got up for a moment and we shook hands.

»I go by Pan; nice to meet you.«

»Pan? Huh. I haven’t heard of you yet. But I am still learning everyone’s names«, she said as she sat down again.

»Yeah, don’t worry about it. What are you working on?«, I asked before she noticed I didn’t belong.

»Ticker’s shoulder is making problems again. This one gear keeps grinding off. I can’t fixate it because it has to move along but that way it always springs out and the teeth break off«, she starts explaining and with a pair of tweezers pointed at one gear.

I had little knowledge about clockwork-things but it had always looked really cool so I had watched the clock maker at home many times.

»I can’t really say I know much about mechanics but … if I remember right, Moe would sometimes put something like rubber over gears like that.«

»Rubber? I haven’t heard of that yet«, she sounded dismissive.

»He said it would give the gear more grip but at the same time allow it to move better – like around, left and right. But you need to make sure to keep it can still turn properly so it doesn’t grind on the stick in the middle«, I pointed to the center rod.

Arkaniest looked at me doubting, put on her eye glass again and bowed over the wyvern. I watched her for a bit as she started to inspect the mechanism around it. But a few moments later I began looking at the other things in the workshop: blades of all kinds laid between unfinished household objects like pans and pots. There were chains of different sizes, and even some jewelry. That blacksmith working here seemed to know what they were doing.

»That might actually work!«, Arkaniest shouted excited and the moment I turned to her, the clockwork suddenly jumped out and into her face. She cursed and immediately started gathering the parts that had fallen off the table.

»You wouldn’t believe how often that happens to me.«

»Oh, I do. Every third clock Moe opened exploded in his face. Something about tension and springs?«, I helped her gather the cogs that had rolled in my direction.

»Exactly that. Thank you«, she said as I handed her some.

While reaching under some shelf to pull out a cog and some spiderwebs my eyes fell on a bottle of sword-oil in the back of the lowest shelf. I took that out too and uncorked it after getting up. The smell was familiar reminding me of Brassy’s workshop back home.

»Do you mind if I used some?«, I asked Arkaniest who was lining up all the parts.

»It’s Iron’s but I don’t think she’ll mind.«

I nodded and took my sword out, putting it on the whetstone. Looking over the sword I thankfully couldn’t find any damage from the Kraken fight. Then I took a piece of cloth and started to apply the oil diligently.

»Who are you?«, another woman suddenly asked. She was an elf as well and had strong arm that were currently carrying a plate of sandwiches. That must be Iron, I thought.

Arkaniest looked up too, confused.

»I’m Pan«, I smiled and waved lightly.

»You don’t know him?«, Arkaniest asked confused.

»No. Are you new in the organization?«, Iron asked me.

»Uhm...«, I didn’t really get time to speak. Iron had set down the plate and as she came closer she started shouting: »You’re using the last bottle of Dihkaor Baltufk’s sword-oil!«,

So it was from Brassy!

»Do you know how hard it is to get that?! He doesn’t sell that to anyone and you’re just using it for your –«, her rant came to an abrupt stop as she lifts up the tip of the sword and

became wide eyed.

»Oh! That's good work...«, she muttered and tried to take the sword from me, pushing me aside. But I didn't just let go of my sword.

»Sorry for using the oil, I can pay you for it, although I just took a bit.«

»Shush, let me look at the sword«, she commanded and tried once more to push me away again. Careful not to cut her, I pulled it back and sheath the blade.

»Sorry, it's mine.«

Iron looked at me with sad puppy eyes.

»I could also write down a recipe for a sword oil, but I don't know if it would compare to your Dihkaor’s or what was his name?« It would be exactly like his recipe but I couldn’t really tell her that, could I?

She looked doubtful: »No, it’s fine. To be honest, I doubt it will be any good.«

»That is fair. Then I thank you for your generosity and apologize for my ignorance. I should probably get going«, and I was already walking towards the door.

»Alright, see you around. Come back and show me that sword some day!«, Iron called probably not realizing what she was doing right now.

On the way past the table, I stole a sandwich and waved them goodbye. Then I was out the door, continuing my way with a quick pace.

At the end of the hallway was a huge door and I knew an entrance or exit when I saw one. I heard a sudden: »WAIT!« when I slipped outside.

 

 

Art made by the incredible @kusanyagii (on Twitter or Ko-Fi)

Notes:

Shout out to the beautiful and stunning "The Other Side" animatics from @Kemirii_png on Youtube that had a big influence on the scene with Kambera. (There was another animatic to the same song, by another artist, but that video got deleted :'( )

Pan did end up joining the organization after some guy tried to steal his dog (long story), but he has "negotiated" with Kambera that he can fully cut ties with it should he want to.

After joining Pan also messed with Iron more, eventually making her believe he's a ghost. He did apologize to her just before she started putting up wards in her workshop.
The orb turned out to be a pretty powerful magic item which doesn't really appear in the short stories, but it did save Pan's life once.
The last part of (Pan meeting Arkaniest) was an actual RP that my DM and I did later on

Chapter 3: Spring Festival

Summary:

Pan visits the Wolkrim Spring Festival and the local Fey Prince takes an interest in him.

Notes:

23.03.2023 - 1996 words

Chapter Text

It was early spring. The ground was still frozen but the snow had melted the last days just in time for the spring festival to wake Gaia. I had arrived in Wolkrim three days earlier, taking on a job to get rid of a Feymire Boar that had started nesting on some of the field in the past winter and that had started to attack the nearby houses.

They had written out that job for a while and I was the one who managed to slay the boar, after a few groups – including the guards – had tried and got badly injured or even killed in the process.

The fight had indeed been a tough and I had received me a bad slash over the face, causing me to bleed heavily in the face when I dragged myself and a trophy back to the nearest guard post. They had called for a healer and he kept me from scaring, but for the moment my face was still bandaged up.

The next day, as I was resting in one of the taverns, I got an invitation to the festival closed to the public. The invitation was made out to ›Thanis‹ as this was the name I was currently traveling under. In the name of the crown they thanked me for getting rid of the boar and invited me to the celebration the next evening. Appended was an instruction for the royal seamstress to turn me into something proper looking for that evening.

So I went over to the sewer, he took my measures and we argued about colors and my sword for a while because I refused to leave it in the tavern during the celebration.

Eventually he grunted: »I’ll speak to the ruler about it.«

Then he sent me to a bathhouse and told me to come back the next day to pick up the clothes for the celebration.

I did as I was told and very soon I arrived at the court. I was feeling pretty secure about my real identity staying a secret as almost half of my face was still hidden under the bandages. And I was almost looking forward to the evening, to playing the commoner idiot-hero who didn’t know full protocol.

 

As the celebration started, I kept watching the entrance to check I noticed anyone I personally and who I needed to stay away from, but luckily the place filled and nobody like that entered.

I was expecting this evening to go two ways for me: either the nobles would stay away from me like I carried a contagious disease or they would swarm me like I was the monkey of a traveling showman.

It began slow, but as soon as the ruler opened the buffet and movement came into the nobles – and me – I was surrounded by almost a dozen people who wanted to know all the gory details of the fight and asked way too many personal questions about my life.

I was telling stories, some real, some made up and answered all the questions. I was polite and behaving, but I did none of the subtleties of court-protocol a noble would do. Right now I was Thanis, who wasn’t trained in this and only used common sense to copy what he saw from the nobles around him.

 

After a while the doors to the garden were opened and the nobles took me outside where the orange rays of the setting sun made the world look like it was on fire.

The garden hosted a beautiful, massive, old tree; all around fires were lit in braziers.

The Ruler Narmud of House Karnchin first welcomed the guests and then welcomed the representatives of the spring court: Queen Thanay, Lord Noctiflorou, and Prince Equadorius.

They stepped out from the shadow of the tree and they were some of the most beautiful people I saw. I had heard of Queen Thanay naturally and Prince Equadorius, but I hadn’t known he was that hot. A young face with chipper eyes; black tussled hair; a golden twig like a crown on his head.

Even as they started the ritual – chanting in a language I didn’t know and dancing around the tree until it started to develop it’s first leaf pods – I couldn’t take my eyes off him. I had to put real effort in to not bite my lips or have my tongue move around. It was probably fey magic that pulled me in.

The ritual ended in the moment the sun had fully set – too soon for my liking – and the three vanished back into the tree. I was majorly disappointed. But I kept my face under control, played amazed at the magic – which wasn’t hard, because I was – and clapped with the other spectators.

Soon everyone returned inside and the evening continued with more food, more alcohol and more stories.

I didn’t actually have hopes of seeing Equadorius again. Then, all of the sudden as I was telling some tale, the nobles facing me bowed deeply.

As I I turned to look, I heard a soft voice: »You must be the hero who got rid of the Feymire Boar.«

When I saw him again, my brain stopped working and I just bowed, deeply and properly, forgetting that I shouldn’t be knowing all the details of a proper bow.

He smiled as I rose again and my heart was melting away at his smiling. A memory ending in a black pearl told me I would regret this later.

»It is an honor to meet you, Lord –«, I stammered and he waved his hand dismissive.

»Walk with me – Thanis, is it?«

I nodded and followed after him. The other nobles had stepped back and bowed again as we left. My heart was racing now and I tried so badly to hold on to my composure – thank Leira for all my training.

»What do you think of the celebration, Thanis?«

»It is great. I have never seen something like this«, I made a small gesture pointing towards the tree. Equadorius gave back another smile.

»It is nothing you see every day. But I can imagine you see a lot of interesting things on your travels?«

»Nothing that compares to this«, I answered – only partially truthful – with amazement in my voice. After the dragon rituals there was little that could compare.

»Indeed«, he just said. After a second he continued: »You can count yourself lucky that you've been allowed to see this. Not many people of your status are.«

Internally I laughed out, but I kept a straight face and just bowed my head: »I know. And I am thankful for it. The rest of the time, people of my status just watch the trees sprout in real time.«

He looked amused and curious at my re-tour: »You have some guts. I respect that and therefore will not be offended by this statement.«

»You honor me, my-Lord«, I grinned maybe a bit too hard.

»And your lack of manners are excused too. As is your wearing of a weapon,«

»Bandits have tried to rob me one to many times in taverns during meals.«

»This is no tavern.«

»There is no doubt about that. But there was no doubt about the security of these places either until bad things happened.«

»The guards will take care of any bandits.«

»You mean the guards who failed to take down the beast that I felled?«

He looked at me for a moment and I worried that I had gone too far with my teasing honesty; that he would be angry and I would have to make a quick exit over the garden walls. But then he started laughing out loud and slapped my back: »I like you, Thanis. You are honest.«

I grinned and as our eyes meet, I saw a gaze of admiration and playfulness in his. Below that his gaze was as sharp as a razor. I had to assume he was able to see similar things in mine.

 

He took me down a dimly lit hallway. We encountered a few scattered nobles with their partner, talking quietly or having already finished that part. This was going great for me.

»So… Are you just not the type for celebrations like that or what plans do you have for me?«, I asked him teasing. He started grinning.

»It is dangerous what you are doing, Thanis«, he spoke my name like it held weight. Which would be correct, if it were my actual name. »...Getting involved with the Fey.«

»And I am sure the people already speak about you stealing the traveler boy«, I answered like his action was as dangerous as mine.

He stopped for a moment and thinks. Then he smiled again and pushed open a door. A lightly lit lounge room appears. A couple of armchairs, but also a couch and a chaise lounge are placed around a fireplace in which only a few candles were burning.

»Please enter«, he said.

»If you promise not to pull me into any obligations that I will only learn later about?«, I looked at him sharp.

He nodded: »I promise this evening will not cause you any unforeseen consequences«, he answered honest and added in a bit more of a goofy tone: »Technically you are my father’s guest so I should not lay a hand on you.«

I offered him my hand as I step towards the door: »But will you do that in the literal sense?«

And princely he took my hand and I lead him into the room, pulling the door close behind him.

 

We sat down in two armchairs next to each other. We both knew how the evening would go eventually but it was not the time yet. Instead, he was letting me tell him the story of how I felled the beast and I add a couple of other stories similar to that. I choose smaller feats of mine, to not have me found out should he try looking me up after a while. It was a real role reversal for me. Normally I was the one sitting in his place, letting my guest tell me stories before I would fool around with them. But I remembered my current place and played my role well.

 

Finally wordlessly he got up and moved to sit on my lap gently.

»Can I?«, he asked softly and gestured to my face. I wasn't sure what he wanted to do, but I gave a light nod. He smelled of blooming flowers and wet dirt.

Carefully he started taking off the bandages around my eye. I let him since I knew it was mostly healed up.

A minute passed and he removes the last bit of cloth from my head. Then he stopped for a moment, just taking me in and eventually he traced his thumb below the wound. He was so gentle and soft. I almost didn't felt worthy of his touch.

Another moment of silence passed where he was just looking at me, and I was looking back, still not having opened my eye.

He had a slight smile when he whispered: »Your face is beautiful«

I couldn’t help but blush in that moment and automatically I lowered my gaze.

When I looked back up, I had both eyes open and husked: »Thank you.«

His smile grew wider – it was honest and deep.

»Truly beautiful«, he confirmed and leaned forward into a kiss. I complied happily. He tasted sweet and I wanted more but he and soon pulled back.

»I shouldn't get involved with someone of your status«, he said and it was with pained tone.

»Pretend I am of your status then«, I suggested like it was the easiest thing in the world to do. I kept my voice low and smooth like butter.

He smiled shyly and after another moment he closed his eyes again and pushed me back with his next kiss.

Chapter 4: Arkaniest

Summary:

Arkaniest looks for Pan, and finds him sparring with his old teacher.

Notes:

7 July 2023 - 1k words

Chapter Text

Where did Pan disappear to this time?! Or Faber as he called himself in these lands? And why was he so sketchy sometimes?

Anyway, Arkaniest had been looking for him for half an hour now – he had been gone for two by now. She was walking through the outskirts of the town, looking for any hint of him, asking people if they had seen that scrawny human gremlin. But so far, nobody had. Ticker was soaring above the city, checking if he was sitting on some roof, watching people. The chance was surprisingly high for that.

But Ticker hasn’t found him either and by now Arki was worrying a bit. He had a knack for getting himself into trouble. What if he traveled under Faber here because he was wanted? And someone had found him?

 

Another 10 Minutes passed and she noticed the sounds of fight-training: wooden swords clashing against each other; grunts of hits; panting; shouting.

There was a chance it was Pan, but there was a higher chance it stemmed from kids playing or something similar so she tried not to hold out too much hope. Still she checked, walking down a narrow allay, out of the settlement and into a connecting forest.

She found herself at the edge of a clearing: On it two people were training with each other. One person was half the size of the other. They were moving with such incredible speed, their movements blurred. The smaller one, a halfling as she came to realize, was jumping around the bigger, hitting him repeatedly but somehow he was managed to keep up, moving with the halfling and blocking almost all the attacks. It almost looked like a strange dance.

Every time the halfling hit a female voice called: »Slow« and despite the call happening almost every minute, he still kept blocking around 90% of her attacks.

What Arkaniest was watching, was a show of fighting skill on the highest level. Eventually the human broke through with an attack of his own, hitting the halfling hard and throwing her back. He himself yelled: »Slow« and she could hear the grin in the voice.

 

Now that he got the hit in, they both stopped and Arki could get a proper look at both of them: The halfling looked surprisingly old and not as kick-ass as she obviously was. Her hair was tied back in a round braid that started up by the facial hairline. She was stringy, lightweight but the way she moved, made it obvious that she had fullest control of her body. Despite her muscles being almost invisible, they would be made of steel fiber.

The other fighter was a young man, with loose, dark hair. He was not wearing a shirt and therefore revealed a chiseled muscular body, almost looking like a statue. He wasn’t big-muscular, he was lean-muscular. He wore a key on a necklace.

He turned towards the halfling, his muscles rippling under the skin: »Are you good?«

And hearing the voice again it hit Arkaniest who she saw: Pan. FUCK. He was buff. She had seen him shirtless before but it had been some time and with the clothes he wore, she had forgotten what he hid below them.

»Of course. I had worse. You have gotten good, Panagiotis«, she answered grinning. Panagiotis?

He grinned back – that same damn grin Arkaniest had fallen over. »I was good when I left you. I had a good teacher.«

She went over to him and boxed his arm lightly. »Traveling did not hurt your ego.«

Pan continued grinning. He wiped some sweat from his brow, and smeared a bit of dirt over his face with that gesture.

Why did he have so much abs? Why was he suddenly so pretty? Arki was completely stunned because she just wasn’t used to it. With more clothes on, he looked pretty and cute, but now he looked sexy and hot, and Arki was not prepared for it.

»- but apparently I am already missed«, Arki heard Pan saying after zoning out and staring for a moment. She noticed he was looking at her.

The halfling followed his gaze: »Is this your companion?«

Pan nodded and waved her over. Arki wished her brain would work properly while she came and called out: »I was looking for you!«

»You found me«, he answered grinning and went over to his clothes laying at the edge of the clearing, »Sorry for letting you wait so long. I found an old friend of mine.« He picked up his shirt and wiped his face with it. Arki’s only thought was just ›Don’t put it on again.‹

She noticed she was staring at his naked body again.

He noticed too and chuckled, without any malice. »This is my old teacher Xieni. Xieni, this is Arkaniest. She is currently traveling with me.«

The halfling came over to her: »Nice to meet you! I hope you are treating my old student well.«

»I– I am trying«, she stammered.

»She is«, Pan teased, »But I think she needs to restart her brain.«

She tried to punch his arm but he caught her hand. Sometimes she disliked his quick reflexes.

»I should probably go on. And you have left her waiting long enough. But it was great seeing you again. And sparring«, the halfling told him.

»Yes. Same for you. I hope to see you again. Maybe for the Solstice?«

»Maybe. Probably not. Another time in another place certainly. I am glad you kept up your training. Hopefully you do in the future too.«

»That is the plan.«

She smiled, jumped up and slapped his shoulder: »Until then.«

»Stay safe.«

»You too. And you take care of that silly boy«, Xieni told Arki who needed a second to realize she was talking to her.

»Eh, yes. Yes! I will. It was nice to meet you.«

Xieni smiled: »You too« and with that she started to walk away. As Arkaniest looked back to Pan he was putting on his shirt again and a feeling of major disappointment spread in her belly. She tried to push it aside, reminding herself to use logic and to keep her feelings in check.

Pan snapped before her face: »Earth to Arkaniest.«

She looked at him confused.

»Ready to leave or did I fry your brain?«, he asked.

»You did not! And I’ve been ready for an hour!«

»Then let’s go«, and he started walking. Now that he had put on his clothes, he looked so normal again, almost boring.

›Arki, concentrate‹, she scolded herself.

Chapter 5: Forest Ambush

Summary:

Pan retells a story of how he worked as a guard for some traveling nobles who got ambushed in the forest.

Notes:

29 July 2023 - 1464 words

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

This story happened a couple years ago. I was traveling incognito somewhere between Goldrolitin and Esimarkill. My path coincided with the one of a local noble family, traveling to some city a couple days away. Since the noble couple was not a big name and there was no risk of us crossing paths in any official way, I got myself hired as a guard on their travels. They were having some trouble with another group and feared some kind of attack or ambush during their time on the road, which is why they took me on in addition to four more guards. I would get some coins and free »lodgings« out of it – or at least company.

 

It had been three days and I was bored out of my mind at that point. One of the four guards was driving the carriage while the rest of us were mostly walking around the cart where the noble couple stayed. We had been traveling through a forest for some time now and we approached the edge of it, the foliage getting lighter. According to one of the guards we would have another hour, maybe two until we were fully out it. There would be a place to rest for the night at the edge of it, he had said.

I was lying on the back of the carriage on a chest looking into the sky and playing with a copper coin, when the guard driving let out a grunt and the following ›thunk‹ told me he had fallen from the carriage. I jumped up, looking over the roof of the cart and saw he had been hit by an arrow. From the looks of it the injury would not be fatal but he was out of it for now.

I was still looking when a flurry of arrows rained onto the carriage and I ducked behind the cart again. Somehow I was the first to yell out »Ambush!«, then jumped onto the cart drawing my own bow. I shoot in the general direction of where the arrows had come from and get another flurry in return. Five or six projectiles rain down but luckily don’t hit anyone. Another arrow of mine was answered by a cry that told me it had found flesh.

The other three guards had raised their shields but were still stunned by the sudden attack. One had had the presence of mind to grab the reigns keeping the horses in check.

»Get him back on the cart!«, I yelled to the others and they finally got a move on. Four more arrows flew and were deflected by the shields; one more whistled past me.

»Get ready to ride!«, I instructed them. The next flurry came solely for me; the attackers probably thought I was the leader. I counted four missiles this time. I returned the favor, shooting more arrows myself, but couldn’t hear another one landing.

Two guards were pulling and pushing the hurt one back on the driver’s block. When they were done, they were basically cuddling on the small bench. The fourth guard tossed one of them the reigns and hurried to jump on as well.

I whistled and the horses were spurred on forcing me to steady myself as the carriage surged forward. Thankfully there were no more arrows but a moment later somebody broke out of the bushes on the side of the road and brought down a war hammer on a wheel of the cart. The impact and subsequent swerving threw me off but I managed to break my fall and roll to my feet. The cart was loosing speed as quickly as it had gained it. Three more bandits had appeared and were going for the drivers and the carriage door but the guards fended them off.

»Keep riding!«, I yelled and drew my sword in a wide lunge bringing it down on the handle of the hammer and causing it to splinter. The hammer wielder punched up and hit the base of my chin before lifting the heavy hammer. I bashed the hilt of my sword into his throat and dodged his hammer as it came down on me again. When it hit the ground the handle broke and surprised the bandit tumbled forward, head first into my knee. He dropped like a sack of root vegetables.

Even before he had hit the ground I was already racing after the cart and the other three bandits. Full sprint I vaulted myself on the back of one of them causing him to topple over as I slammed my sword onto into the back of his neck. I had to roll myself backwards to dodge a sword slicing through the air where my neck had been just seconds before. I tried to stab his shin but his boots and the weird angle of my attack kept it from hurting him. In response he brought his sword down like a hammer and once more I manage to roll away just in time.

»You don’t look like a leader but you are annoying enough«, he growled attacking again. This time I blocked with my sword and as pushed away his I jumped onto my feet again. I could see the last bandit by the cart getting knocked down by a guard and the carriage finally started gaining. I just smiled widely at the man before me and attack. I wasn’t a fan of violence like this, but gods be damned if it wasn’t good to be challenged and to move again.

The guy was a good foe but in the end I knew he would not be a match for me: he wasn’t fast enough to hit me properly or often but equally – since I tried not to kill him – my blows weren’t really effective either.

When I had eventually just managed to wrestle him down, someone punched me right in the face. I spat blood and the guy below me struggled free, delivering another punch. Two against one would still be possible, but the guy who I jumped was also getting to his feet.

At least the carriage was so far away that the three would not be able to catch up to it. I knew when it was time to opt out of a fight: I parried another set of attacks and gave them back before I threw myself against the third guy and tackle him down again. Then I took off running, sheathing my sword in full sprint.

The swordsman I had been fighting yelled something I didn’t understand but a glance back told me they were following. I ran into the forest hoping I could loose them faster there but I did not know this forest, this vegetation, these lands. I jumped over roots, scrambled around boulders and charged through bushes. I tripped and slid often and often with little control. And one of the bandits kept up with me well while the other two were falling behind. I continued running, eventually circling around a tree and jumping him as he passed it. I caught him by surprise and he toppled over, going to the ground as I stabbed his leg with a dagger.

Then I took off running again, scrambling on all fours for a couple of meters before I managed to pull up my center of mass and continue normally. The other two were still on my tail, even if far behind so I dove into a thicket and scaled the next tree, hoping they didn’t see. Soon they passed by below me and I held my breath but they continued without noticing me. It seemed like I had managed to escape.

But I was now hiding in a tree, in the middle of a forest, in a strange land, with some bandits out for my blood, and sundown approaching quickly. I was basically lost.

If I wanted to find my way back to the road or directly to the town, I would either need to get high enough to see the stars or wait until morning. Either way I would probably not be able to catch up with the carriage again. With some luck I would find them in the town we had been heading to but by foot it would take at least another day, maybe even two.

 

I ended up »resting« on that tree for the whole night. It was too dangerous to travel without light in the forest with wildlife, traps and other dangers, but it was equally dangerous to travel with light thanks to the bandits. At least I was certain that they had missed me the next morning. At dawn, I climbed down and made my way in the general direction of the town.

Notes:

Shoutout to the band "The Amazing Devil", which are as amazing as their name suggests, and their music video of "King". I have written this chapter (and many other scenes) to that exact video.

Chapter 6: Masquerade Ball

Summary:

Notes:

07.08.2023 - 2180 words

Chapter Text

»A celebration... of what form if I might ask?«

»A masquerade ball! My wife and I met at such an occasion and we decided to honor that this year«, he sounded excited.

»And if I might ask further who is invited? I have some history with one noble or the other...«, I said careful, making it sound like one of them despised me, even though that was not the case. I just needed to be careful not to be recognized.

A servant came over and handed the Lord the invitation only for the Lord to hand it to me.

Chapter 7

Summary:

Pan runs in to an old one night stand of his

Notes:

31 August 2023 - 2438 words

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

It was turning autumn. The morning air was crisp and heavy with moisture. Fog hung over the fields in big tufts. The sun, despite having risen, was still covered by the nightly clouds on the horizon. Soon it would rise over them and burn the dew from the grass. But for now, I buttoned up my coat, as I left the inn.

I was in a somber, tranquil mood and all the people, I met, seemed to share that. There was no haste in the air, the people were moving slowly, some still had sleep in their eyes.

I didn’t know what this day held and if I actually wanted to find out; I would be fine with a lazy day.

Slowly I made my way over the market, bought breakfast pastries and headed down to the harbor to watch the ships coming and going. I find a rooftop to sit on, let one leg dangle and had the other one pulled up, as I slowly ate my breakfast. Some people seemed to notice me up there but nobody bothered – I wasn’t sitting in the way and I wasn’t breaking anything. ›Let the guy do what he wanted‹, they probably thought.

 

Ships came and went, sailors loading cargo on and off, captains arguing with the harbor-masters and custom-officers. And yet, the scene was still peaceful.

As the sun rose the air got warmer. The wind from the sea was a gentle breeze, ruffling my hair softly.

I watched as a bigger galleon left with the flood and a different, three-masted bark takes its place. The sailors moored the ship, the gangplank was lowered and the waiting officer went onboard. He argued with the captain who eventually snapped and sent a runner into the belly of the bark. After a couple minutes a taller gentleman leaves it, papers in hand and approaches the officer with quick steps.

They spoke, the officer looks over the papers and eventually nodded, before walking off the ship, to instructs his deputy.

The gentleman spoke with some of his sailors and soon they run off, darting down into the ship. The captain comes back, discussing something with the guy and soon they nodded, handshake and the man left the ship just as the first boxes are unloaded.

I continued watching the man as he made his way into the harbor and, as he came closer to my vantage point, I finally got a better look of him. It took me a moment to realize who this was and it left me stunned: it was Arledge Draftwavers from Quante.

Five years ago, when I met him last, he played something enchanting and then left only a black pearl behind. Today, he was at least as pretty as on that party, but it was a different type of pretty. He wasn’t styled up like on that evening but he was forged by the voyage on the sea. His skin was a dark bronze and he had more freckles as back then.

I can’t stop looking at him, so I watch as he takes care of his business, talking with different people, exchanging money, pointing back to the ship. And every once in a while, he looks up to me, probably feeling my gaze on him.

When noon comes around, Arledge meets up with his captain on one of the ships again. He hands him a pouch of money and they handshake again. And as Arledge comes back to the harbor, I decide to go for lunch, so I drop off the roof and find myself a nearby tavern.

 

After a bit of time, a person sits down at my table with their plate and mug. As I look up I see the familiar face.

»You have been watching me«, he says sharp and with a glare in his eyes.

»I’ve been watching everyone«, I reply dismissively.

»I felt your eyes on me since we docked. What do you want?«

»Nothing that you are guessing right now. Chance had it that I was watching the ships and my gaze fell onto your and then you«, I speak soft against his sub-textual accusations.

»But chance is not responsible for said gaze to stay on me for hours«, he says sharp and disapproving.

»That is true«, I simply answer.

»Then what is it you want? You should know I am not a person easily messed with«, he threatens – or at least it could have been a threat, if I were the bandit he assumed I was.

»Not your money, believe me.«

»But?«, the way he held that fork, he could have jumped me any moment and rammed it into my flesh.

»I was just curious about the person that charmed and then left me hanging all those years ago«, I explain soft. His expression immediately shifts from hostility and anger to confusion and horror. He studies my face closer. A moment passes and a flicker of recognition passes over him, but the confusion only grew at that. If he had realized who I was, he was probably trying to guess why I was dressed as some random citizen now, and why I was eating in a mediocre-at-best tavern quite far away from home.

»It can’t be...«, he mutters in disbelieve.

»You only left me a black pearl«, I add another hint, my voice detached from the hurt I had felt back then.

His face turns pale, his eyes growing wide: »L-L-Lo–«, he stammers as I slightly raise my hand and silence him effectively.

»Call me Bernd, we don’t want unwanted attention, do we?«

He lowers his gaze ashamed and nods.

»Just – what are you doing here?«, he asks shocked.

I lay my head to the side, thinking and eventually answer: »I have no requirement to leave you with more answers than you left me that night.«

I could have slapped him as well. He blushes and admits: »No, you don’t.«

»Will you stay in this city longer?«, I ask formally.

»I will leave in the morning.«

»Do you have any plans for the evening?«

»I do not«, he was answering me in the way a noble’s-subject would answer them. I asked my questions like I was said noble.

»Would you like to exchange some answers this evening? Over dinner and a bottle of wine maybe?«

He looks up at me surprised and doesn’t answer for a while, trying to understand what my plans were. Eventually he nods: »It would be an honor, ...Bernd«, he stumbles over the unknown name.

»Where do you stay?«, I ask still in that factual tone.

»The Golden Hoof Inn.«

»I’ll meet you there at six«, I say and get up, leaving before he could protest or stop me.

Outside I quickly scale the wall of the tavern and if he would have come after me, it would look like I had vanished.

I return to my own inn, wash and change clothes; preparing for the evening.

 

At six I was in front of the Golden Hoff inn and waited. Arledge emerged a few moments after the bell rang, having cleaned himself up as well. He looked anxious like he was stepping in front of his angry father. From his demeanor I could tell, he was unsure of my mood: Was I angry for leaving me? Did I resent him? But why did I invite him to dinner?

»Good evening, Arledge«, I greet him warmly and with a smile: »Do you have an establishment you would like to go or shall I take the lead?«

»Good evening, Bernd. You can take the lead.«

I nod and do. We go to a higher priced place where we get a little private room. Neither of us speak until we sit.

»You do not need to fear me. If you like, this will be a meeting between two soul that will have no effect on the official relation of our two names.«

He nods: »I would probably like that.«

»Then so shall it be. Let’s not talk about this to our parents.«

He nods again and seems to relax a bit. We take a moment to choose and order our dinner. I get a bottle of wine for both of us. When the waiter leaves I speak again: »I still have the pearl.«, I smile warm.

»You do?«, he sounds surprised.

»Since that was the only nice thing I was left with on that evening...«

»I am sorry.«

»Can you explain me why? What happened that night?«, it was a soft offer to speak; there was no malice in my voice, no resentment, at most a bit of sadness. He inhales his eyes pained, maybe even scared. Seconds tick by until he eventually shakes his head: »I cannot. I should not. I am sorry, Pa– Bernd.«

»It’s alright. I understand there are things one cannot talk about.«

»I assume what you are doing here is one of those things?«, he tries to change the topic.

I nod: »Exactly and it would be good if nobody hears of where we met – or that we did at all.«

»I understand. Of course. I will keep it to myself.«

The food arrived at that moment.

»So how were you doing? I heard the business is running well.«, I say, speaking slowly.

»Yes, it’s going good. As you saw I was on the sea; the pearls don’t come from thin air after all. In the south there are some marvelous island and beaches where they basically grow in abundance. And I like being on the ship«, he tells me.

»That is good. And you look good. The sun does wonders to your skin«, I smile, careful and anxious myself. I had no idea how this night would go. Would we eat and part ways afterwards? Would we share multiple bottles of wine? Would the night from 5 years ago repeat? Or would we go the whole way this time?

»And you’re traveling as well?«, he asks.

»I do. It’s been quite interesting. ...Not being trapped in one place the whole time«, I answer in a way where it was impossible to say how much or little traveling I did.

»Yes, you were said to be restless«, he gives a little smile. I just nod and we fall silent, eating a bit.

 

Suddenly Arledge speaks up again in a demanding tone: »Why did you ask me for dinner?«

I lay my hand on the base of my wineglass and look at him for a moment before I answer: »I am not sure. Our last meeting had been so ...short and unsatisfactory. Now I meet you suddenly, five years later, on the other side of the continent and … «, I shrug, »Maybe I was hoping this one could be different? We’ve grown both. Whatever kept you last time, maybe this time it won’t?«

He is also silent for a bit. He lowers his eyes and doesn’t dare to look up again.

»I apologize if I have put you into an uncomfortable situation again«, I continue.

»No! No, you haven’t! At no point, Panagiotis!«, he calls distressed. »You were perfect from the beginning to end – even going against your desires – and I wish we could, but… it’s a horrible idea: me and you...«

»I understand«, I answer soft, »Maybe I don’t know all the details but I will not ask further. And I will not put you in any more situations like back then – or this. If you would prefer to leave or have me leave...«, I offer.

Arledge just looks more miserable. With a quiet voice he pleads: »Please, don’t. I wish we could forget the last time and just meet for the very first time...«

I am silent for a moment, thinking about how I should respond. The responsible answer would be to say ›We can’t. And we shouldn’t‹, but was I that responsible?

I look up with a soft gaze. He looked back at me desperate. If I stopped listening to reason for a moment…?

»We could pretend to...«, the words eventually leave my mouth and I instantly regret them as I see the longing in his eyes mixed in with the pain.

Seconds pass by until he shakes his head: »No, Panagiotis. Let’s enjoy the rest of the dinner and then go our separate ways again.«

»As you wish«, I nod and we continue to eat in silence for a bit.

 

When we do start speaking again it’s about vanities: the weather, the trade, some fun encounters. In the end of the dinner I actually had some fun.

I pay for our meals and together we leave the restaurant.

»I need to head into the same direction as you for a bit. Will you bear me a bit longer or sh–« He stop me from talking with a head shake. We start walking, talking about the starts that dotted the sky.

Eventually we come to a junction and I say: »This is where I leave. Thank you for–«

Suddenly he pushes me back, against the wall of a house, kissing me passionately and intently like he had been starving and could not longer stop himself form tearing into the food. He was gripping my shirt around the shoulders and after a moment of surprise and shock, I lay my arms over his back, leaning into the kiss. Enough time passes before he pulls back. He had tears in his eyes.

With a soft whisper I ask: »Would you like to come back to mine?«

Instead of answering he just kisses me again, one hand laying itself against my chest.

»I love you as much as I am sorry«, he husks when he eventually pulls back: »Maybe we could find each other in the next life … when I … I can allow myself to love you.«

I push a kiss onto him now: »In our next life then.«

»Stay like you are – you’re perfect. Promise me«, he husks again. Would he actually use his voice, he would probably be weeping.

»I cannot. But I’ll try.«

He blinks some tears away: »That is enough for me. Good night, Panagiotis«, he kisses me again and I just close my eyes.

When I open them again, just the memory of the touch remains – he had vanished.

I stay for a moment then check if I still had all my things or if he had left me anything. This time everything had stayed the same.

Notes:

All of Pan's aliases are references to something or other. Bernd was a late addition that emerged from our actual game. The first time I introduced him, another player declared him "Bread" and that nickname STUCK. Mix that with my 2000s born German roots, and the leap to Bernd wasn't far. And if you people don't know "Bernd das Brot", google it and have a mare.

Chapter 8: Bar Fight

Summary:

Pan fights off three goons in a bar

Notes:

07 September 2023 - 679 words

If you want to have the right music too, I've written this while listening to "Noel's Lament" (Ride the Cyclone Musical), with some inspiration taken from the animatic "Noel's Lament | oc animatic" by @sneezyhead.

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

It was a shady bar in Ultrawood. The air hung full of smoke, the lights were dimmed and the musicians sat behind a curtain to protect their identities.

Of course I had seen the three ruffians entering the bar. Everybody was looking at the opening door when the breath of fresh air blew threw. Of course I had watched them scanning the crowd and noticed their glances stick getting stuck on me, even as I kept my head down and pretended to nurse my beer. Of course I saw them looking at that paper and then head in my direction. And of course I knew this was about the other night when I had beaten up a more well off guy harassing one of the prostitutes. The only thing I was surprised by was how long it had taken him to send someone after me.

The band was playing something in the direction of a tango. Perfect for dancing.

I took a drink from my mug, then threw my body back against the chair and looking up at the men: »Gentlemen...«

»Any last words?«, one rumbled.

I chuckled: »Not to the gods, but to you: I prefer to burn out than to fade away.«

They looked confused for a moment and I used that moment to push my chair away from the table and jump up. They move in, but the first got his jaw kicked in as I jerked my leg upwards. And in time with the music I charged at the second dude jumping right into his face, my knee connecting with it. His dagger flew out of his hand and I grabbed it out of the air.

I flipped over the third guy and swiped someone’s cigarette, pushing it out on the base of the ruffian’s neck. He tried to punch me with a wide motion but I literally danced and ducked under him, as the band played on undisturbed by the sounds coming from the other side of the curtain. I tackled the guy onto a table, bringing the knife down just shy of his throat, instead pinning his shirt down. And then I add insult to injury by using him as a ramp onto said table.

The other patrons of this establishment had given us fighters a wide birth – if they were still conscious enough to do so. One guy had passed out at the very table I was standing on and his beer became a casualty in the fight as I kicked it onto the first attacker, causing him to stumble backwards and fall over a chair.

Before he could hit the ground, I pulled the paper out of his front pocket where he had half-heatedly stuffed it in. Guy #2 came at me with blood covering his face but I danced back and light the paper on a cone on the wall. It went up in flames and when I pretend to throw a punch with the burning paper in hand my opponent flinched back. I didn’t punch him, instead I twirled, dropped the paper, and forced my elbow into his voice box. The sound was not pretty: a yelping gurgle, as he fell back passing out.

Number 3 had pulled himself and the dagger free and now threw the dagger after me. I sidestepped, grabbed it from the air again and flipped it around in my hand. Then I threw it back, the butt of the handle hitting the guy square between the eyes, causing him to collapse.

I turned to the last guy who had managed to get up again. With a wide, wild smile I offered him: »Do you yield?« But his answer turned out non-verbal as he charged at me. I roundhouse kicked, my foot connecting with his cheek hard. The momentum turned him around, then he crumbled.

I gave a stage-worthy bow, then flee the scene.

Terror was in the eyes of some of the onlookers as I passed: they had realized I had not drawn my sword at any point in the battle.

Notes:

Pan was a bard even before I started multiclassing him into a bard. I love this one - it's very fun.

Chapter 9: Equadorius reunited

Summary:

Pan (and his group) return to Wolkrim and meet with Equadorius.

Notes:

23 September 2023 - 2371 words

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

»Last time I was here, I went by the name ›Thanis‹, so please refer to me like that«, I told the group as we came into town.

»Tanis? Like the Professor?«, Serina asked.

»No, Thanis. With an ›h‹. I haven’t met the professor back then.«

»I’m still gonna call you ›Bread‹«, Tatiana noted.

I nodded and with a tiny sigh in my voice I answered: »That works too.«

Surprisingly the group had never asked why I kept changing my name – or what my real name was – but I wasn’t complaining. It was one lie less I had to tell.

 

»We request an audience with a member of the Spring Court«, I told the guard outside the castle.

»Why?«, he demanded ruff.

Tatiana flew in front of his face and answered: »I am from the Spring Court! I want to meet my people again!«

He nodded: »Wait here«, and then headed inside.

I was tense. Who would we get an audience with? Archfey Thanay? One of her children? Equadorius? Or just Ruler Narmud? I wasn’t sure whether I wanted to see Equadorius or hoped he wasn’t around.

 

The minutes dragged on and once again it was Xvatil who noticed my tension. She stepped next to me and asked low: »Are you feeling alright?«

»Yes. Just... Last time I was here things happened and the time passed probably made these things weird.«

She looked at me with a raised eyebrow, communicating her guess quietly. I nodded back as I noticed the heat in my face.

Neither of us say more. Serina was complaining that the guard was taking so long.

»We showed up randomly demanding an audience. There is no guarantee that we will be seen now – even today. And it might not hurt to have a night in the inn and an opportunity to wash«, I told her and noticed the other guard had an expression of affirmation to my words – and a bit of disgust at our appearance: the travel time hadn’t been too kind to us.

Serina continued complaining but not much later the first guard came back, with a page in tow who announced: »The prince will receive you.«

My heart dropped a bit: ›Prince‹. There was still a chance it wasn’t Equadorius, but I didn’t believe in my luck.

As we followed him, Tatiana was the first in line. We were lead through the castle and I noticed it hadn’t changed much since I had been here last. Soon the page knocked at a door and opened after a short call from inside. I recognized the voice immediately: soft like a gust of wind in spring. As the group entered I kept a step back and so became the last to enter.

Equadorius still looked like the day I last saw him: black tussling hair, bright eyes and soft features. He wore golden earrings and a rather simple, yet fine attire. I thanked the gods he was focused on the fairy.

The page announced his name and title once we all had entered: »Prince Equadorius, the fourth prince of the spring court and third prince of Wolkrim.«

Equadorius smiled and my throat turned dry. I swallowed and was glad for Xvatil bowing in front of him when I did. Serina took a second, then quickly followed our example. Only Tatiana flew around excited.

»And who do we have here?«, Equadorius scanned over our group; I didn’t dare to raise my head or even breathe.

»I can’t believe I actually get to meet someone from the spring court«, Tatiana spoke excited.

»So you are the fairy from my realm?«

»I thought everything burnt down! I thought I was the last one remaining! And then Bread here tells me I can find you here! Are you real?«, she pulled on his cheek and I bit my tongue. He gently took her off his face and sat her down on his desk with a pat: »I appear to be. Maybe we should take a step back and introduce ourselves. Well who I am you already know.«

He looked at us again. We had risen from our bows, but I still kept half a step back and was hiding just a tiny bit behind Xvatil and Serina – but mostly Xvatil.

»My name is Tatiana Yosei and this is my group«, she gestured to us. We all introduced us with some fake name: I gave mine as Thanis.

His gaze remained on me longer than the others: the silence between us was deafening. But there was nothing but interest in his gaze. Interest and surprise, nothing else. I kept my own face void of emotions.

One too many heartbeats passed before he turned his focus back to Tatiana: »Your story is an interesting one. We will need more time to discuss and I am sure your group doesn’t need to stand around listening the whole time.«

Xvatil and I nodded quietly, but Serina announced she would not leave Tatiana’s side.

»Very well. You are free to leave or stay as you prefer.«

Xvatil and I bowed then left. Just before I whispered to Serina quietly: »We will find a place to stay in the meantime.«

»You do not have a place to stay yet?«, Equadorius asked; those ears of his were too good.

»Not yet, your Grace«, I answered formal.

»We can provide you rooms. The ruler of Wolkrim shall welcome you as your business with the Spring Court gets conducted«, and before any of us could say anything, he raised his hand: »And my father is no Fey, so you will not be pulled into any obligations.«

»We would be honored«, I spoke for the group. He smiled, called for a servant and told him to »Get us situated«.

 

After dinner that day, I was alone in the room provided for me. I was still a ball of tension so to counter it, I trained and stretched. I was just wearing a simple shirt and pants – and my sword of course – but none of my other gear or clothes.

Eventually there was a knock on my door. Assuming it was one of the party I just called: »Yes! Come in«, without breaking the figure.

The door opened and someone entered with a soft step, before closing it again. But these weren’t the steps of anyone of my group so I look up.

And there he was again: leaning against his hands that rested on the closed door, and smiling softly. I let myself fall out of the pose startled and my weight came down hard on the leg that just had been in the air as I half stumble a step back.

»Prince – I didn’t expected you...«, I fumbled for words.

»Then you know how I feel«, his words were soft, without malice but a sad tone rang in them. He headed over to the armchairs and took the one I hadn’t thrown my bag and coat onto.

»Normally it’s us Fey who appear for a night and then vanish into thin air«, he said pensive. I was still standing over to the other side of the room without moving. He continued after a long time: »At this point I didn’t expect you to appear again. Not four springs later. And much less so close to my wedding«, he spoke and pushed his hand through his hair. I felt like he just thrown a bucket of ice water over me: he was getting married?!

»Would you be willing to entertain me one more night, Thanis?«

»I – I shouldn’t«, my voice caught as I tired to speak.

»Not like that. At least–«, he shook his head quickly, »I am glad to see you again. Would you sit with me?«

I came over to him, put the bag down and the coat over the backrest, before sitting down.

»I wondered about you so long: you came into the city with a fake name from the start. Took down a Feymire Boar that our guards couldn’t handle, stole a night from me and then vanished into nothing. Four springs where I only ever heard rumors with your name. Then Prince Fernios tells me you give your regards while he celebrates over in the jungles of Esimarkill. And suddenly you arrive here with a lost fairy of mine… You are quite the enigma.«

I nodded quiet. There was nothing I could say to this; no explanation I could give.

I couldn’t apologize: I wasn’t sorry. I never expected to be here again – to be near him.

»Would you at least look at me?«, he asked after some moments passed. I looked up at him. His expression was sad. No, not just sad: he was in pain as he looked at me. An incredible and painful longing.

And I was sure, there was pain in my eyes too. I realized how much he had held onto that night – a night I had half forgotten; one that mixed in with half a dozen similar ones – while I had left fulfilled and satisfied, while I had left him wanting more. I hadn’t even left him anything to remember me by I realized with a sting of guilt. I had left him in a similar position Arledge had left me, but at least Arledge had given me a pearl before he had vanished.

I had been at the giving end of this awkward conversation – now I was on the receiving one: Tyr had balanced the scales again.

»There is nothing I could say that could excuse me«, I eventually told him.

»I don’t want excuses«, he answered.

»What would you want then?«, I asked despite knowing the answer.

»Answers.«

»I cannot give you any. Despite knowing how you feel I can’t give you the answers you deserve.«

»Can I still try asking the questions?«

I exhaled: »You can.«

»Did you use a fake name back then because of us Fey?«

I shook my head silently.

»Where did you come from?«

I looked at him silently. Moments pass and eventually he moved on: »Did you plan that night?«

»No. I had heard of you. But I hadn’t planned anything. Neither getting invited by the Ruler, nor what happened afterwards...«

»You were wonderful, Thanis. Beautiful in the shine of the candles. Your eyes full of wonder like looking at a masterpiece of art. Your hands soft, skilled, powerful and careful. Your lips sweet like the wine and honey«, he spoke soft and I wasn’t sure if he thought of then or now. I felt a charm taking a-hold of my mind.

»Never before or after I felt like during that night. And you left me with nothing. Not even your name«, he sounded desperate. There was power in his words especially the last. I feel a pull on my mind, my tongue, but I wasn’t about to spill my secrets. I took a moment to breath in and my mind became still again. Then I looked at him with a clear gaze and his turned even more desperate once he had noticed I had broken his charm.

»Equadorius, we are no teenagers anymore. You are about to get married and I will disappear again once Tatiana has gotten the things she came here for. If you want a repeat of the night, we can do that, but I don’t think that would be smart of us. We both have duties and one of us is marrying a good wife.«

»It doesn’t have to be like that«, he pleaded, »We could disappear together...«

»You wouldn’t know what you’d be in for. You can’t do that. And neither can I.«

Equadorius was taut like a spring; a balloon too full: he was either about to explode or deflate.

A full minute passed until he eventually did deflate: »Back then you told me to pretend you are of my status… Can’t you do that?«

»No«, I answered soft but decisive.

»Why not?«

I was silent for a bit: »I need your word that this information does not leave this conversation.«

He looked surprised but quickly said: »You have it.«

»We are«, I told him, the context already being lost thanks to the intermission.

He blinked once, then twice.

»Of… The same status...«, he slowly pieced the information together.

I nodded: »And that is all I will tell you about that. If you would run away with me, that would cause huge issues between you and your family, but also your family and mine. And I will not be risking that.«

Equadorius breathed in but the air tumbled down his lungs like marbles down a staircase: »I understand.«

»I never intended to trouble you. And certainly – by my life – not that much«, I added.

He was taut again and I tensed.

A breath passed; then two. What would he do? Five, then ten.

»Equadorius«, I started slowly. He looked at me with the hunger of a starving animal. His demeanor of a soft spring rainfall was gone. He had turned into a developing storm.

I inhaled and braced myself: »It might be better if you would leave now.«

»Give me one night«, he husked out desperate.

I might have wanted it as much as he did, but my brain remembered that this would have consequences – his didn’t. I wanted to feel his soft skin and hair, breath his fresh smell, kiss his soft lips, feel his heart beat and watch his bare chest rise and fall in the moonlight coming in from the window.

»You will be married...«, I objected pained myself.

And suddenly the explosion happened even if it was silent. Equadorius jumped onto me with such speed and force, the armchair toppled. He pressed me down with his weight kissing me with a passion of a yearning lover. And I wasn’t much better: drinking up his touch, pushing against his lips, breathing his sweet scent of flowering nature. His hands pushed under my shirt and mine rested on the back of his neck and in his hair.

»Don’t do something you’ll regret«, I whispered when we both drew a breath.

»I would regret not doing it«, and he forced me down again. I was not about to protest any more.

Notes:

A few parts of this scene are actually canon in our home game. Serina, Xvatil and Tatiana are the other PCs. Tatiana is a fairy coming from the Spring Court, so when she first decided she wanted to reconnect with her family, I wrote this scene. In the end, nothing like this happened.
I love the concept of a fey being so puzzled by a "bog standard" human, that they are obsessively in love with them. And since Pan never mentioned his real name to Equadorius, Equadorius cannot do any magic to find out more about him.

Chapter 10: Graton

Summary:

Pan is a perfect storm of enraged noble to free Arkaniest from house arrest

Notes:

25 September 2023 - 2130 words

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

I once had heard that sons of nobility were some of the most destructive natural disasters. If you were hit by one all you could do was brace yourself, try to do damage control and hope it would pass quickly. I had watched a display like that once from afar and since then had been glad to not have gotten involved in one. But apparently this time I had caused it – or at least called it forth.

Four days ago I had tried to enter the archive of Graton and they had some questions. They thought the document had been forged and wanted it clarified before I could do anything. They had asked me to enter a Zone of Truth and answer some questions, which only went horrible, but at least took me out of the suspicion that I had forged those documents myself. For »security reasons« they still imprisoned me in a rather nice apartment and called it house arrest.

The first thing I did there was cast a Sending to Pan, telling him about the situation and asking for help. He had told me he was on his way and asked me to keep him updated. So every day, I told him there were no news and he told me how much longer he’d be.

That morning he told me he would arrive in a few hours and indeed a couple hours later the storm suddenly broke loose.

I didn’t hear the beginning. I heard the slamming of a door and then multiple voices shouting.

Calls of »You can’t enter this!« and »Trespassing« were audible from the two guards at my door but a voice thundered over them: »I am the representative of Millguard you requested. You are holding my researcher on wrong assumptions! Let her go now!«

The voice of one of my guards – who had been rather nice to me – answered quieter, and tried to smooth things over. I didn’t quite catch his words so I moved to the door to listen in.

»No! You will get your supervisor or whoever is responsible for this mess or I will tear down this whole building myself! You have three minutes.«

Someone went running; I heard the door slam again.

»Is she in there?«, the representative demanded. I guessed a second guard was still there.

»You cannot-«, he started but a moment later there was a knock on the door: »Arkaniest? Are you in there?«

I thought I recognized the voice now that it was a bit less thunderous: Pan? What was he playing?

»Yes. I’m here. I’m alright.«

»Hang in there«, he told me, apparently turned back to the guard and barked at him: »And I hope for your sake she was treated well or you will regret ever taking a breath after falling out of your mother.«

Holy hell. Was that really Pan? It was his voice but nothing about his words or the way he spoke was like the easy-go-lucky adventurer I knew.
