He continued to intimidate the poor guard who I could only assume was ducking in a corner by now. When I heard the door open again Pan barked: »Are you the one responsible for this?!«

»My name is Khidell Tracaryn. I am handling access to the archives. I am surprised you have arrived that -«

»Get to the point. You are holding an official Researcher of ours prisoner for no reason other than suspicion. I am here to tell you your suspicion is incorrect«, Pan thundered.

»Of course«, Khidell also tried a calming tone, »Would you mind showing –«, he stopped suddenly, I assumed Pan showed him something.

»And just to be extra certain, since we are fond of a truthful statements, would you mind if you could repeat your words while being in a Zone of Truth.«

»If this is the quickest way to bring my Researcher to justice.«

I heard Khidell cast the spell and Pan spoke again in the same domineering tone he had the whole time: »I am Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd, Eleventh Lord of Millguard and Second of the name. I have personally appointed Arkaniest Karanista to Official Researcher of Millguard in the matter of the combat orbs Graton has created in the Union Wars. I have personally signed the document you believe is forged and handed it to her. You will let my researcher go in this instance or I will bring the wrath of Loviatar and Tyr down on you!«

Behind the door, my mouth hung a gaping wide open. I had to assume he was telling the truth, which meant – holy shit. It meant so much.

Moments later the door got unlocked by fumbling keys and I just managed to step back and close my mouth before it hit me in the face.

And as it opened, I indeed saw Pan, but he looked nothing like the guy I came to know – and love. First of all, his expression and demeanor were exactly as they had sounded behind the door: a hurricane and earthquake in one; a force of nature you didn’t want to mess with. And then he was dressed completely differently. There was no way in the world this was the same guy who stepped out of a lake and shouted »Who wants a chair leg?«, while holding up a broken one. This was not the same man that sat on roofs, watching people and joking around all the time.

This man looked like more like a king, than some of the kings I had met. He was wearing green and yellow/gold regalia; a deep green cloak of heavy velvet with the insignia of Millguard on it; heavy and expensive boots. He had a hand on his sword; which was sheathed in green-ish metal and decorated with golden ornaments; the hilt was expertly carved. On his thumb gleamed a signet ring.

As he saw me, his face softened a bit and with two long strides he stood in front of me. In his voice, none of the hostility remained as he asked: »Are you alright?«

»Yes«, I husked overwhelmed.

He smiled lightly and as long as I only focused on his face, he looked like the man I knew again. »Good. Gather your things, we’re leaving«, then he turned around sharp, nothing of that softness remaining.

»I want an official apology of your office and you personally. I want her to receive proper lodging and free access to all parts of the archive while she conducts her research! If I hear she doesn’t get treated like a lady, any and all relationships between Millguard and Graton will suffer greatly of this. Repent and I will let this offense slide.«

I watched as all three bowed deeply before him.

Khidell spoke up: »We will prepare proper accommodations for you and your ward.«

He nodded sharp and one of the guards disappeared, running. I quietly got my bag and return to the tense scene in the entrance hall where Pan was glaring down Khidell. I stepped next to him and gently lay a hand on his arm and onto the cloak for a moment. He looks at me critically: »Do you have everything?«

I nodded. Behind me Irvan and Ticker waited too, which he didn’t seem to miss. His glare returned to Khidell: »And?«

Khidell bowed deeply again, addressing me now: »Lady Karanista, I apologize deeply for the inconveniences we have caused you. I hope you can forgive us. You will not be hindered while accessing the archives anymore. We will prepare proper accommodations for your continued stay here. We deeply regret the suspicion we have placed on you.«

Silence developed after she finished. Pan gave me a little shove and I stumbled for words: »Ah, yes. Uhm. I accept your apology and hope we have a good cooperation from now on.«

He nodded, still bowing, and only then raised his head.

Pan spoke again: »Once we have discussed things between us, we will come to the archives. You will be able to show us our rooms then.«

Khidell nodded and Pan strode out with long, purposeful steps. I followed him after a moment of surprise. He whistled and as we head down the street a giant dog joined us. Pan had such a walking speed, I almost had to jog to keep up.

 

»Where are we going?«, I called to him.

»Somewhere quiet«, he answered still in a sharp tone, but the longer we walked, the more I noticed him relaxing. We headed out of the city and up a hill. There he finally slowed down.

»This should be good«, he said, sighed and then flopped down into the grass like he wasn’t wearing the most expensive clothes I had seen on a real person. The dog followed his example.

I still stood there, looking at Pan dumbfounded, until eventually it broke out of me: »Excuse me, what the living FUCK?!«

He started to laugh and all his anger disappearing as quickly as a summer storm ending.

»No, seriously, Pan: what the fuck?!«

He sat up again, still grinning: »Well, you needed help and I came to give it.«

»You’re a LORD?!«

»Not technically…«, he started.

»No, no. I heard you. Eleventh Lord of Millguard. Panagio– I can’t even repeat the name. What?!«

»Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd«, he repeated slowly, »Yes, that’s me.«

I was pacing over the hill, Pan just watched me.

»You’re a liar!«, I eventually shouted.

»Have you forgotten all the lies we told Iron?«, he chuckled.

»No, that’s not the same! Not at all! You lied to me! For months! I thought you came with your ›connection‹, or whatever. But you turn up. Yourself! And suddenly it’s LORD?!«

»I apologize for deceiving you«, he said calm, »Nobody in the Guild knows. And that includes the leaders. Actually, Loka knows. But that’s because she’s the firstborn of King Simonkari and Millguard subordinates the King.«

»Stop!«, I shouted at him. »Give me a moment.«

He was quiet while I sorted my thoughts, gathered all the facts and pushed my memories of him into a different light. After a few minutes I had calmed down: »Alright, tell me again. Who are you?«

He actually got up for that and very calm and rather slow he repeated: »I am Panagoitis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd, Eleventh Lord of Millguard, Second of my name.«

I nodded slowly: »Go on.«

He looked confused.

»Tell me why you lied.«

»Well, it’s much easier to experience some freedom when you don’t drag around that name and the responsibilities connected to it. So while I travel, I am Pan, or Thanis, or Faber, or Kote, or whoever. And I am free to do what I want.«

»And nobody knows?«

»Some people know. In the organization Loka and now you are the only ones.«

»And your group?«

He shook his head: »They wouldn’t even know if they saw me right now.«

»Do I need to bow in front of you now?«

He laughed: »Gods, no. I mean, I would love if you could just forget this fact about me again.«

»Nope. That will be ingrained in my brain now.«

»Now, that you are over your initial shock, do you want to tell me how they actually treated you? Do I need to blow up on more people?«

He finally managed to make me to chuckle: »You were like a banshee. That was pretty epic. I would have believed that you were the wrath of the gods incarnate.«

He sat down in the grass and I joined him now: »But no, they did treat me well. Gods, Pan...«, I sighed.

»I’m sorry. Would you tell me the whole story?«

»Well, when I tried to get into the archive, they thought the document was forged. Something about ›no notice‹ or whatever. They also did the Zone of Truth spell – with my consent. But since I didn’t know you were actually the Young Lord of Millguard when you gave it to me, I couldn’t say that at that time. At least the allegation that I forged the document myself dropped quickly through the spell. They still held me on account of possible impersonation and sent a messenger to Millguard to get this cleared up.«

»I came as soon as you sent me that message. Their messenger probably just arrived in Millguard. My parents will handle him.«

»Your parents… The Lord and Lady of Millguard...«

He nodded slightly. Suddenly I grinned: »I really do need to join your birthday celebrations!«

His face became one of pain. He groaned and let himself fall backwards in the grass again. The dog moved his head and licked him over him as he came into reach. And I just started laughing out loud. What a ridiculous situation this was!

Notes:

Again, all of Pan's names are references to something. Pan and Thanis are some abbreviations of his birth name. Faber, I will explain another time. And Kote is a reference to the Kingkiller Chronicles by Patrick Rothfuss. I love Kvothe (/Kote) and incidentally they do have a lot in common. Kote also fits, considering Pan's most striking feature is his bear fur coat, which he rarely takes off. The first time I had Pan go by Kote in game, it instantly devolved into Coat.

Chapter 11: Future Duke

Summary:

An outwards look on the (future) duke of Millguard

Notes:

30 September 2023 - 578 words

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

Lord Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd of Millguard and surrounding regions was an interesting character to say the least.

He took over the throne at 30, after he had been away from court for almost a decade between the age of 17 and 28. The family had never explained where he had been in that time but many rumors surrounded the issue seeking to explain it:

- He had been lost in the Feywild.

- He was a grand adventurer, traveling the continent.

- He spent the years on sea and across the ocean.

- He jumped between planes and has danced with the gods in their own domains.

What the actual truth was, many people could just guess. When someone asked him about it, his smile turned to mischief and danger. And even though he was moving towards 70 now, he still looked like when he assumed power. This also prompted many theories, explained by the years he had been missing:

- The fey had gifted him eternal youth and life.

- The gods have blessed him with the same.

- He had been cursed by something or someone.

But the explanation most sensible and most used was that he was a grand master Monk who had gained such mastery of his Ki that he could stop himself from aging. And this theory was supported by his fighting skills.

Everyone who had seen the Lord fight knew of the immense might hiding under his good-looks. Blade or no, he could move faster than the wind, take out many dangers in less time that other people took to scream and dodge most attacks as if he wasn’t fully tangible.

The blade he carried was fabled itself. Crafted for him by one of the best blacksmiths of the continent, the blade was forged out of half bone-white steel gleaming in the light and half out of pitch black metal, consuming any light that fell on it. It’s name? Eclipse.

If you asked the Lord about the metal he just answered with a light shrug: »The gods gave it to me.« And somehow nobody ever doubted it.

 

Many loved him, some feared him. The Lord couldn’t recount how many times they tried killing him but his ears and eyes were too sharp to miss even someone invisible and no poison was strong enough to pull through his Ki. He could appear behind you, a dagger on your throat and put the fear of god inside of you.

Yet the Lord ruled peacefully, never starting a war, never hurting people without good reason and never overdoing it.

If you tried lying to him, he knew. If you tried negotiating with him, he would get his way and make you think that you wanted that all along.

There was only one reason he wasn’t King of Algoria yet: he didn’t want to be. And yet, nobody feared the moment he did. Everyone who knew him, knew him to be a just and kind ruler. His lands prospered and grew and like his ancestors he gathered the best around him.

Many equated him with the mystical God Pan. Some even said he was the God disguised. And with the tiny Pan Pipes that he had on a necklace and his ability to invoke panic in the people he threatened, it wasn’t the most absurd notion. But asking him if he was, only caused him to break out laughing: »Am I a Satyr?«

Notes:

This chapter is an interesting one to look back on. It's the first marking of Eclipse and I had already decided to make Pan into a bard, but he doesn't have any actual connections to the gods yet, which he gains later.
I had gained the black metal for Eclipse in our game, but I hadn't actually created it yet. Visually, think of Backbiter from Percy Jackson, just with a wayyy more pronounced difference between the black and white blades. The metal was actually given to Pan by a goddess, the Earth Mother, after our group helped defend a shrine of hers. Somehow Pan had sassed the Earth Mother into helping them ("We could really use some help here" and "Help us to protect you"), and not only did the Earth Mother help, but also rewarded him for his insolence afterwards.

Chapter 12: King’s Guest

Summary:

Panagiotis and other nobles are invited to a ball; chaos ensues. The King learns of Pan's truth.

Notes:

October 2023 - 2008 words

Chapter Text

»Lord Jazrueltehd, what an honor to have you!«, the King spreads his arms in a warm and welcoming gesture.

»The honor is all mine, King Simonkari. That you would think of me in this matter is the highest praise one can get.«

He smiles warm: »I do as you do: get the best. But please, come in. I am sure your journey was draining.«

I nod and we head inside.

»Lord and Lady Khamil were so kind to host tonight’s ball. And the day after tomorrow the negotiations will be held on the neutral grounds of my castle.«

»I do not want to doubt you, my King, but are you sure it is sensible to lead both sides into the heart of your realm?«

»Fear not! There will be all kinds of security measures taken. Magical and non-magical. There will not be a safer place to reside in, in two days time.«

»I am sure of that. And I am sure that you will tell me all about it tomorrow.«

»Indeed, but for now, I will but you in the trusty hands of the chamberlain. Welstam?«

A well dressed servant steps forward: »If you would follow me, my Lord.«

 

Later that day we get taken over to the mansion of the Khamli-family. It was an extravagant ball full of boot licking nobles trying to gain the favor of everybody higher up the chain than them. And for pretty much all of them, that included the King and me.

When I was younger, I had hated events like this, but years ago I had started finding fun in this peacocking and now I was able to revel in it. Where the other nobles laughed about the cheap jokes being made, I could laugh about their ridiculousness. It was a dance, it was a game and since I could see the whole board, I did not fear it anymore.

And this evening isn’t any different. At least the wine is quite good, the food on par, and the music asked for dancing, causing many to comply. So did I.

 

Until the window breaks. Someone screams. A dozen or so people clad in black, with daggers and scimitars flood in. Panic breaks out and the nobles stream away from the shattered window front. Most guards move towards it, others follow the shouted order: »Protect the King!«

Two bandits rush forward to reach the king first, but I am faster, intercepting them. One gets knocked down with a kick as I come to stop in front of them, the other gets my elbow in his stomach and as he starts to double over, I ram it onto his shoulder making his fall final. The guards were just starting to engage the attackers, one of which calls: »Take him out!« Then three of the attackers manage to slip away from the guards hurrying over to me.

The nobles were pushing through the doors, blocking themselves from leaving the room at the same time.

I pull Eclipse, the blade shimmering into existence as the runes of invisibility on sheath and hilt get broken. One side of the blade gleams in the candlelight, the other one was black like a living shadow.

I make a half step forward, striking the blade down hard. It sinks into the shoulder slicing through the armor like it was made of warm butter. I quarter turn, pulling the blade out and the bandit falls, bleeding heavily. In the same motion I bring the butt of the hilt into the face of the next guy. One step back and I raise my foot for a frontal kick. The guy grunts and falls back – but he would get up again.

All bandits are now engaged with the guards. Two of them were being taken down right now. Another one had taken out one guard and is holding his own against two more.

The one who had shouted before is back to back with someone defending against four guards.

The one I hit in the face lunges at me, but a half turn by me makes his sword miss. The other one rises and picks up his weapon. His attack goes wide and misses even without any reaction from me.

The nobles get more successful in leaving the room. King Simonkari isn’t visible anymore.

I draw my sword upward, slicing open the arm of the kicked one who drops his sword screaming. The other one flinches back but I catch his wrist and pull him to me, throwing him to the ground, delivering an elbow punch onto his spine. He was passed out before he hits the ground.

The guards manage to take down the guy fighting with the presumed leader, and one more who had fought alone. The one holding up against two guards had taken a couple hits but it didn’t seem he would go down soon. The rest are still involved but it looked like the guards were gaining control here.

I touch the pan pipe necklace and whisper: »The gods watching are not happy with your actions. They will take the stars from the skies to burn your being from the weave of planes.«

The leader and the capable bandit might not have heard the words but the spell still wraps their minds and the fear of gods takes over their emotions. Quickly I move forward, push through the lines of guards around the leader. From her point of view I appear behind her with the black side of Eclipse resting against her throat.

»I would advise you to call down your people«, I tell her in a low growl.

I feel her throat rise against my blade as she swallows. Her voice trembles as she calls: »Fall back!«

The blade cuts easily as I pull it back ever so slightly. Growling I call out: »Surrender!«

The leftover attackers see the blood running down their leader’s throat. They get discouraged and the guards quickly take them down, arresting all of them. As they move towards me and my hostage, I take down the blade. The cut I had caused was minimal, just enough to draw blood. But only as I take it away, the leader dared to breath again.

I do not break concentration on my spell yet and instead wipe the blood of my blade and sheath it. For a second sword and sheathe shimmer, turning translucent and then both are gone – or at least seem like it. I could still feel the weight on my belt.

When all bandits are restrained I let the spell go and watch the relief spread on my victims’ faces. The room had emptied now, leaving the guard, prisoners and me.

Multiple guys were bleeding badly, but a couple of moments after the fighting had stopped a guard with priest insignia entered and healed any bigger wounds with magic. Soon he hurried over to me: »Are you alright, my-Lord?«

»Fully untouched«, I nod to him calming: »Is the King alright?«

»Yes, the King was taken to safety. Our deepest apologies that you had to witness and engage in this.«

»It’s fine. I had certainly worse. Just take care of the others properly.«

The priest nods, bows and hurries over to the guards. I start to follow the nobles, striding away with a straight back. A rather scared looking servant boy was waiting on the other side of the hallway, bowing to me, as I am walking past.

»I- I- I will g-g-guide you back t-t-t-to the c-c-castle«, he stammers, clearly terrified of what just happened.

»Of course«, I just nod and he guides me outside, to a carriage and opens the door for me. I enter and just as I sit, the carriage lurches forward.

 

When I enter the castle, the chamberlain was waiting for me. He bows before he speaks: »The King wishes to speak to you. I will be taking you to him.«

I just nod and he takes me into the office of the King. He rises as the chamberlain knocks and I enter. I give a quick bow. With a hand wave the tells me to take a seat in the little couch area. I do. He pours two glasses of alcohol and offers one to me. I take it and he sits in the armchair across from me.

»I apologize for what has transpired this evening. The attackers have managed to break through the guards outside with the help of a mage. They got away sadly.«

I nod and take a sip. I felt the burn but my Ki neutralizes the effect of the alcohol. I hadn’t gotten drunk once since I’ve been 23.

»I was surprised to hear you had your sword with you«, he says after drinking too.

»I apologize for deceiving you and everyone else. In my youth there had been one too many times where a seemingly secure situation as turned sour.«

»I do not blame you. I actually thank you in this case. I am just sad that I didn’t get to see the fabled sword – and you – in action.«

»We could duel tomorrow to remedy one...«, I offer.

»And the other?«

I chuckle, set down the glass and stand up. Both, sword and sheath, flicker into visibility as the sigil gets broken again. I hold the sword for him to see. He marvels at the opposing blades, eventually reaching for it. I pull it back.

»My apologies, but please don’t touch it.«

He nods and after a few more moments I sheath it again. The runes connect and activate. I sit down.

»A masterful crafted weapon. Worthy only for a fighter like yourself.«

»You flatter me, my grace. But if you don’t mind… the incident…?«

»Yes, of course. Thanks to your help, everyone who entered got apprehended. As I said before, there must have been a mage who manged to flee. Sadly there had been a couple of major injuries but no casualties, thanks to your quick handling of the situation. The attackers, especially their leader will be questioned this night. Tomorrow we will know more. If it has something to do with the negotiations, you will be informed about it of course.«

I laugh dry: »I am almost certain it has to do with the negotiations. If I were a betting man, I would even go as far as saying they were after the negotiator.«

»Meaning you...«, the king says slowly.

I nod. He is silent for a bit, then asks: »How does it come they didn’t send someone more capable? You have a reputation after all.«

»Two possible reasons. One«, I hold up a finger, »They couldn’t afford or find anyone more capable. These people might have been all that they could rally for their course. They were acting on their own or something along this line of reasoning.«

The king nods pensively. I hold up a second finger as I say: »Or two: they didn’t know – or believe – what is said about me.«

Now he laughs like that was a stupid idea. I take a sip and smile. Let him not take it serious, that worked in my favor. Soon enough he stops: »You’re being serious.«

I nod: »Very much so, indeed. If you want to do a fun game: ask people what they know or believe about me. You will get the wildest answers. And then give those answers to the smartest people you have and ask them what they believe is actually true: only the bare minimum remains.«

He nods slowly and says after a moment of pensive silence: »I suppose that makes sense. I would not believe many things about you if I didn’t know you or Loka hadn’t confirmed it.«

»And she could only confirm because she knows me personally.«

He nods again.

»Anyway, if there is nothing else you need me for, I would like to leave you for the evening and head to bed.«

»Of course«, he rises and as do I: »It has been a long day.«

Chapter 13: Old Ghosts

Summary:

Arkaniest goes to search Pan, who had disappeared from the Guild suddenly and without a word years ago. Her one and only lead is Millguard.

Notes:

08 October 2023 - 6.821 words

Chapter Text

08.10.2023 – (Arkaniest) Old Ghosts

One day, Pan disappeared. His former group said he had gotten a message, gave them a note for Madame Kambera and left. He had not said where he was going or if he would come back. But in hindsight it was clear: he would not.

I never knew what kind of talking his former group got from Kambera but afterwards they would not speak about him. Everyone pretended like he never existed. I tried talking with basically everyone in the organization about him but there were two possible answers that I got: »Who is Pan?« or »I don’t know where he is.« Some people admitted he existed, others didn’t. Iron belonged to the second group which might have been the thing that infuriated me the most.

One day I went to ask Kambera about it. She tried giving me the »Who is Pan?« story too, but I was not going to let her off that easily.

»No, you’re not giving me that excuse. Tell me what happened to him«, I decided and crossed my arms.

»I really don’t know who you’re talking about, Arki«, she answered.

»He was in that chaos-group? With Serina, Tatiana, and so on? Lanky guy with a bear fur coat?«

She didn’t look at me and continued studying the document in front of her: »Doesn’t ring a bell.«

»Yes. It. Does. You were the one that invited him into the organization!«, I called back, »Don’t take me for a fool!«

»Maybe you imagined him. Iron had a ghost problem for a time. Maybe that was connected with your ghost.«

»YES! Yes, it was! He played some pranks on her and she started believing he was a ghost.«

»Maybe she was actually the sane one?«

»I will give you insanity, if you want«, I growled, »Just tell me!«

She raises an eyebrow and leans back, looking at me for a moment. When she spoke it was intently, words chosen wisely: »Listen, Arkaniest, it would be better if you believed me: there was never a guy named ›Pan‹ here. And if there ever was, he has cut all ties with this place. He has never been here. He doesn’t know you anymore. Let this matter drop.«

It was obvious what she wanted to tell me: for some reason he had dropped out and Kambera had decided to pretend he never existed.

»What has happened to him?«, I asked again.

»He left. I don’t know why. I don’t know where he went. But that is not important. He left.« Her words were sharp.

»If you don’t want to tell me – or help me -«

»I forbid you to go looking for him«, she interrupted me. »As long as you are part of this organization you are not allowed to look for that ghost. Is that clear?«

I bit back something and nodded stiffly. Without another word I left and slammed the door behind me.

 

Years later, I decided to leave the organization behind me. The original purpose has been fulfilled and it was time for me to learn something new. Go somewhere new. And find an old ghost…

At some point I had stopped thinking about Pan, but the feeling of »you forgot something« had never stopped. But now, that I had no more ties to the organization I was free to pursue whatever that was.

I did not have many leads, but I did have one: Millguard. He had sent me to Graton with that letter of Research made out by the eleventh Lord of Millguard. And he had promised me that it was from a legitimate source and not forged. I did not know in what relation he stood to that Lord but maybe – just maybe – that Lord would tell me. But first: Millguard.

The city had grown in the last years, the trade flourishing and the young ruler apparently doing a great job – at least that was the current information you could get in Glaboris. As it was just a dukedom and the city had no university it was not important enough that proper reports were delivered down here: I would need to see once there.

So I made my way up north and eventually I reached the land of the dragons.

Millguard was surrounded by golden wheat fields, Mills dotted in the scenery here and there. The roads in and out were often used and as I came closer the city looked like a beehive – but, to be fair, every good city did.

Entering and walking through the city I could see huge variety of people and quite a few were impressed by my two clockwork companions. I followed the winding main road deeper into the center and let Ticker fly high to get an overview. To the east a mansion stood, bigger than any other house around. I was on the way to a market. Interestingly, I could not see any slums from Tickers vantage point – every city I had been to had some slum-ish part; sometimes bigger, sometimes smaller. But I had heard this city was wealthy and apparently it had enough money to hide away its poverty.

Coming closer to the market place, I saw many shop signs which interestingly showed a real name. It was no ›Black Anvil‹ or ›Potion Potpourri‹, they all were in a form of ›[Name]’s [Type of Shop]‹: like »Horith Zinrieth’s Carpets« or »Rugtheus’ Glass Works«. And some of these names I recognized. There was a master potter; that one was a famous dye-maker. And there was »Dihkaor Baltufk’s forge« and that name rang a bell. Iron loved his work.

»Ticker, didn’t Pan use sword oil from that guy?«, I asked the little wyvern. He clicked confirming. And he had made that replacement oil himself. Iron had treasured it. Had Pan learned the recipe from that dwarf? That couldn’t be, right?

I looked around, suddenly feeling lost. I needed to find a tavern and talk with the innkeeper. Maybe they could tell me some pieces of information.

As I looked back, a few kids were looking at Irvan impressed. I smiled: »You can try petting him, if you like. He’s just not very soft«, I told them. One of them stepped forward and reached out, letting Irvan smell his hand and only after he did he petted him.

»He’s hard...«, he noted.

»That is true. Do you want to ride him?«

The kid looked at me with big eyes and I went over to them. Quickly I picked him up and set him on Irvan.

»He’s haaaard«, he complained again and slid off after a moment: »But still cool! Are you new?«, the question went to me.

»Yes, I just arrived in the city. Can you tell me where I can find an inn to stay at?«

»Does your bear need a stable place?«

I chuckled: »No, he doesn’t.«

»You can go to the Bronze Jug down the road!«, he pointed one way.

»Alright, thank you!«

»You should give your bear some fur. He deserves it!«, and then he ran off with the other two kids.

 

I started heading down the road and quickly find the place. It was a well kept inn, with a large tavern room and a full floor of rooms upstairs that you could also reach through a flight of stairs outside.

I let Irvan wait outside as I head inside. The tavern room was clean and as it was the middle of the afternoon rather quiet. There was a group of people playing cards, some waiters cleaning after the midday-rush and one lounging behind the counter. He looked up as I entered and smiled: »Greetings!«

»Good day«, I smiled too and headed to the bar: a long piece of furniture, five meters at least, crafted out of a single piece of wood as it seemed.

»How can I help you today?«, he asked warm as I came closer.

I took a seat at the bar: »I could take an ale for a start. And I will need a room for a few nights at least.«

He filled up a mug for me: »A single room? Do you have any special needs?«

»I do have a metal bear with me, he does not make messes but I would need a bit bigger room. Otherwise yes, single would be fine. Thank you«, I drank some of the ale.

»Maybe a double room would be better then, one bed can be pushed to the side so your bear has enough space?«

»That would be a good idea.«

He nodded and left for a moment, coming back with a book and a key: »That would be five silver per night. Would that be alright?«

I nodded and pulled out two gold: »I don’t know exactly how long I will be staying but I am sure it will be four days at least.«

»That sounds fine. When you leave, we can talk about your tab«, he explained and opened the book: »Might I have your name?«

»Arkaniest Karanista«, I answered and he wrote it down with the amount I had paid, what room I was taking and when I started staying here.

»Alright then, thank you. You will be in room 17«, and he handed me the key.

»Thank you.«

»Anything else I can help you with?«

»I am actually looking for an old friend of mine. Maybe you know him?«

»Oh, hit me! I can’t promise I’ll be helpful but I definitely try!«

»I haven’t seen him for some years, so it might be a bit foggy, but here goes: I am looking for a like … 30? 35? years old human, male; his name is Pan. He has dark brown hair, bronze skin, brown eyes. He is a Monk, very well trained. Back when I knew him he always wore a bear fur-coat and a sword...«

The innkeeper thought for a bit: »I mean, Lord Jazrueltehd might fit that visual description? I wouldn’t know about the trained parts...«

»Lord Jazrueltehd. I – um… can you tell me about him?«

»Well, he is a human, 36? I think? What do you want to hear exactly?«

»Is he the Eleventh Lord?«

»Yes, he is. Eleventh Lord and second of the name.«

»The name? What’s the full name?«

He looked pained for a moment, inhales and on the exhale he quickly rattled down: »’anagiotis Thanassasis Bert Jazrueltehd? Or something along the lines. I am so bad with names, I am sorry.«

»It’s ‘anagiotis-Thanassis«, one of the servers chimed in as she passed. »There’s a silent P in front so maybe you are looking for him?«

I looked doubtful: »That can’t be… Can it? Is there a possibility to speak with the Lord?«

»You can always try going up to the mansion, tell one of the servants what you want. Otherwise, you can hope to meet him in town?«, the barman spoke again.

»Is he a people person?«

»Oh, definitely!«, he laughed, »He was a rather wild kid and is still friends with a lot of craftsmen here.«

»Did he learn with Dihkaor by any chance?«

He shrugged: »Could be, but I wouldn’t know about that. I can’t say I’m that close with him...«

»Alright, thank you. I’ll try my luck at that mansion. I can’t say I am confident about it, but well… better than no lead at all.«

»You could try leaving a message there today still, maybe tomorrow you will get an answer«, he suggested.

I nodded and finished the ale: »If you say so. I’ll try.«

»Good luck on your search! Also, since you aren’t from around: maybe you could be interested in visiting the clock maker. They are basically under the mansion of the Lord. The entrance is ›next door‹ of it«, he made air quotes and winked at me.

»I’ll keep my eye out, thank you.«

And with that I left again, heading east to the mansion.

 

It was a huge building but got even more impressive as I got closer. On the walls green and gold-ish banners with the insignia of Millguard hung, two, three stories high and that was just the front. With Ticker I could see there was a huge garden area around the already large house. The entrance was hard to overlook: a massive iron gate with guards posted at it. I almost got anxious, casted a quick Prestidigitation on me and then approached.

»I’m sorry, hi. Uhm… I wish to speak to Lord Jazrueltehd? Is that at all possible?«

»Do you have an appointment?«

»I do not. Could I make one?«

»Certainly, although the Lord is rather busy at the moment. What is the matter on which you wish to speak with him?«

»I am looking for an old friend of mine. He had some relation with the Lord but I have to admit that I don’t know what kind. I was hoping the Lord could offer me some information to find my friend?«

The guard nodded: »Do you have a letter explaining the situation with you that I could give the Lord?«

»I do not. I just arrived in Millguard and I am just going by instinct a bit...«

He nodded again: »You could speak with one of the servants and explain the situation if you would not mind waiting a few minutes?«

»No, I have time.«

»I would ask your … animals? To wait outside, if that is alright?«

I nodded and Irvan laid down next to the gate. Ticker flopped himself onto him. The guard opened the gate and lead me to the entrance of the mansion. He knocked and a servant opened. They exchanged some quick word and the servant then points me to a seating area inside: »Please wait here.«

I nodded and sat down. The inside of the mansion was... exactly what I had expected from everything else so far. There was no way Pan would be living in this fancy place, I thought.

After some minutes a dashing-looking winter-elf came over: »Greetings, my name is Nathaniel Thethyra. I heard you were looking for someone, Miss?«, as he approached, he stopped for a moment, did a half bow, waited another moment and then continued to me.

»Yes, uhm. My name is Arkaniest Karanista. I am looking for an old friend of mine.« I wasn’t sure if I should bow too? Could I do that seated or did I need to get up? I ended up doing an awkward bow on the edge of the seat. »I am sorry, I am not from these lands or knowledgeable about... like… the exact court protocols?«

»That is alright.«, he smiled warmly and sat on another armchair: »Please, tell me about the person you are looking for and how you think Lord Jazrueltehd can be helpful in finding them.« As he spoke he took out a notepad and an ink pen – but no inkwell.

I started giving him the rundown of information and he just starts writing without dipping the pen into any ink.

I ended up scrambling my words: »I’m sorry, I like your pen.«

He chuckled: »Thank you. Have you not seen any of that type until now?«

»No, I don’t think so. How does it work?«

»Some ink is stored inside and it flows out while you write it. I don’t know exactly how it works but it is rather nice. A new idea from Brush.«

»Brush?«

»A crafts-person here. She is still working on them so I don’t think you will be able to get your own yet.«

»Could I take a closer look?«, I asked.

»If you don’t mind, I would ask you to focus onto the matter at hand, Miss Karanista.«

»Yes, I apologize. So: Pan, human, mid thirties, brown hair and eyes, bronze skin. A skilled fighter of a monastic type. But he carries a sword. And I – I saw him last like ten years ago? He was part of this organization ›Force of the Right Hand‹ for a while and then vanished. He had this uhm… like… magic orb. And one day he gave me this official letter of Research to get into the Archives in Graton. The document was signed by the Eleventh Lord of Millguard. Pan did tell me he had it from a proper source and I was hoping that maybe the Lord would know who he gave the letter to back then and could help me find that person...«

As I talked I watched the pen gliding over the paper with ease.

»Anything else?«, Nathaniel asked after I was silent for a moment.

»I think those were the most important parts.«

»Alright, Miss, I will speak with the Lord about it. Do you know where you are staying while you are in the city?«

»The Bronze Jug. Room 17«, I answered quickly, only adding the room number because the address felt so short otherwise. He wrote it down too.

»Thank you. We will send word to you there. Although it might take a bit of time, as the Lord is rather busy at the moment.«

I nodded: »Of course.«

»Anything else?«

»I don’t think so, no… Just: Pan is not the Lord, right? The innkeeper said the description might fit onto him but… that’s not the case, right?«

Just maybe, my face displayed distress – a plea for it not to be the case.

»The Lord has not been part of any organizations, so no, that cannot be the case«, he answered in the same warm but matter-of-fact tone he had been speaking in the whole time.

»Alright, thank you. Thank you for your help«, I rose stiff and give a bow too.

»It was my pleasure, Miss Karanista.« And he guided me to the door.

Just after I left I stop again: »I heard the clock maker would be around here, can you point me in the direction?«

»It’s just around the corner and the door there. But I think you might be a little late today. They closer rather early.«

»Alright, thank you.«

»Have a good evening, Miss.«

And then he closed the door and I went to scope out the clock maker’s shop to come back the next day.

 

Inside Nathaniel went straight to Panagiotis’ office. Panagiotis had been overworking himself the last days and that would be a good distraction. He knocked and after a grumbling: »Yes?«, entered.

Panagiotis was hunched over his desk, reading and writing letters, contracts and negotiations. The air was basically filled with darkness radiating from the Lord.

»What is it?«

»Someone is looking for you«, he said factually.

»They can come Wednesday like everyone else«, Panagiotis mumbled and let his gaze drop again.

»No, you don’t understand. They are not looking for Lord Jazrueltehd of Millguard. They are looking for Pan.«

He looked up a bit surprised – or was it irritated? – either way some of the darkness lightened.

»And who are ›they‹?«

»A lovely Elf of the name Arkaniest Karanista.«

Now the bad energy around him fully lifted and turned into shocked surprise instead: »What?!«

Nathaniel grinned: »Yes, she is looking for you. She asked for an audience with the Lord so he can help her find her lost friend.«

»Well… That’s…«, Panagiotis shrugged after a pause: »We can look for Pan together. How did the conversation go?«

Nathaniel gave him the rundown and as he did Panagiotis leaned back in his chair, pushing his hands on his face. He sighed loudly after Nathaniel finished.

»Did I do anything wrong?«, Nathaniel asked.

»No, you did not. You did everything as right as you could have. Just… of all people it’s her.«

»If I recall you spoke fondly of her.«

»Yes! That is the problem! Everybody else I would give some excuse why I cannot help them but it’s her. She is here because she is genuinely worried about me. And you did just tell her I am not the person she is looking for even though you knew that was a lie while you were saying it«, he was pulling his lower eyelids down, still facing the ceiling.

»Any ideas what I should be doing now?«

Nathaniel was silent for a bit before answering: »Two general directions actually: Official and unofficial.

»You either invite her as the Lord seeking to help her and oh it turns out you are actually the person she is looking for and I was just uninformed and told her something incorrect…«

Panagiotis grunted, unhappy with the idea.

»Or -«

When Nathaniel finished talking a grin tugged on the lips of Panagiotis’ mouth.

 

 

 

The next morning, as I was sitting at breakfast a messenger from the mansion came in and handed me a short letter: »Pan has been sent for. Please wait until he arrives.«

And because I had nothing better to do, I headed to the clock maker again, met Moe and Brush and clicked so hard with the little Tabaxi, that Moe had to throw me out in the afternoon. I returned to the inn where no further messages have arrived in the meantime so I went to my room to tinker a bit with the new skills that Brush had shown me.

As always I don’t notice how quickly the time passed as I do so and only when I needed to get up to light some candles, I realized how late it was. I opened my window and went downstairs to check for a message again.

When I returned to my room I hear Pan-Pipe-music from outside. I looked out the window but saw nobody. But something about the music… Something drew my attention. I sent Ticker out to check.

And on the roof Pan sat and played. He hadn’t changed much: still wearing that huge coat, a sword by his side, sitting on a roof like the Gargoyle he was.

Ticker descended onto him, basically flew into his face and Pan stopped playing to catch him. He was laughing – it almost looked like he was glowing from inside – even as Ticker tried to scratch him. I felt two things: »Oh my gods, he is alive and safe and as perfect as the day I last saw him« and »That little shit! I’m gonna kick his ass so badly!«

Laughing Pan asked Ticker: »Will Arkaniest also try to kill me when I go to her?«

»I will kill you should you not move your ass down here immediately!«, I called out of the window. He laughed and let go of Ticker before getting up and walking over the roof until he was right above me. With the same dexterity as years ago he jumped down, caught himself on the drainpipe and moved over to my window. He pushed his head up and grinned at me: »Hi!«

I smacked him with a wrench: »That was for disappearing!«

»Owww! It’s nice to see you too, Arkaniest. Do you mind that I come in?«

I stepped aside and quickly he swung himself inside. He looked a bit older, more mature, a few wrinkles, and dark shadows under the eyes. Otherwise: it was still Pan.

»Hey, Arkaniest«, he smiled.

»Don’t just Hey me!«, I tried to smack him again but he ducked away: »It’s been ten years since you just vanished!«

»I am sorry. I apologize. It’s nice to see you again«, he said soft.

And I just hugged him. He hugged me back tightly. He smelled well washed and not like he spent most of his days in nature anymore.

After a while he let go: »How have you been?«

»Good, good. But: where have you been?!«, I sat down on the bed and Pan leaned against the table.

»Here. Mostly. I probably owe you an explanation...«

»Yes! You do!«

»I had gotten a message that my mother got very sick. I took over her work and it just got busy for me. Even if I had had the time to write you a message, I just didn’t think about it, if I am honest. And then time passed and … yeah… I’m sure you know how it is.«

»I am sorry about your mother. But that still only explains half.«

»Half? What do you mean?«

I crossed my arms: »In the organization they pretended you were never a part of it.«

»They did?«

»Kambera told me – indirectly – that you cut all ties with it. She even forbid to go looking for you. Explain that to me.«

»I can’t speak for Kambera...«

»Stop that. Don’t give me excuses. She would not have done that without reason.«

»I don’t know why she does things or not. But yes, you’re right. When I joined I told her I want a way out, to cut ties with the organization in case something happens.«

»Why?«

»That’s... complicated. Or maybe I’m just paranoid. With all the traveling I did, I sometimes got into a pickle with people going after me and whatever, so I just decided: I would only join if I could cut ties and then nobody would – or could – come after me.«

»That makes sense. But fuck you!«

He laughed out.

»You could have at least told me.«

»I’m sorry. I could have, yes. And I don’t have a good explanation to why I didn’t. I had hoped you didn’t care enough. But I was wrong.«

For a moment both of us were silent until he said: »So you left the organization?«

»What?«

»You said Kambera forbid you from going after me. You left the organization?«

»Yes. I wanted something new in life. And they fixed the problem they had with the inter-planar teleportation. After that quite a few left...«

He nodded: »That makes sense. What are your plans now?«

I shrugged: »The first one was to find out what happened to you.«

He nodded again: »By the way: I still owe you something else.«

»You owe me lots of things, but what exactly do you mean?«

»I once promised you you would see me dressed up...«

I took a moment to remember and then nodded. He continued: »The Lord invited both of us for dinner tomorrow«, and he pulled out an invitation and grimaced as he spoke on, »I will need to dress up for that.«

»Oh. I am surprised he wants to see me after you were found that easily.«

»Something about ›Meeting the Researcher I had trusted‹ or something«, he shrugged.

»That’s nice. I heard he’s rather busy.«

Pan just shrugged.

»But back to you: what have you been doing all these years?«

»Oh, this and that. But mostly around here. Only light traveling. I had to talk over more work and that kept me busy.«

»What are you working as?«

»I am working for the Lord actually, a general dogsbody, chief cook and bottle washer, wherever I am needed.«

»That makes sense. You were always up to everything.«

He chuckled: »That is true.«

»Hey, I have a weird question.«

»Everything about this meeting is a bit weird. What is it?«

»I saw that the blacksmith here is the same name as that sword oil you used on our first meeting. And you did brew some yourself then. Did you learn that from the blacksmith here?«

»I actually did. I hung around him a lot when I was young. Making that oil was a way for him to keep me busy and from touching the hot metal he pulled out of the forge. Technically there is a small chance that I made the batch that Iron had...«

I laughed a bit: »So when you pretended you didn’t know who made it, you lied.«

He grimaced: »Well, are you surprised?«

»I suppose I shouldn’t be. You lied to Iron often enough...«

»Sorry.«

 

We continued to chat for an hour or so. I did notice that he dodged some questions and never got specific about what he was doing, but I decided to let it go.

At one point I asked: »Say, where do you live?«

»In the city?«, he asked back like it was the most obvious thing.

I gave him a look of ›Seriously? You know what I mean.‹ and he chuckled: »I can show you after the dinner tomorrow, if you like.«

»Why not today?«

»It’s late, Arkaniest. And I should probably clean up before I am visited by a lady.«

»That’s fair… But tomorrow!«

»Promise«, he smiled, »But maybe I should get home, it’s late.«

»If you want. I’m not gonna throw you out«, I smiled. He did too.

»It was great seeing you. I am looking forward to tomorrow.«

»Yes, see you tomorrow. I am glad you are well«, I got up and hugged him again. He huffed in a chuckle and hugged me back.

»Sleep well, Arkaniest.«

»You too, Pan.« I let go and he left – through the door. I barley believed my luck. I found him – after one day in Millguard! Or at least he had found me. And I was excited for tomorrow when I would see him again. And when he would finally fulfill his promise of dressing up. And I would see where he lived so I could always find him again!

 

The next day I got myself some proper clothing for the occasion and in the evening I went back to the mansion, all dressed up and excited.

I approached the gate and told the guards: »I was invited for dinner«, and I showed them the invitation.

»Yes, please follow me, Miss«, and we head inside. A servant took over at the mansion ad lead me through the pompous halls. It wasn’t a long walk until she stops me in front of a door: »Please wait here for a moment. Lord Jazrueltehd will be with you momentarily« And then she left.

It didn’t take long and Pan turned a corner and came down the hallway. He was followed closely by the winter-elf Nathaniel.

Pan apparently hadn’t spared any expenses when dressing up. From the bottom up he was wearing forest green leather boots; wheat-yellow trousers; a green button-up jacket-type thing? – a frock? – with golden buttons and threads. Around one shoulder he wore a short cloak in the same color combination. On the back part was the crest of Millguard. A golden chain went from his shoulder, where the cloak was fastened to a button on the frock. On his side, a sword hung in a sheath of green metal with golden ornaments; the hilt was expertly carved and also sprung the crest of Millguard.

Pan started smiling when he saw me: »Arkaniest! Good evening! It’s great to see you. How are you?«

We hugged lightly while I stammered: »Good, hi, um, good evening.«

Nathaniel bowed to me as well, silently.

»You look good, Pan.«

»Is it not too much?«, he spread his arms a bit – I noticed a ring on his fingers but didn’t get a good look of it yet.

»Well, you let me wait 10 years to see it, so no.« We both chuckled.

Nathaniel went over to the door and opened it: »Please enter.«

Pan didn’t hesitate and stepped into the room. It was smaller with a table set for the dinner. On each side a chair stood and Pan gestured to me to sit on one long side of it while he passed me. We sat down. And… he was sitting down at the head of the table.

»Wait, isn’t that normally the seat for the –«, I began saying and noticed only two places had been set.

»Wait. Stop.«

He didn’t say anything, just smiled formally. He had lain his hand on the table and now I could get a good lock at his ring. It was a signet ring with the crest of Millguard.

I closed my eyes and sorted my thoughts for a moment. Then, very calmly I noted: »I fucking hate you.«

»No you don’t«, he answered soft.

»You’re the Lord?!«

»I am sorry for deceiving you.«

»No, you’re not!«, I called out.

»I am a bit sorry. I suppose I should properly introduce myself: My name is Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd. I am the Eleventh Lord of Millguard, second of my name. I left the organization because my mother got sick and I took over many of her responsibilities before eventually taking over the government affairs. And those are no lies.«

I was too stunned to speak for a few moments.

»But he… He told me that...«

»He lied on my orders. Cutting ties with something goes both ways after all. Kambera pretends like I was never part of the organization; I pretend the same. And if anyone comes by asking about any connection with it: I did not have any«, he explained patiently.

»That makes sense. But –«

»Fuck me?«, he chuckled. I turned red.

»Should we eat?«

»Yes, please.«

He nodded to Nathaniel who did something and soon two servants entered and served us the first course.

»And you live here?«

»I live here, yes.«

»But this is so unlike you!«

He laughed: »Tell me about it. I got used to it. You don’t choose where you are born.«

»No, you don’t«, I sighed a bit and ate the soup.

Then I started laughing out of nowhere.

»What’s so funny?«

»Remember when you gave me the research letter?«

»Yes, what about it?«

»Just imagine I knew back then. The future Lord of Millguard handing me the that important document while he is not wearing shoes, after a night where you had fallen asleep on a chair.«

He started laughing too: »Good times. Oh, I miss them.«

We reminisced for a while and were served the second course.

Eventually Pan said: »Arki, I want to invite you to say here for a while – if you like of course.«

I smiled: »I would like that.«

»Can I provide you with a room here or would you prefer to stay in the inn?«

»Oh, ehm… Either is fine«

»If you decide to stay at the inn, I will take over the expenses. I totally get should this place be a bit too much for you«, he said quickly, seeming a bit stressed out.

I laid my hand on his lightly: »It’s alright. I am fine with either. I am happy to stay in the inn and catch up some more and if you would like to host me, I’d like that too. But Irvan and Ticker should be allowed to come in.«

»Yes, of course, they will. We can give you a room with a ramp so Irvan can go in and out as he likes. I hope he will be friends with Ruffos.«

»Who’s Ruffos?«

»He’s our Mountain Dog. I can introduce you two later.«

»I would love that too«, I smiled and he did too, before he stifled a yawn.

»Are you alright?«

»Yes, I had a long day, but that doesn’t keep me from enjoying some time with you today.«

And so we continued the evening. Chatting about loads of different topics. Pan was much more open than the day before and I eventually came to accept and slightly forgive his lies and deception for such a long time.

We sat there even after we finish eating, drinking wine and telling stories.

Eventually, the candles on the candelabra were burning low and we decide to end the evening: »Just come by tomorrow with your things and you’ll get situated here«, he told me while he was leading me back to the entrance: »If you have any questions, you can just ask any of the servants or Nathaniel. I’m not sure how much time I can spend with you, but I’m sure a few hours won’t hurt anyone.«

 

The next day, I moved the mansion – and what a mansion it was. At least the servants weren’t too stuffy or ever condescending when it came to me not knowing my way around the place or didn’t follow noble-protocols.

I did get a few hours every day of Pan. Sometimes it was lunch or dinner, one time he showed me around the mansion, introduced Ruffos or showed me the library. One time he went out to town with me and one day he took me up to the dragon lake. And the rest of the time, I explored Millguard, Millcoast and the surrounding regions. On Pans recommendation, I made friends with Dihkaor, even if the dwarf was quite one worded and more focused on his work.

What I noticed with time moving forward was that Pan was often tired. He stifled many yawns and the dark rings around his eyes seemed to grow each day. When I asked him about it, he dodged the question, giving some half-excuse about »A lot of work«, but I still felt something was off.

 

One day I crossed paths with Nathaniel.

»Sorry, do you have a second?«, I stopped him.

»Yes, of course. How can I help you?«, he smiled warmly.

»About Pan...«

»Yes?«

»He seems tired. Is he alright?«

»Yes, he is fine. But he does have a lot of work to do.«

»Am I keeping him from any with the time he spends with me?«

»Not at all. You are a welcome distraction for him and balance him a bit.«

»But I feel like every day he’s a bit worse… You’re his personal servant; don’t you notice it?«

»I do«, I noticed Nathaniel looking around before he continued more quietly: »He doesn’t sleep much at the moment, to catch up the work he misses when he’s around with you.«

I narrowed my eyes at him: »Would it be better if I left? So he could focus on his work?«

»No!«, he called sharply and quickly, before he touched my elbow and pulled me into a side room – the furniture there was covered with sheets: apparently the room wasn’t used for a while now. He also continued on Elvish, speaking quickly: »I probably shouldn’t be telling you this but I know he trusts you. He was already overworked before you came. There is a reason why normally noble children take over their parents positions of powers when they get married: it’s a two-person job. With his mother falling out of the picture, he first took over her parts and then more and more of his father to help him out too. Since Lord Taraxacum is currently away, he is doing everything right now. And with the new trade relations that he forged in the beginning, it is just overall a lot.

»When you arrived, I saw him genuinely smile for the first time in a while. If you left, he might get some more sleep, but he will not get better.«

»What would you recommend I do?«

He gave a light head shake: »It’s not my place to say...«

»But…?«

»You could try to take some work off him.«

I nodded slowly: »How would you recommend me do that?«

He shook his head again: »I really can’t answer that. What I will say is that Pan takes an hour before lunch to play with Ruffos every day.«

I nodded: »I understand. Thank you.«

 

A bit later I sat outside, cuddling Ruffos and playing catch with him with a massive branch. He and Irvan raced each other to catch it. Eventually Pan came out.

»Fancy seeing you here«, he smiled.

»You too. Taking a break from work?«

»Ruffos gets sassy with me if I don’t give him enough attention.«

I scratched Ruffos’ cheek as he put down the stick again: »Good boy. Giving him some time out.«

Pan joined me on the other side of the dog and Ruffos gave a deep »Ruff.«

»What is that supposed to mean?«

»Oh, nothing«, I smiled. Ruffos laid down between us and started turning on his side. I may or may not have spoken to him before to get Pan to sit down with him and the plan worked perfectly. Pan moved to pet his belly and Ruffos laid a huge paw above him, effectively trapping him in a hug.

»Yesss, big guy, I am here«, Pan continued to pet his belly.

»I might have already tired him out«, I smiled cheekily.

»As long as he had fun.«

Ruffos gave another deep »Ruff« and moved his paw trapping Pan. But Pan didn’t argue and as I continued to play with Irvan, he quickly fell asleep.

 

About an hour later, Nathaniel came out to look for Pan. He first noticed me, smiled and winked at me, then looked over at Ruffos and Pan.

»He might have fallen asleep«, I winked back.

»It’s time for lunch«, he just said, went over to Ruffos and with a whistle and a gesture he commanded the dog to let go of Pan. The motion woke Pan who looked around in a daze for a moment.

»It’s time for lunch, Sir«, Nathaniel informed him. Pan got up and patted the dog hair from his clothing.

»Do you want to join me, Arki?«

»I would love to.«

Chapter 14: Soldier

Summary:

Pan fights a boar in Wolkrim

Notes:

1 November 2023 - 1223 words

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

BOUNTY: Feymire Boar

For destruction of property and

endangerment of local population!

 

Whoever brings proof of the beasts demise to the guards will be rewarded greatly

 

Be warned: The beast is very dangerous and requires skilled hunters or adventurers to be taken down

 

Signed: Ruler Narmud

 

And next to it the local royal’s seal.

»Sure, why not?«, the adventurer thought to himself. He had slightly bronze, weather-beaten skin, brown hair that fell to his ears and wore a big bear fur-cloak. He didn’t look like too much in this state, but his eyes were clear and sharp and one could only assume the same about the longsword by his side. He moved with purpose, every step a decision well made as he walked to the edge of the city. Two guards stood attention there, watching over the fields surrounding the city.

»Excuse me? Where can I find this Feymire Boar?«, he asked one guard right out.

The guard sized him up with his gaze. The boy might look almost twice his size with that coat but underneath was a slim, young body, not even 20.

»That beast is for someone more capable to slay«

»Come ooon, humor me...«, he casually laid his hand on the knob of his sword.

The guard shrugged: »Go that way, towards the edge of the forest. You should be able to find the tracks easily. But be warned: that beast bested our best guards!«

»How many of them?«

»It has hurt 30 by now.«

»And how many at a time?«

»8. Three of them lost limbs.«

The adventurers noded unimpressed. What the guards didn’t know about him, was that he once was trained as a guard and he could defeat most guards 5 to 1.

»It’s suicide trying to take down that thing alone«, the guard continued as he saw him seriously consider the opportunity.

Then the adventurer shrugged: »We’ll see about that.«

And he smiled and walked away.

»What name should we write on your tombstone?«, the other guard called after him. The boy turned again, walking backwards for a few steps: »Thanis!«

 

The boar could be heard from further away, a beast towering the adventurer with it’s 2 ½ meters. It was routing through the field with tusks the size of his leg. But it didn’t hear Thanis coming who moved silently over the field like he had played hide-and-seek on farmland his whole childhood.

He crept closer to the boar until he could smell it’s stinking breath. And then he dashed forward, launching himself on top of the giant. Before the boar knew what had hit him, he punched down his sword into the top of his head. But he hit the skull, unable to pierce that and the boar roared up, throwing the boy off it’s back. He rolled himself off, coming to his feet again before the animal could turn. And as the animal started turning, Thanis dashed along his side with it, ramming his sword down to the hilt into the stomach of the creature. It roared again, in anger and pain, body slamming the man who went flying – never letting go off his blade. He tumbled over the ground, eventually managing to find his bearings again and getting up with an exhale. That moment the boar charged at him and he threw himself to the side to dodge it. But he was a split second too slow. A razor sharp tusk sliced over his face. As he hit the ground again, the fighter stood his sword up and the movement of the boar above it made it slice it’s own skin open. The wound might not have been too deep, but it was large and by now the was bleeding heavily from three injuries.

Thanis got to his feet again, pushing with the back of his left hand against his face, feeling his own warm blood pool over it. He had no time to feel pain right now or worry about how close the wound must be to his eye; the boar was turning again.

And he waited.

The boar screamed, scraped the ground and then charged again. And Thanis jumped in the air. He caught himself on one of the massive ears, his feet sliding over the back to a stop and before the beast can roar up, he stabbed his sword in one of it’s eyes.

The boar winced, screamed, almost threw him off again, but the sword was stuck in his right hand like glued into it and he didn’t let go. For a moment he struggled to pull the sword free and keep on top of the beast but then he managed.

»One more«, he mumbled and let himself drop off it’s back. The boar turned during another roar but Thanis moved with it again, stopping on its side again, pushing his sword in between two ribs and with all his adrenaline-fueled might he pulled it upwards, slicing open the animal that screamed again and collapsed. Thanis found where the heart should be and pushed his sword in. A horrible squeal escaped the boar before it stopped moving fully.

Thanis exhaled, pulled out a cloth and cleans off the blade, before finally sheathing it. He then wiped over his own wound again: it was bleeding heavily and with the tension passing it started to hurt badly.

But he wasn’t done yet. He needed some trophy, so he took out a dagger and got to work sawing off a giant hoof. Maybe it was good he couldn’t distinguish the blood of the beast from his own blood dropping from his face. He just knew that his face hurt. And the longer he took, the worse it got and the more tired he got.

Furthermore, even the foot alone was heavy and he needed to tie some rope around it to lug it after him, instead of carrying it in his arms. Eventually the only thing remaining was dragging it and himself back to the town.

 

He gave a sorry – and frightful – sight as he dragged himself towards the two guards. The boy was covered in blood, bleeding in the face heavily and looking like he had ran for three days straight.

When the guards saw him coming, one of them took of running to get a healer, the other hurried towards him.

»You survived, boy!«, he shouted excited and Thanis gave a grin, only to regret it seconds later as the pain flared up. He stumbled a few steps and the guard caught his shoulders just to be safe.

»Jep is getting a healer; it’s good to see you alive.«

Thanis stepped back a bit, out of the hands of the guards and announces: »The beast is dead.« He gestured to the hoof on the ground: »You’ll find the rest of it that way, towards the edge of the forest.«

The guard just looked stunned.

»This is the only injury I have. You guards need to step up your game«, he grinned again, only for it to change into a grimace of pain immediately after.

»We’ll take care of that«, the guard said about the hoof – and the rest of the boar – »You go into town, get yourself some help.«

Thanis saw the almost panicked expression of the guard, nodded and started to head into town.

Notes:

This was once supposed to be a triptych Soldier / Poet / King in reference to the The Oh Hellos song with the same name. Poet and King never happened. And yes, this is the fight that leads up to the Spring Festival Chapter where Pan meets Equadorius.

Chapter 15: Real Name

Summary:

Equadorius tries guessing Pan's name while they lie with each other

Notes:

02 November 2023 - 633 words

Chapter Text

The next evening Equadorius and I laid side by side again. His dark eyes were looking softly into mine, his hand resting warm on my chest. His hair was damp with sweat.

»I heard one of your companions call you ›Pan‹ today. Is that your name?«, he asked softly.

»You tell me«, I answered equally soft. For a moment he closed his eyes. Then a whisper of my name escaped his lips and I felt a soft pull on my being. But Equadorius furrowed his brows, opened his eyes again and shook his head lightly.

»It’s part of your name...«, he decided.

I nodded softly and traced my fingers down his breastbone.

»One day I will know. You could just tell me.«

»Wouldn’t that be boring?«

»You tell me«, he smiled too as he gave me back my words.

»You enchant me enough without possessing my name«, I noted, pushed forward and pressed my lips on his. I felt the smile on them still and he returned the kiss happily.

»The things you could do with it, I can’t imagine«, I continued teasing when we parted.

»Would you like to know?«

»Tell me about it.«

»I could demonstrate…?«

I chuckled: »Very good try.«

This time he pushed a kiss on me and silenced my chuckles.

»You could be mine, Pan«, he said while I stroked over his ear. He had a dreamy look in his eyes.

»And what would you do with me?«

»All kind of things«, he answered.

»Do you want to demonstrate?«, I grinned and bit my lower lip lightly.

He grinned back too and slid his hands down my arms. He grabbed a-hold of my wrists and started pulling my arms apart, turning me on my back and moving on top of me to sit on my hips. I let all of it happen smiling. He pulled my arms up and bowed down, kissing me lightly at first but soon more forcefully. I pushed my head up to meet him, kissing his ears as he nibbled on the base of my chin and my throat.

»Pancho«, he tried between caresses. I wrapped a leg around one of his giving a little chuckle: »Are you really trying to guess it?«

»You think I would strive so low, Darpan?«

»I think you would, Equadorius«, I try going for his mouth but he kept me pinned down by a gentle grasp on my arms.

»You’re not gonna escape me, Kopano.«

»How many names with Pan do you think you can think of?«, I grinned and wrapped my other leg around his hip.

»I think I have a few more, Pantalion«, he moved his lips over my collarbone.

»How much do you need of my name for it to work?«

»How many nobles are there with your first name?«, he pulled his arms down on mine again. I try to follow his to lay them around his neck but learned he had tied my wrists to the bed. I wasn’t sure how, but it didn’t matter. It was a soft material; maybe his shirt that had been pushed into the crack between mattress and bed frame.

»We will be here all day, Stepan.«

I grinned and with a thrust I threw both of us up. And as I did, I turned my body around, to lie on my stomach – but since I was still holding Equadorius with my legs, he fell under me. I pushed my lips on his again. It’s a long kiss in which I managed to escape out of the loose knot planting my hands on his shoulders. When I pulled back to breathe I noted: »You talk too much«, and I pushed down on him again. I didn’t let him get another word in for a while.

Chapter 16: Cracks

Summary:

Someone notices the cracks in Pan's daily mask.

Notes:

30 November 2023 - 556 words

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

Panagiotis was letting his cracks show and he didn’t stop himself from doing so. All the time he was so composed, joking around and smiling so you didn’t think he had a care in the world. But now he was letting them show and he looked like a vase smashed and glued back together.

The biggest crack was naturally coming from the whole thing at the dragon lake – the refugees; the robberies; the ritual; Zahela, Xvatil and Tiamat; Reth who had tried to steal Ruffus and who had seriously wounded Pan back then; next Serina and the beast of shadows and nothing, able to use Tiamat as a chewing toy. All of that was enough to paralyze a man with worry but Panagiotis had to work through all that and look further. He had to make sure Millguard, the dukedom, and the whole land in a way, would be alright and save. His duty as a noble born and heir to the lands was to make sure his people were thriving. And he had to make sure he’d survive this turmoil too as he did not have siblings who could take over the dukedom.

Those were the obvious – the biggest cracks – but not the last. After all his mind was also occupied by the Artifact in his pocket: the combat orb requiring souls to work and that held the conscious of a friend; the Death Urn on his belt to collect the souls; and Arkaniest in Graton investigating this whole ordeal in his stead; the not-so-slim chance that she would meet the crazy inventor that put Jarami – and the rest of his platoon – in these orbs in the first place. He had to pray to Helm that she would be safe.

Whatever she would uncover was the next crack. That and Jarami’s fate should it be bad. Not to mention the fates of the souls that already had gone into the orb.

The strain of the last months had also produced cracks. He was not used to belonging to a group and hiding his true identity was as taxing as almost dying two times in two missions with them. If his parents knew how close they had been to loosing him, they would chain him in his rooms so that no more harm would befall him. Hiding that was also tiring.

And then there were the little things too: Nathaniel’s illness for example.

All of it produced cracks in Panagiotis and as he drank the alcohol without it having an effect, all the cracks showed in his face, around his eyes and the rest of his body.

He finished the drink, put the glass down and opened his eyes. Curious they looked at me and as he shifts the cracks vanished from sight again.

»You look like you’ve seen a ghost«, he noted with a little laugh in his voice that didn’t reach his mouth or eyes.

»You look tired«, I just gave back.

»Nahh. Can’t be. After all, I was just running around the whole city today and fought some monster in the past. Why would I be tired?«, he brushed it off with a joke, yet speaking the truth.

I nod silently. He wouldn’t tell if he didn’t want to – I learned that a long time ago. And he didn’t want to.

Notes:

This is basically straight out of our home game. All these things were things Pan had to deal with, some he still has to deal with, whether he wants it or not. As I just recently said to another player: "There is always a conversation about duty to be had with him."

Chapter 17: Poisoned

Summary:

Pan drinks poison. Nothing happens, everybody is confused.

Notes:

5 December 2023 - 983 words

Chapter Text

Panagiotis sat square between two other Lord of Algoria and enjoyed a not completely dull conversation with Lord Sensua to the left of him. He could have imagined more interesting ways to spend the evening but there were definitely worse ways too.

Yet Panagiotis’ attention was divided. At some point during the preceding banquet he had noticed one of the servants side-eyeing him multiple times and now he was watching that servant from the corner of his eye. Panagiotis had the strong suspicion this boy – barely fifteen – was up to something.

So when the boy stepped up to refill the glass of Lord Basker to Panagiotis right, Panagiotis kept close watch on him, despite being turned to towards Lord Sensua on the other side.

And as Lord Sensua was explaining how he – meaning his gardener but for the Lord it was one and the same – had managed to cultivate the rara Selasflower, Panagiotis’s hand suddenly and lighting fast shot up and grabbed hold of the lower arm of the servant with a grip hard like a steel clamp. Lord Sensua got startled by the quick movement while the boy squirmed under the tight grip.

Panagiotis politely told Sensua: »I apologize, I need a moment to handle something«, then turned towards the boy without moving the boy’s arm.

»And what will that become once it’s done?«, Panagiotis spoke soft but polite, looking at the hand of the servant that had been pouring something from a small vial into his glass. Despite the Lord’s soft tone, the squirming of the servant alerted everyone to the situation and slowly the conversations died down, everybody watching the scene. All of them saw the vial over Lord Jazrueltehd’s glass, and all of them watched as the Lord reached out with his other hand, took the glass and to everyone’s horror, drank it. He swirled the liquid in his mouth for a moment and then swallowed as if he had just tried a new wine.

»Tamasroot and Blightpowder?«

Everyone froze up at these words, some people gasped appalled. Each was a powerful toxin but together they were deadly, causing major internal bleeding leading to an excruciatingly painful death mere hours after the ingestion. And they had all seen the Lord swallow.

The Prince signed to the guards and when they grabbed the boy, Panagiotis let go. The servant boy gasped and dropped the vial when the grip lets up, which was the only perceivable sign of how strong it had been. The vial shattered as it hit the table.

Prince Simonkari stood suddenly and bowed: »Lord Jazrueltehd!«, he spoke, his voice audibly shaking: »I profoundly apologize for letting an event like this transpire!«

He stood straight and hurried over to Panagiotis: »If we move quickly and bring you to the court healer you might be–«

Panagiotis raised his hand consolingly: »This won’t be necessary.«

»But if this is really Tamasroot and Blightpowder–!«

»Oh, I am sure it is and I am happy to bring my glass to the herbalist to confirm my claim, but I assure you, I will not require any kind of healing«, he spoke calm and certain.

No matter his word, he could see the objection forming on the Prince’s face and added: »I am also happy to let myself be checked out by your healer.«

The Prince nodded gravely, waved to the servant that had been exclusively serving him – his valet – who now came up and bowed before Panagiotis: »If you would follow me, my Lord.«

Panagiotis nodded and stood up. The glass was taken by another servant and carried after him. The valet had a brisk tempo but Panagiotis could have easily outrun him still – the other servant had some problems keeping up though.

 

They hurried through the long, pompous hallways and eventually came to the medical wing. Panagiotis was asked to wait in an elaborately decorated examination room while the two very stressed out servants went to get the chief healer, to stress them out too.

And soon enough a mature woman dressed in a healer uniform knocked politely and instantly stormed in.

She bowed quickly and deeply: »Lord Jazrueltehd! My name is Elfaso Gwarthsburogh. I am the master healer and–«, she was speaking fast, clearly wanting the formalities over with. Panagiotis interrupted her and said friendly and calmly: »Thank you. There is no need for these formalities while everyone worries pointlessly sick about my well being.«

»Pointlessly?«, the healer asked surprised.

»I am not poisoned.«

»Lord – you drank from the poisoned cup...«, she said in disbelief.

»That doesn’t mean I am poisoned though.«

Both, the healer and the valet look very confused for a moment and like they wanted to argue, but then they remember their – and his – places again and shut up.

»But please, test if I am poisoned or give me the antidote for all I care. After all, that’s the only way to stop everyone from worrying.«

The healer nodded: »This will only take a moment… if I may?«

She came over to him and started to reach out. He nodded and offered her his hand. She mumbled a few word, while touching his hand. Unsurprisingly Panagiotis didn’t feel any different afterwards.
