»Depending on how strong the poison was, it might have already made some damage. You might experience a stomach upset, vomiting, diarrhea or nausea. If any of those symptoms or any other discomfort befall you, please report it to us or call for us.«

»I am sure I will be fine«, Panagiotis just repeated calmly another time.

»I pray for that. You are free to leave«, she bowed deeply.

Panagiotis just bowed his head: »I thank you«, and got up, leaving the room.

»I will retire to my room. Please tell Prince Simonkari that I am and will be fine«, he told the valet who bowed. Then everyone headed into different directions.

Chapter 18: Bard Whispers

Summary:

Pan wakes up kidnapped by a strangely contradictory bard.

Notes:

17 December 2023 - 3126 words

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

I woke with a start but with a heavy head at the same time. There were a couple things that I noticed immediately thanks to my little jolt. Number one was the most obvious: I couldn’t see thanks to a bag on my head – at least I couldn’t see more than spots of light coming through the weave.

Second were the rope bindings tying my writs together and keeping my hands behind the backrest of the chair I was seated on. With a little shuffle of my feet I learned that those were also tied together but not to the chair at least.

Third was the thick headache I could trace back to getting downed by a club when I was taking a shortcut through a backstreet. How much time had passed since then? I wasn’t sure. Where was I now? There was no way of knowing. But I was alive which meant the kidnappers wanted something off or from me. Either way it meant I had an opportunity to get out of here alive.

I moved a bit, testing the rope binding. There were just a bit too lose and I could reach the knot with a bit of fidgeting so given some time I would get out of them.

»Sleeping beauty is finally awake«, a voice says a bit to my left. I could hear a chair being pushed back and someone with heavy boots came over to me, before pulling off the bag from my head.

A surprisingly young looking elf – half-elf? – with a kind face and wheat colored hair pulled in a bun stood before me. From looks alone, he was certainly not the type to be involved in shady business but he did have me kidnapped.

The elf wore loose, colorful clothing and had the sharp eyes of someone overlooking the playing board and moving the pieces from the background, without over getting his own hands dirty. Still, one of his hands rested on a rapier handle hanging off his belt on the side.

»For all the trouble you’ve already caused, you were surprisingly easy to capture, my dear. One thing that wasn’t troublesome about you«, he smiled a smile like sunrise and deep cuts. A man of opposites. I stayed silent, just looking at him curious.

»You’re not all tears and begging. This is not your first time being kidnapped?«, he asked chuckling. A certain melody was laying in his voice as he spoke.

»I’m sorry. Do you want me to?...«, I offered and the man laughed louder. And as he did he had his eyes closed and I continued fiddling with the ropes.

»No. It’s good. At least you know the drill then«, he answered after a bit.

»This is just like any other Thursday, Sir?«, in the last moment I pulled up my voice into a question, as I realized this could very well be a woman just passing as a man.

»Sir is right. And good, you know your manners too. So let’s be quick about this whole thing. We’re both rather busy after all. What’s your name?«

»Sven, Sir«, I answered the first name popping into my head.

»Sven? Interesting. If that is what you would like to be called then I can certainly do that, but I will inform you, that’s the only lie you get this evening.«

»Lie, Sir?«, I played dumb.

»Oh, please. Don’t pretend. The drunken bee inn doesn’t have a ›Sven‹ renting a room, yet you go to sleep there every night. Save your games for the next Thursday if you will – and someone more gullible than me. Now: what is your name?«

»Bernd«, I provided the name I rented the room under. It was also a fake name but my kidnapper smiled content: »See, that wasn’t so hard, was it? And what is your connection to Mr. Savos?«

He took a few steps back and I used the moment to look around the room: it was a surprisingly nice office, a large desk to the left of me, walls filled with shelves and cabinets. Nobody except us was here but I suspected this guy’s goons waited outside.

»I took a quest for him after coming to town«, I answered truthfully.

»And the content of the quest?«, he leaned against a shelf.

»Find some missing documents«, as I speak I realized there was a high chance I was talking to the thief.

»And that’s the only thing you’re supposed to do?«

»Extra points for tracking down the thief of course but I don’t really need to tell you about a quest hanging off the notice board for everybody to see, do I?«

»But it’s not hanging there, is it?«, he asked in a playful tone.

»What?«, I was confused.

He didn’t answer the question. »What other connection do you have with Mr. Savos?«, his words were suddenly sharp, his whole demeanor switched. He looked quite scary as his eyes basically glowed dangerously now.

»There is none. The tavern keep handed me the questing page when we first talked«, I recalled.

»And you’re sure that’s all there is? I suggest you tell me the truth«, he pushed a few fingers in a pouch on his belt while speaking.

»Yes. I have never met him in my life«, I answered easily, not even noticing the spell twisting my words before they left my mouth. But I had spent enough time with spell casters and fey to notice the setup for casting a spell so I steeled my mind, calm it and end whatever influence he had gained on me.

»What are your connections to nobility then?«, he continued.

I caught myself from asking ›Nobility?‹ like the village idiot that I was – and instead answered: »I do not have any connections with nobility other than the odd job I did for them in the past?«

»Explain to me your sword then?«

»My sword? What about it?«

He walked over to the table and picked up my sword. As his back is turned I pull on the bindings and free myself, but I do keep my hands behind my back as if still tied.

The elf stretched out my sword towards me, holding it on the sheath to show me the handle: the beautiful and expertly carved handle with floral engravings, the Millguad crest and my Initials – all four of them. I did have to overplay my surprise and shock, but the other didn’t seem to notice.

»I nicked it from this kid once. Probably some noble boy playing hero. It’s a good blade so I kept it but covered up all that for obvious reasons«, I explained and was this close to grabbing the sword as it was basically offered to me on a silver platter. But when I looked up, I saw the narrowed eyes of my kidnapper before he pulled it away again and puts it on the table.

»Who is Jazrueltehd?«, he struggled with the name.

»You sure you wanted me tied up in your office and not your bed with that much stalking you’ve done?«, I chuckled. He did catch something I would have preferred to keep under wraps. He must be pretty perceptive – which was interesting as he hadn’t notice my ropes were on the ground.

He turned back around to me, a daring smile on his lips: »You really want to go that route, Bernd?«

»You started it, mystery man. Although, gotta say: buy a man dinner first.«

»That’s not my style. Now: who’s Jazrueltehd?«

»It’s a nice story actually. Over in that city I did a quest to gather fireroot. It was medicine for this fiiiiine lady – terribly sick though and fated to die – and we had a flirt and a bit more«, I winked at him, making everything up on the spot, »And well, I did travel on but I’ve been writing her of my–«

»Shut up!«, he thundered, the tip of his rapier suddenly on my Adam’s apple. I swallowed and felt the sharp, cold metal push against my skin. I had to fight the urge to raise my hands – as they were supposed to be bound behind my back.

»Kinky«, I still muttered looking up the blade at a not-at-all amused person.

»I understand why you get kidnapped this often with how annoying you are«, he growled.

»I try my best«, I tried another flirtatious smile. But his gaze was dark, no joke left. I tensed up, ready to strike at him before he could attack me.

»Say that Sven is your name«, he commanded and it sounded like a threat.

»My name is Sven«, I spoke slowly and as the words leave my mouth, his eyes basically flare up and he drew his blade back to strike me.

With the tension I had built up, I launched myself towards him, striking him once, twice, three times with my fists while landing my knees on his chest and toppling him over. With the first strike I hit a nerve center, stunning him and he dropped his blade. The other punch caused him a bloody nose. Quickly, while he was still stunned, I drew his dagger and cut the ropes on my ankles. I took his weapons with me as I headed over to the desk.

»You thought I was in over my head all of the time, but it was actually the other way around«, I told him. On the desk my things were spread out and I put on my sword on my belt.

Behind me, the man rose with a little whistle. It filled my mind, raking it in pain. And following the urge the spell put on me I moved away from the table, away from him. I also realized I hadn’t expected a bard to be my kidnapper.

He came after me to the table and picked up his own weapons again.

»I wouldn’t be so sure of your dominance«, he smiled dangerously. I didn’t dare go after him. Even with a bloody nose he was terrifying – maybe even more with than without.

Next I knew he shot forward, slicing my shoulder with his rapier. The hid didn’t just feel like he was hurting my flesh but also poisoning my mind again, filling it with voices and pain.

I did what I do best: I punched him – at least I tried as I swung wide all four times, the psychic pain doubling my vision making me miss. He still retaliated and I spat blood. I drew my sword and sliced at him, somehow missing again, silver barbs drawing my blade off course. The following two punches though aim true and the second knocked him unconscious.

»Fucking hell. What an asshole«, I muttered, spat out more blood and set out to tie him up and gag him. A bard this malicious wasn’t allowed to run his mouth.

Then I gathered the rest of my things, putting my coat on and wrapping the sword hit once again. Furthermore I searched through the desk and shelves only to come to the realization that this wasn’t the lair of the bard but the office of Mr. Savos in his factory. I actually started laughing about how genius and stupid this move was of him. He had my respect for that.

Nonetheless, once I was done with snooping, I picked up the unconscious man throwing him over my shoulder and left the factory through the unlocked backdoor – the same he had brought me in probably.

I took him over to the city guard post. explained the situation that he had kidnapped me but I managed to break free and overpower him and that he probably was the thief that had stolen from Mr. Savos. I explained why he was gagged and recommended them to keep it one but warned them about him choking. Then I explained that I would head to bed because it was an hour before the crack of dawn and I wasn’t feeling too hot after the fight but that they – or Mr. Savos – could send for me should the need arise. The officers let me leave after some more questions of their own and off to bed it was.

 

I woke slowly from unconsciousness. That fuckhead’s fists were too hard, I thought with pain all over my body. As I opened my eyes and the surroundings blurr into existence I realized: he had brought me to the guards and I was tied up and gagged in a prison cell. Of course the town with the bloodsucking patron would employ inhuman mannerisms for their half dead prisoners. But I did hear guards outside and they hated their prisoners dying without giving them information first, and I also knew of a way that I could get rid of the gag quickly.

I started pretending to vomit and choke on the gag with such a level of conviction and volume that the guards were in my cell in less than 10 seconds, pulling me off the ground and removing the gag with a panicked haste. I kept it up for a bit longer, gasping for air and spitting out spit and blood from a split lip and bleeding nose, providing a great performance altogether.

»You’ll kill a man with a thing like that!«, I yelled at them between gasps.

»Ugh, yes, sorry«, one of the guards gruffed and put it away.

»What even is going on? Why am I tied up!«, I cried out further, pretending to be an innocent bystander.

»You are arrested on charges of theft, kidnapping and battery«, one of the guards explained.

»Wha– And you believe that? Look at me! How would I be capable of doing any of that?! If anyone got assaulted it’s me!«

»You– You–«, the guard fumbled to make my words make sense, »The man who brought you in said he overpowered you in a fight after you kidnapped–«

»Me?! Kidnapped him?! If anything we were fighting because he tried to set me up!«, I shrieked, »Assaulted me in the middle of the night only to – I don’t know! Get a quest reward for finding a thief? I never did anything! He just wanted a scapegoat!«

All three guards exchanged confused, fearful and panicked gazes – they believed me.

»Probably hoped I would die before I could tell of his misdeeds! If there is no evidence I demand to be let go! This instance! And I want damages for the harm done to me!«, I continued rebelling. The guard shifted even more uncomfortable and I knew I had them hooked. They were seconds away from letting me go.

Then one of then nodded, another cuts the rope before the third one helped me up to stand. I gladly played the injured damsel in distress. They helped me out of the cell and let me sit on a chair in the front room as one went to fetch my things – and maybe a bit more.

»Can you tell us where you’re staying? So we can inform you about the investigation? Or find you should the need arise?«

»The drunken bee in! That fiend is staying there too. I saw him. Probably decided to use me as his scapegoat when he noticed me coming home late.«

»What were you doing out so late?«

»I had visited a lady«, I answered in the most demeaning tone. The guards looked a bit flustered and the third came back with my pouches and weapons, as well with an additional pouch of gold as damages.

»We apologize for all of this, we will find that criminal and you can get proper damages then. Please stay in the city until we have found him.«

Dramatically weak I put on my things and nodded: »Of course. I am looking forward to it.« Then I rose unsteady and moved towards the door, still angry. They didn’t try to stop me.

Once I’m off the street the prison was on, I passed myself a healing word, get my things out of the Blue Skies tavern and left town before anyone could guess it.

 

A knock on my door woke me. Tired I get up, grabbed my sword in one hand and opened the door with the other, hiding the weapon behind the door.

Two guards stood there, one of them I recognized from the previous night. I wasn’t sure how long it had been, but it wasn’t a full rest yet.

»How can I help you?«, I asked stifling a yawn.

»Good morning, Sir. You will need to come with us.«

»What is going on?«

»You’re under arrest for assault, battery and wrongful imprisonment«, one explained.

I blinked surprised: »What?«

»You heard me, Sir. It would be easier for both of us if you’d cooperate and come with us.«

»Just… what are these charges based on?«

»The man you’ve brought in tonight has explained how things really went down and–«

»You believed him?«, I didn’t keep the disapproval out of my voice, »Is he still imprisoned or did you let him go?«

»We let him go...«

I groaned, then sighed: »Alright. I’m coming with you but I want you to apprehend him again. Right now it’s word against word and neither has evidence. It wouldn’t be fair if one ran free while the other didn’t.«

»That’s– Alright. Fair. Leave any personal belongings here, we will make sure the key to this room is kept safe.«

I nod, open the door a bit wider, placing my sword behind it. Then I let them watch me put on my shoes and after stepping out and locking my door, I handed them the key. I let them take me back to the jail and continued my rest there, as I wait.

 

Hours later, when I woke, there was nobody in the other cell or with me.

»Excuse me?«, I called to the guard keeping watch outside.

»Yeah?«, he came in.

»Did you find the other guy?«

»Uhm, no, Sir. We’re still looking.«

»How long are you already looking?«

»About five hours.«

»How long will you keep looking before realizing he ditched down after lying his way out of the cell?«

»Uh–«

»You won’t find him. And now we have a net zero. Although one of us could be found where he’d said he’d be...«

The guards nodded uncomfortable, finding sense in my words: »I’m not allowed to make executive decisions. I will need to talk to the captain.«

»Do that. I’ll be waiting here.«

It took another hour or so until I was let go with apologies. I had listened to the heated debate in the other room. Apparently the bard convincing them to be let go, made them question reality – understandably.

 

 

I have commissioned a scene of this story by the wonderful rain / kusanyagii

Notes:

May I introduce you to Larkspur. They are a level 5 bard I made for a game that then never happened, but I love them just as much as Pan. They are intersex and will respond to any pronouns.
I did play out the fight between Pan and them, and let me tell you: Larkspur is terrifying. I was using the level 8 version of Pan, and at one point both characters had 19 hp, and both were more than capable of inflicting more than 19 damage per round. I was afraid. Suggestion is also a really weird spell that I have opinions on. RAW Pan probably shouldn't have been able to break the spell with Stillness of Mind, but the spell is too broken for its own good.
I also rolled for when Larkspur was speaking with the guards, and legit rolled 2 nat 20s and nothing below a 15. As if the +8 and +11 modifiers weren't enough. Honestly, had Larkspur stayed in the city, Pan would have been in serious trouble.

Chapter 19: Lyubystok

Summary:

Pan makes friends with a contradictory bard and they plan to do a crime together.

Notes:

19 December 2023 - 3434 words

Song Lyrics by »The Crane Wives«. Songs in order: »Ribs«, »Steady, Steady«, »Hand that feeds«, »Shallow River«, »The Crooked, The Cradle«, »I Ain't Done«

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

Thanis was watching the bard set up their instruments with great interest. Since he had started playing his Pan Flute and learning for a bard, he had new interest in the high bards and this one certainly knew what she was doing.

The Bard was a half-elf with long beautiful cornstalk hair, a large, extravagant green hat and light green clothing you could move well in. Thanis was certainly not just enamored by their craft alone.

The inn had a little stage where they were currently pushing a special made drum in the right position to their stool. Then they spent some time carefully retuning a delicately beautiful lute. The inn was filling slowly by tired workers and crafts folks.

Thanis sat at the bar to the side, where the bard now came over to get themselves a last drink before the show.

»You have a very interesting setup there«, Thanis spoke to them soft.

They smiled: »You need ingenuity when you're a bard traveling alone but used to playing songs normally performed by multiple people.«

»I am looking forward to learning from you in that care. See the tricks up your sleeve.«

»You will see them alright. Don't put your expectations too low though«, they smiled flirtatious.

»Too low? Never«, Thanis smiled back and winked. He might not have been certain of their gender but he didn't actually care either. You could have fun with either and all.

»Then you buy me a drink later if you are impressed?«, they asked before taking a big gulp from their ale.

»Gladly. I'll buy you one even if I am not impressed and just have a good time. Thanis is my name by the way.«

»I'm looking forward to the drink then, Thanis. I'm Lyubstok.«

»A unique name for a unique bard. Fitting.«

They chuckled, looked around the room, then at the tavern keep and nodded: »Showtime.«

Lyubstok stroked over Thanis arm – or more exactly the bear fur coat he wore – before heading back on stage. They put on a chain of bells on one ankle, and a special sole on the other foot, tests both out, then took their instrument up.

Thanis watched the crowd fall silent as the bard started playing an echoing rhythm, picking the lute strings gently but with such expertise that it sounded like two people were playing. With the lightest movement of their ankle they imitate a brushed over Cymbal. And just as they start singing, a hit with their other heel defined the beat.

Marrow made a wife of Eve
But no one gave up a rib for me and mine

They sing soft, their voice filling the whole room and silencing the last conversations and noises.

My hearts did expose to the elements
Calloused and untouched by a man's design
Oh, my ugly organs
How lucky we are – how lucky we are

And with a minor illusion cast offhand while playing they actually created a second voice echoing the last line. Thanis was definitely impressed. He knew the song. He had seen it performed a couple of times by groups of three or four bards and none of them had ever played it as good as this.

In the brief instrumental, Lyubstok started playing the foot drum with the attachment on the sole, always continuing the rhythm on the lute.

They played through the song, making playing for three look easy, casting echoing minor illusions offhandedly and figuratively enchanting the whole room with this song of wanting.

When the song came to an end, a deafening, captivated silence filled the room before the first few people start applauding this masterful performance.

The bard smiled a warm, gentle smile: »Thank you, thank you! I know many of you had a draining day at work, but I invite you to lean back, relax and listen. If you know the songs, feel free to join in when it’s appropriate!«

Then they pull up the lute again and they start playing again, plucking the lute in a rolling rhythm, soon joined by the drum and their voice:

Steady, steady

A small amount of people joined in, providing the supporting voice, Thanis being one of the few. Together they sung, guided by Lyubstok.

You know when you're ready
It's a long road walking into the sun
the heat can make you lose your head
Your sense of direction
Oh steady, steady
I am ready
to be the one

With the last line the lute switches to hasted playing, like someone running.

I can take for better but for worse can't condone
Most of all for good just makes me ache to be alone
How long is forever?
I'm swimming in the dress like a child in her mother's clothes
This ring around my finger's like a chain around my throat
Are you so sure you tamed me?

The bard sung the lines with passion, pulling along some people in the audience and Thanis noticed them grinning as they get completely enveloped in making music.

Then the part is done and it’s right back to the soft rolling melody from the start. A couple of lines like that, then back to back to the rushed parts. It was longer now, and in the right moments Thanis provided the claps made by bard groups here. This singular bard smiled over to Thanis as they hear him do so.

And suddenly the only thing remaining in the song is a singular string being plucked and their voice softly going

Steady, steady
You know when you're ready
It's a long road walking into the sun
The heat can make you lose your head, your sense of direction
Oh steady, steady, we weren't ready.

The bard stopped, seemed to silently sigh and took a swig of their drink. Their gaze seemed sad when they started playing again, a waltzing pattern first, switching over to a rolling legato melody while the other continued with a minor illusion. Both break away into longer strokes when they begin to sing:

I've seen good men spoiled
Chained to their jobs like hounds
They work and sleep and work again
In the darkest nights they howl

Nobody joined singing along in this song. It was said that this was the life story of the bard that wrote this song, just that nobody knew where the song actually came from. One day bards started playing it and telling the story, one pointing to the other, pointing into nothingness.

Lyubstok played the song like a devil, with passion and emotion, whispering some parts, screaming out others, plucking on the heart strings of everyone present like they were strings of their lute.

It was a song of loss and rebellion. Of workers breaking their chains and how freedom was more important than money. Thanis started pondering the choice of songs, especially in this city. Like Lyubstok was trying to get the people to rebel and wake up.

There was no pause between the songs, just a momentary break in the lute playing and the switch of the rhythm played on the drum. Then a a different melody started.

Red sky morning,
Lovers' warning
Oh I know that the promise you wear, well, it ain't for me
Shallow river
Shining within her
Flood it all, drown the crops, but just please don't swallow me

Thanis smiled as he recognized a rather unknown, rebellious song, made up of multiple voices singing. They were right: they were used to playing songs for more than one bard.

But as they sang the next line, it was only Thanis joining in the echo.

And so it became a duo between them, Lyubstok nodding over to Thanis after the first chorus.

Once the song was over, Lyubstok grinned over to Thanis and called: »You know ›The Crooked, The Cradle‹ too, Thanis?«

»I do«, he grinned back.

»Come on up, it’s more fun singing it with a partner«, and Lyubstok winked at him. Thanis took a last swig, before heading onto stage, with a sudden bit of stage jitters.

Lyubstok drank something too while he got his applause. Thanis wasn’t dressed a bard with the bear fur coat and traveler clothing, but he had sung well enough before so nobody minded.

When he was ready, Lyubstok started playing the lute again, adding in the drum just before they start singing, Thanis providing the second voice.

There's blood in the water
There's blood in the water
The quiet are restless, the silent are still
 
I'm nobody's daughter
I'm nobody's daughter
My enemies crawl, we're alone with the kill
 
If mercy's abound
I'll be safe, I'll be sound
And the devil won't know all the love I just couldn't let go
I won't pretend
My season won't end
But I pray
When it's done, when it's through, I'll have something left for you

To Lyubstok surprise, Thanis pulled out a Pan Flute towards the end and added to the instrumental part where they would have normally casted a minor illusion.

They did the second go round, Thanis playing again, then the instruments fall away as their voices intertwine in the first part of the chorus. They were looking into each others eyes like lovers. The instruments reappear with more force and both of them were grinning.

The crowd broke out in applause, once they fell silent.

Lyubstok grinned dangerously and it somehow reminded Thanis of someone who he met years ago.

»Another one?«, Lyubstok asked.

»Alright«, Thanis grinned back.

Lyubstok started a fast, staccato play, managing two voices their his lute. Thanis pulled up an eyebrow but nodded. He knew the song. Either way, it would have been too late to stop now.

I am a pretty young thing
I am consumed with selfish wanting
Carelessly broke you down but I ain't done
 
Felt love's searing sting
And you expose my nightly wandering
You put me in the ground, I ain't done

Lyubstok sung the first part, Thanis the second.

I have all the time I need
She leaves her sleeping spouse
To repent my wicked deeds
Where by dark, she meets her lover
Plant my guilty seed
A woman in love has no regrets
By the moonlight in the cemetery

And like that they continued singing the duet, grinning like idiots in love, both consumed by the music they created, even if Lyubstok was doing eight percent of the work.

»Give it up for Thanis everyone! Thank you! Thank you all!«, Lyubstok called and put down their lute, giving a hand to him too. Thanis bowed extravagantly.

»Thank you for letting me join, Lyubstok! It was a great honor«, he grinned and clapped their shoulder, redirecting the applause back to the original bard. Together they bow again and both head off the stage to get a fresh drink. Lyubstok was jingling clacking with every other step alternating, which made Thanis laugh.

»I legit love your extra setup. It’s specially made, I suppose?«

»Yup. And the bells are the change jingling in my pocket«, they winked and Thanis laughed.

»I’ve never seen a bard play only those songs«, he said.

»Those songs?«

»Yeah. Nobody knows who wrote them but they popped up in the last century or two. Do you know who wrote them?«

»Nah, I also just picked them up. But with your knowledge of them I can ask you the same: do you know who wrote them?«

Thanis shook his head and both drink from their ales.

»You don’t look much like a bard. But you sing like one«, Lyubstok noted with some respect in their voice.

»I’m not much of one. It’s just a little side interest I’ve picked up during my travels. At the moment I’m mostly relieved that I didn’t diminish your performance.«

»On the contrary. It was fun playing those duets. They don’t feel right with just audience participation or continuous minor illusions.«

»It’s amazing how you cast it so offhandedly!«, Thanis exclaimed and now it’s Lyubstok chuckling.

»Everything is possible when I have my lute in hand«, they winked and drank some more. Then they rose again: »Break’s over. You stay here, before I fully loose the limelight.«

Thanis nodded dutifully and watched Lyubstok go back on stage, playing a second set, this time with songs of other bards and general crowd pleases.

When Lyubstok finishes and packs away their instruments, Thanis ordered another drink for them. He reached it towards them and received a blindingly warm smile from the bard, moving like they were dancing with each step.

»I have impressed you then, even in the second part?«

»Very much so. I’m a big fan of how you make music. Although, I am curious about the themes of your songs.«

»Theme of my songs?«, Lyubstok said surprised, but Thanis could tell without any trouble, that they were impressed he picked up on it, and not confused by what he meant.

»›The ring around my finger is like a chain around my throat‹, ›Waste it all for the title / Waste it all for the crown‹, any of ›The Hand that Feeds‹?«, he asked with a half laugh, quoting from the songs.

»Nobody ever pays attention to the lyrics«, they answered nonchalantly.

»You had to have noticed that«, Thanis noted.

They shrugged and answered: »You and I are friends of empty graves, then«

Thanis picked up on their message and nodded. In a flirting tone he asked: »Can I convince you to play a bit more for me upstairs?«

»Maybe with«, Lyubstok smiled, lets their hand stroke over Thanis’ hand, caught a finger and gently pulled him with them.

They end up in Lyubstok’s tavern room, Thanis hung his weapon belt on the wardrobe and his coat over it, before Lyubstok could notice the invisible longsword pulling it down on one side.

»You are the bard that wrote all those songs without author, right?«, he then asks, watching the reaction closely. And Lyubstok catches their hand for a moment before saying: »I am really not. Just because I play them, doesn’t mean I wrote them.«

Thanis spoke soft: »It’s hard to lie to me, just so you know. Why don’t you put your name behind them? You could be famous! Some of your songs already are!«

Lyubstok sat down on the bed and leaned back, looking at Thanis for a moment.

»Fame isn’t my goal«, they say after a moment, »It’s a way to survive while I live my life.«

»The hand that feeds doesn’t work so well when you have more than change in your pockets, does it?«

Lyubstok started laughing at that, rose and walked over to Thanis with dance in their step. As they came close to him they spoke, stroking down his arms and chest suggestively: »So that’s the kind of bard you are! A silver tongue-d devil.«

»Says the bard who wrote Metaphor«, he answered slyly and bowed down to kiss Lyubstok. And to very much no ones surprise was it a good kiss.

»So about your rebellious music, do you want to do more than just sing to the crowd?«, Thanis whispered when he backs off.

»Do you have anything in mind?«, Lyubstok answered interested and Thanis noticed he was planting on fertile ground.

»Nothing definite yet, but I am sure it will not be entirely legal...«

Lyubstok laughed: »Is this supposed to scare me? Not entirely legal is my middle name.«

They stepped back and tie their hair into a bun. And as they do, Thanis realized why Lyubstok was familiar. And as he did, he also knew he played with fire: almost a decade ago, in another corner of the land, Lyubstok had kidnapped Thanis for meddling and to get information out of him. Thanis had managed to get free and eventually knock out Lyubstok, bringing them to the city guard. But Lyubstok not just managed to talk his way out of the cell but also denigrate Thanis before ditching town. Thanis was glad they were fighting on the same side for now.

»Anything on my face?«, they asked and pulled Thanis out of his thoughts and staring.

»No, you just looked familiar for a moment.«

»In life, you always meet twice. So maybe this is number two«, they said shrugging and Thanis knew Lyubstok hadn’t remembered him yet.

»So, what are your skills, silver tongue?«, they asked and sat cross legged on the ground

»Waitwaitwait. What is your plan? Because you seem to have one already.«

»Plans, ideas, whatever. We’re going after Evisca.«

Thanis nodded as he heard the name of the local not-technically-a-lord-but-rich-asshole-in-charge: »And free his slaves.«

»We can agree on that.«

»And what is your plan?«, Thanis asked again.

»What are your skills?«, Lyubstok gaveback.

»I’m dexterous and fast. I have a silver tongue and the ability to put it to paper too.«

»Alright. That could be useful.«

»What is the plan?«, Thanis tried again.

»We break in, kill Evisca, free the slaves and get out of there again«, Lyubstok explained like it was a cake recipe.

»We need to get the paperwork for the slaves too, so they can actually be free. Also: kill him?«, Thanis wouldn’t have gone that far.

»Yes. He is not the type to change when scared.«

»How are you so sure about that?«

»Because I’ve tried that already a few years ago«, Lyubstok explained with a darkness and venom in his voice that scared Thanis. »I’ll string this pig up and spread his innards for the world to see. If you want to back out, I’ll recommend you not get in my way.«

»What did he do?«, Thanis asked.

»Apart from the obvious of choking the land and the people he should take care of? He’s a rapist and a killer, using lives like tissue paper. He’s a cancer ever growing.«

»I understand. Let’s get rid of him. Get in, get the paperwork, free the slaves, kill Evisca. Not necessarily in that order.«

»You’re getting into the spirit. I can get us in and out no problem. But then things are up in the air.«

»Do you have any idea of the layout of the place?«

»Not really.«

»I can do some information gathering tomorrow during the day. Talk my way into his home, take a look around, report back.«

»You sound like that’s the easiest thing to do for you.«

»Silver tongue«, Thanis winked.

»Alright. If you can find out where he has his office, that’d be great.«

»That’s not my first time infiltrating a noble’s home. Trust me. I will know the place in the end.«

»I’ll look forward to seeing the tricks up your sleeve«, they grinned, giving back Thanis words from the beginning of the evening.

»Any other plans you have for this night?«, he just smiled flirtatious again.

»You wanted to make some more music, silver tongue?«, they smiled back the same way.

Neither of them slept much this night, fooling around, making music with and without instruments.

Before lunch, Thanis went up to the mansion: »I’m a messenger from Sire Hazebone of Turinao. I have a message for Sire Evisca.«

»You can hand me the message«, the servant answered. It was an older gentleman, well dressed.

»It is a verbal message, only meant for the ears of Sire Evisca«, Thanis gave back formally.

The servant gave him a once over, then nodded: »Please follow me, Sir.«

He guides Thanis through the house and Thanis took in every little detail. Thanks to his own experience in larger houses like this, he quickly got an idea where everything was located.

And the servant brought him right where he hoped: Evisca’s office. Now the only thing Thanis needed to do, was make some shit up for Evisca to believe and he was proficient in that too.

After 10 minutes Evisca sent him out of the room gloomy. As Thanis was leaving Evisca reached for a folder that he also memorized the location and look of.

He started heading outside and soon met a servant: »Excuse me, might I bother you for a cup of tea or similar? The way here was rather long and I will need to head back straight away.«

»Of course, please follow me.«

He was lead him into the kitchen, where a couple of slave girls were working. Young things, some barely of age but already with empty and broken looks. They gave him a cup of tea but otherwise kept far away from him. It broke his heart.

Soon enough he fled from there and made his way back to the tavern after scoping out the rest of the property.

Notes:

Oh gods, formatting this took so much timeeeeee. But I am happy with it, that is the most important part.

Anyway, I had way too much fun writing lyrics into the story. Pan has actually multiclassed into a bard, so I can do it with him!

Attentive readers can guess that Lyubystok and Larkspur from the previous chapter are the same character. This chapter happens a few years later, so Pan doesn't recognize them right away.
Fun Fact: Larkspur's names are all name variants of Jaskier / Dandelion from The Witcher. If you want to have a fun time, look up Dandelion's Wiki page, go to the "in other languages" section and translate them back into something you speak. Special shout out to Belarusian and Slovak. You'll thank me later.

Chapter 20: Identity

Summary:

Pan muses about who he is.

Notes:

14 January 2024 - 522 words

Chapter Text

»I’m so many people, I don’t know who I am. I am an adventurer, hired hand, errand boy. I’ll do whatever you ask of me and I am capable of doing so many things. You want a skilled fighter? A scribe? A diplomat? A soldier? A caterer? A thief? You can even ask me to mend things, to do some basic alchemy, some basic tinkering. I’m a mediocre harp player. I’ll blend in wherever and with whatever task if I just have a tiny bit of time to prepare. I have gone by so many names already, sometimes I forgot what my actual name is.«

»What is your actual name?«

»It’s not Pan.«

»What is your name?«

»Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd. That’s my name.«

»That’s the name of a noble.«

»Yes.«

»Are you a noble?«

»Yeah. One day I will take over the duchy, continue the line and try my best to be a good leader.«

»Is that what you want to do?«

»I don’t know. I don’t really know what I want for myself in the long run. I know taking over the duchy is my duty. But I don’t know if I want it. I just follow what other people – or deities – have planned for me. And if I don’t need to take any immediate action for them, I just do whatever feels right in that moment. But I do that without an actual goal in mind. It’s all just short term satisfaction, nothing that will help me in the future, at least not intentionally. Who would I be if suddenly the duchy would disappear? If I had no more connections? If there is nothing in my future?«

»Who would you be?«

»I don’t know. I travel hoping to find a calling for myself. A place where I feel at home at, where I belong. A place or a person. I have such beautiful examples of true love around me that I keep searching for it. I want a connection like my parents have with each other, like Elas and Esbeth have. But I don’t know if I am capable of that. Their love is like a ingle: steady and warm, but not hot, reliable, endless with just a bit of work. But I only love like an oil fire: intense, searing hot, burning people easily and in the end: short. You can’t really keep that alive for a long time without huge amounts of effort. But maybe I am also afraid of what might happen if I were to love long and soft. Not only would the other person need to reciprocate it but I would drag them into the responsibility of leading a duchy. Not everyone is ready to do that. Best if I would find some noble daughter who is already ›trained and ready‹ for that – ugh, I hate saying that, but you know what I mean – but those people are always so dull and shallow. And I’ve met enough of them. My parents host a ball for finding me a wife every year since I was like 15… I hated every single one of them.«

Chapter 21: Homecoming

Notes:

15 January 2024 - 1020 words
Partial inspiration (I wrote this before the Ithaca Saga came out, so I was working with snippets back then)

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

They struck when the mansion was empty. The duke and duchess were traveling north and their son hadn’t been seen for half a year once again. The bandits managed to mix sleeping powder into the guards’ food and then they had an easy time to kick them out and secure the building. Other servants were taken as hostages and after a couple of days and a couple of tries of the citizens and guards to reclaim the mansion they had finally given up, letting the bandits win – or at least that’s what the bandits believed.

Less than a week had passed since then until he arrived in the city, keeping a low profile and meeting up with the guards in secret as they hatched a plan. The bandits didn’t see or hear of his arrival despite keeping a close eye onto the city at every hour. They had also stopped believing someone would try again without them noticing that they were rallying.

So night fell on the unsuspecting bandits when a person obscured by shadows climbs onto the walls of the mansion. A silent killer as he takes out three lookouts around the walls, who hit the ground dead before they could make a sound.

The attacker jumps off the wall and runs over the lawn like the wind, climbing the guard tower and flinging himself into the top level where another two bandits were posted. He jumps onto the first, slitting his throat and throwing a dagger into the other’s heart in one move. Next he cuts the line to the alarm bell, retrieves his dagger and heads towards the trapdoor. Silently he opens it and slips through.

He checks the rooms, taking out everyone he encounters quickly, efficiently, silently, and deadly. Their souls don’t get to leave towards the afterlife but get sucked into a small urn hanging under the dark, many layered clothing around his belt.

The man heads into the cellar where a few prison cells could be found – they were filled with the servants of the mansion.

Most of them were sleeping but those who weren’t watched in horror and relief, as the bandits keeping watch got picked off by arrows. The figure in black steps in front of the first cell door, kneels before the lock and jams lock picks inside. After a few seconds the lock clicks open.

»Stay silent and keep together. Escape through the guard tower and take the southern passage. Just be careful«, he speaks in a low voice. Two of the awake servants had approached him and bowed: »We are glad you came.«

He just nods sharp and quickly unlocks the other cells. He repeats his instructions to each group. A winter elf looked at him especially worried: »What will you do?«

»Take back what is mine.«

»Be careful, please.«

»You too. Lead the others to safety. The guards are waiting in the south – for you and my signal.«

The elf nods and the man opens a secret passage and heads into darkness again.

 

He moves silently, soon emerging into the main house. He pulls out a pan flute and starts playing an eerie, quiet melody. A torch nearby dies down, then the next, then the next. He moves with the emerging darkness, more and more lights extinguishing on his path. Any intruder he comes across he makes quick work of.

Eventually his presence is being noted. A bandit manages to flee before he can take him down. He gets the other rallied but the man just continues to snuff out lights and lives causing the remaining bandits to panic even more.

»Who is that?«

»Where is he?«, they whisper tense, then another one falls.

They run again. But the man follows, the melody playing even as he shoots.

»Keep your heads down. He’s using the lights to–«, the next falls.

»He’s using the darkness to hide his approaches.«

»We know these halls, we –«

»You don’t think I know my own palace?«, the voice suddenly appears behind them. Moonlight catches on a white and black sword for a moment, before all three get struck down by the same blade.

 

In the end, after a real bloodbath, only one bandit remains and he was their leader. He was running for an escape, but doesn’t get to it. When a dagger burrows itself into his calf, he falls and the haunting melody starts filling his mind.

»You’ll not get away from me«, a dark voice calls and approaches the bandit who was trying to crawl away. But the man grabs a hold of his arm and forces metal shackles around his writs.

»Who- Who are you?«, he bandits asks, his voice shaking.

»I am asking the questions«, the attacker answers, stepping onto the bandits head, forcing his face into the ground: »What were you doing here?«

»You are nobody. You will not get answers from me«, the bandit slurs.

»I wouldn’t be so sure about that.«

Then the man pulls him up, throws him over his shoulder and takes him through the halls.

While he is walking, the man summons fire in his hand and strangely enough tosses the flame out of the window.

 

They enter the throne room, where the bandit gets dropped. Playing further notes on the pan flute, two torches get lit behind the middle throne, that the man approaches. The bandit manages to sit up and watch as the man sits down on the throne and crosses his legs.

»You picked the wrong place to mess with«, the man says. »I appreciate that you do it while my parents are out. But you still will pay for this affront.«

»You’re–«, the bandit stammers.

»Yes. I am«, the young Lord answers darkly and leans forward. »And you are the one responsible for this affront. And the only one left alive.«

The bandit fully pales: »You killed them all? You are said to be merciful!«

»To be merciful one has to be powerful«, the Lord answers getting up again and circles the bandit leader. »Now explain to me, why I should give you mercy.«

Notes:

I freaking love dark Pan. It happens rarely, but gods can he be terrifying when he wants to. Also I love Prestidigitation, it's a beautifully powerful spell when you are creative enough with it.

Chapter 22: Commandment

Summary:

Pan boasts about who he is

Notes:

January 2024 - 205 words

Chapter Text

»And who do you think you are that you can command me?«

The smile that laid itself on the man’s lips was animalistic and dangerous: »Me? I’m a traveler, a swordsman, a diplomat, a solider, a thief, an adventurer.

I’m a man of many names, the ghost in the night, the monkey on the roof.

I have explored the coldest reaches of the north and the jungles of the south.

I have fought beasts and monsters, man and machine. I have swum with Krakens, danced with Queens, slept with Fey, and battled gods.

Foes fell to my bow, my sword, my fists, my music and my words.

I consumed the finest of feasts and the vilest of scraps, the oldest of wines and the hardest of liqueurs.

I have even traveled the planescape and experienced worlds you wouldn’t even dream of.

I am the wielder of Eclipse, a master of the Kensei, a silver-tongued bard, light keeper, peace bringer, charmer of the gods.

All-holy, immortal, first born.

I am the next ruler of Millguard, trusted advisor of the King of Algoria. Eleventh Lord and second of my name.

I am Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd.

And I command you by my birthright, expertise and achievements!«

Chapter 23: Catching up

Summary:

Pan and Arkaniest catch up on what happened.

Notes:

06 February 2024 - 1300 words

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

»He’s over there«, the half-elf points to a workstation.

Pan was indeed there smoothing timber. He had put down his coat and rolled up his sleeves, but his sword was still slung across his back. Hair had fallen into his face as he looked at the plank below him; he was fully focused on his work.

Arkaniest walks over to him: »Hi Pan.«

He looks up surprised when he hears her voice: »Arkaniest! Hi!«, he smiles widely, leaves the tool on the plank and hugs her – tightly. He smelled of sawdust and sweat.

»How are you? How was the trip?«

»Good. Good. It’s good to see you. How are you doing?«, Arkaniest stuttered a bit, her mind grasped by the muscle definition in his arms. Then she focuses on his face and it got better.

He smiles widely: »I’m good too. We’re helping building the new settlement – and waiting for you.«

»We or you?«, Arkaniest asks doubtful.

»We. The others are south, helping to build the houses. I’m just not really useful with that task so I’m here, prepping the wood. And if you’re asking about waiting, then also we. Serina apparently wants a proper mage for something and I said maybe you can help her«, he brushes his hair back as he talks.

»Do you know what she wants?«

»Not really? She did a lot of rambling and my brain switched off at some point. But that’s a problem for later. Let’s go somewhere less busy to talk. How does a drink sound?«

»A drink sounds wonderful«, she smiles and he nods, quickly heading over to his coat. As he walks, he rolls down his sleeves and when he puts on the coat, the golden orb joins him, flying down from the ceiling. Arkaniest watches as he exchanges some words with it, smiling the whole time. Then he seems to whistle and returns to her as he closes his coat.

»Let’s go.«

»Don’t you need to tell anyone you’re leaving?«

He shrugs, but turns around and calls over the whole lumberyard: »EY! Elas! I’m leaving!«

Across the yard, the half-elf from before rolls his eyes but gives a thumbs up. Pan turns back and they start walking: »How was the trip here? Any troubles?«

»Thankfully uneventful. But I gotta tune Irvan up now.«

»That’s fair. It’s a good bit between Graton and here«, Pan says as he pets the metal bear like one would a big dog. They were walking back the same way she came, back to the tavern.

»Tell me about Graton!«, gets her going and she does. She tells him about the city and the archive. They pause when they enter the tavern – Irvan surprisingly was allowed to join them inside – and order drinks, before they head upstairs into Pan’s rented room where they continue talking.

Pan was nothing but attentive, letting her tell her story, sometimes asking questions or putting out theories that often hit the nail right on the head. But as Arkaniest talks, she notices some things on him: Firstly, he somehow was clean now. There were no traces of sawdust on him anymore, neither on his hands, nor face, nor in his hair. He looked like he was freshly washed.

Second, the orb hovered close by his shoulder the whole time, following the conversation and sometimes lighting up in one or the other color, therefor displaying similar reactions to a person.

Thirdly, Pan looked …tired? Older? Exhausted? She wasn’t fully sure. Lines of worry had etched themselves into his forehead and around his eyes and – was that gray hair? When she first noticed it, she thought it was sawdust, but once she realized there was none left that theory went out of the window. She would have worried more about the realization, but she also had the impression, that they were mostly past worries – ones that didn’t wear him down anymore. Only when she speaks about the inventor, his brows furrow again.

»Can I get the letter of research back?«, he asks once she had finished. She pulls the rolled up paper from her bag and hands it over. With sharp eyes he scans over it, then opens a hand, whistles a short melody and a flame appears in his hand. Before she can realize what he had done – and was doing – the letter had already burnt up. Arkaniest was so shocked by both of his actions that she flinches back and almost falls off the chair.

»Oh shit, are you alright?«, he stretches the hand out that had previously held the paper.

»What the FUCK?! Since – What?!«, she stutters, yelling.

»What ›What?‹?«, he chuckles and grins – that damned grin he always had when he knew exactly what he did wrong. It was fun to see when he teased others but GODS was it annoying when one was on the receiving end.

»Two things: since when can you do magic and why did you burn the paper?!«

»Since… Tuesday and because it’s not needed anymore.«

»What do you mean since Tuesday and what do you mean it’s not needed anymore?!«, she shouts at him.

»Well, the research has been made and is done, so the research letter is not needed anymore. And Tuesday means two days ago.«

»Gods, Pan, slow down! What if the Lord wants more information? How can I even get to him to tell him about the research without that letter?! And wouldn’t he be the one that’s supposed to destroy that letter – if anybody?! And what if the research isn’t over and he wants more info??!«

Pan shrugs: »It’s fine. I’m allowed to do that. And the research is over. He agrees it’s too dangerous to dig deeper so he told me to destroy the letter once I get it.«

»But – what about reporting the findings?!«

»I’ll talk to him. But it was mostly for my own research. He just allowed me to go for it.«

»Why – How did you even get so close to the Lord that he gives you free passes like that?!«

Pan smiles conspiratorial: »Oh, you know...«, and wiggles with the eyebrows telling.

»Seriously«, she looks at him dumbfounded.

»I have my charms. And swing both ways. He too… So… Yeahhh…. But don’t tell anyone«, he winks.

Arkaniest covers her face and lets out a long: »Paaaaaaaan.«

»What? That’s that not the worst thing I did. But let’s not go down that path. Serina told me to go easy on you and–«

»You did not go easy on me. Let’s go back to that ›Tuesday‹ thing. What do you meaaaan you know since Tuesday? How?«

»Well, on Tuesday I casted my first spell.«

»HOW?!«

»Like that«, he pulls out a panflute, plays the same melody from before on it and produces another flame in his hand.

»Since when do you know how to play Panflute?«

»Tuesday!«

»Paaaaaaaaaaaan!«

He laughs out loud, honest and clear.

»What the fuck happened Tuesday?«, Arkaniest shouts.

»Quite a lot actually but in the context of me playing Panflute and casting spells: I had music lessons with Lord Oghma.«

»Lord . . .« This is the moment Arkaniest actually falls off the chair.

Laughing Pan gets up and walks to her, offering a hand to get up.

»Anything else that happened that I should know about?«, she asks still laying on the ground, not getting up.

»Well – We went back in time to the draconic realm and fought Tiamat there. And I had a literal face to face meeting with Myrkul.«

Arkaniest blinks and raises a hand in a ›Stop‹ gesture: »Yeah, no. I refuse to believe that. No. None of these things were real. No way.«

And she gets on her feet again without his help.

»Actually, very fair. Have a good day«, he laughs.

Notes:

The things that Pan is telling about are things that actually happened in session (more or less). He's honestly just as chaotic, maybe even more during sessions, when I play him.

Chapter 24: Guard Fight

Summary:

Pan fights some guards as training

Notes:

19 February 2024 - 1406 words
I can't tell you which level Pan was at the time of writing this, but I actually rolled all of this out and tracked it in a spreadsheet. I technically made a mistake with two handed spears and shields, meaning their AC has the shield bonus, but they can attack two handed, but maybe they were just particularly good guards.

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

The young Lord Panagiotis was bored while sitting at home, between the Winter Solstice Festival and his birthday. It was nice being home, sure, and the first few days had been interesting as he always had a lot of catching up to do but he’d been here for a week and a half now and was just shy of hanging himself upside down form chandeliers or doors.

So one morning he went over to the guard tower.

»Who wants to train with me?«, he bursts into the morning muster.

Guard Captain Jonathan shoots him a sour look.

»Come ooon. It will be a good lesson for the guards. Aren’t you the one complaining that they don’t get challenged enough?«

Jonathan sighs a bit: »Alright, you’re on. Who think they can take on, Lord Panagiotis?«

Enough guards volunteer.

»How shall we do this? 2 against 1?«, Jonathan asks.

Panagiotis yawns: »I could do 5 against 1.«

»You’ll eat your words. Five against one it is. Non-lethal, non-maiming.«

»When someone is out, send a new person in. We go until I surrender or go down.«

»Hah! You’re so full of yourself. Alright, you heard the man. Everybody who wants to participate to the training grounds!«

And almost a platoon worth of men went over.

 

Five guards position themselves in a half circle around Panagiotis who smiled and pulled his sword, as they grip their spears.

»Ready?«, Jonathan asks from the sidelines.

»Ready!«, all six fighters call back.

»Set. Go!«, the Captain calls.

Panagiotis shoots forward, bringing his sword down on a guard’s shoulder, then jumps upwards trying to kick him, missing once, hitting the second time. The guard immediately looks beaten. Nobody had expected the ferocity of the attack. But the guards grip their spears and try attacking the young Lord who completed his back flip. Three of the spears miss him, but two find flesh, tearing through his clothing and drawing blood.

But Panagiotis doesn’t flinch. He slams the sword against of the guards who hit him, making the guard go down without a second attack necessary. He pulls his sword back, ramming his elbow into the guts of the first guard who also falls to the ground, while the young Lord kicks at the other guard’s shin, making him stumble, but not yet fall.

The three still standing guards try attacking him, Panagiotis slipping under two spear tips, but the third catches him unprepared and rather badly on the arm. The fallen guards pull themselves off the battlefield and get replace by two more.

There’s no rest for the three original ones though: Panagiotis slices with his sword, but the guard manages to duck away, only for him to get caught by a punch. A second slam misses too and Panagiotis grits his teeth, turning and punching another in the face, knocking him clean out. Four spears go for him, all four ending up in the air or deflected – it probably didn’t help the guards’ concentration that Panagiotis kept passing his sword from one hand into the other like the ambidextrous madman he was.

Said sword, held in both hands in this moment, catches one of the new guards on the leg and with a bleeding wound the guard steps back before having managed to get a single hit in. The young Lord tries punching one of the original guards, but slightly slices his arm open on the spear the guard held. The guard grins at him taunting, but Panagiotis kicks the other original one, knocking him out.

Again, only three guards remain to attack him, as the others get replaced. Only the original one, whose spear had drawn the Lord’s blood twice, manages to hit, stabbing into his shoulder, while the Lord deflects and dodges the other two spear points.

Panagiotis thoroughly had enough of him, and as he turns – attacking Guard #7 who deflects – he finally kicks Number #3’s lights out. He dances back, his sword raised to deflect, against the four guards coming for him. Three of them are no problem, and the fourth isn’t either, even if the Lord needs to deflect this attack with his sword.

All five of the guards now were unhurt – and hadn’t hurt Pan either. He goes for the next best, finding hurt with his sword, but not with the following kick. The Lord simply turns, bringing his foot up against the next, while five spear tips go for his upper body that just wasn’t where they had aimed at anymore.

Panagiotis continues with the third guard, pulling his sword upwards and kicking the attacked guard out of the brawl, only to do the same with the already hurt two from before: an elbow blow for one, a kick for the other – and since he was spurred on by the figurative bloodshed he catches a fourth in the same kick making him stumble so hard his attack misses Panagiotis – but not by much. Only one guard remains to attack and he does find ground hurting the devil incarnate before him.

Naturally, this devil tries to retaliate, but by some trick of fate his slice goes too far down and into his own leg. But it wasn’t enough to slow him down. He slams his body against the guard on his last leg taking him out and punching the one he had just missed – now successfully. The guards come for him again and for a moment it seems like Panagiotis manages to dodge all four again, but then he jumps a bit too far left, enabling one guard to get him good. He gasps from pain and the tip burrows into his side.

On the sideline, Captain Jonathan draws hope as he sees this. The Young Lord had already taken out 10 guards of his – and the captain was eating his words watching the fight.

Panagiotis is still solely focused on the fight, trying to hit the guard who just had hit him, but the guard manages to step aside and the attack goes wide. Almost angry, Panagiotis turns to punch another but the hit goes straight against the guard’s shield causing it to hurt Panagiotis’ hand. The Lord exhales hurt and steps back a bit, ready to defend himself. A good decision he learns quickly, as the same guard almost gets another bad hit in. But now they all swing and stab wide.

The Lord now jumps forward again, using the wide swing to take down the guard who had almost gotten him again with a slice against his hand. He goes to attack another, one, two, three times with punches – but missing all three. At least the guard was so overwhelmed by the barrage that he misses too. But another one of the four hits and Panagiotis knew he would soon be defeated. But not yet.

He swings for the guard, missing, then jumps into the air, kicking and stepping on said guard, who goes down after getting a taste of the Lord’s shoe sole. Panagiotis expends himself further to turn into the air and hit another guard. The guards try attacking while he was still in the air, but it’s no use once again. Panagiotis was moving too fast.

He lands, slices at a guard, who leans out of reach only to get a foot into his stomach causing him to fall backwards. Panagiotis ducks and slams his elbow into another stomach, making this guard follow the other’s example. The other three go for him, and one of them finds ground again, bringing the already hurt Lord onto his last leg.

As the two fallen get replaced by new guards, Panagiotis steps back and calls out loudly: »Enough! I surrender!«, raising his hands – sword in one – above his head. The guards lower their weapons and Panagiotis puts his away too. He was panting hard and looks over to the side lines.

He had taken out 15 guards before they could get him to surrender. And who knows how many more he would have gotten if he had been fighting until he got knocked out fully.

Captain Jonathan comes over to him: »You are a skilled fighter, boy. Good job, you proved me wrong.« And he slams onto Panagiotis’ back approvingly – and hard. It rattles the Lord’s ribs and chest, his eyes roll back into his head and he passes out, Jonathan moving quickly to catch him before he can hit the ground.

Notes:

Yes, Pan did roll 2 nat 1s and one of the guards did have a nat 20. Since we're playing with fumble and crit rules in our game but I don't have the tables of my DM I just opted for a bit more damage instead. The whole exchange lasted 12 rounds.
In the end Pan had 1 HP and when Jonathan slapped his back he collapsed. Insert here the moment at 1:07 in the "Warrior of the Mind" animatic from WolfyTheWitch.

Chapter 25: The Assassin

Summary:

An assassin tries to go after Lord Panagiotis Jazrueltehd of Millguard.

Notes:

20 February 2024 - 899 words

Chapter Text

Carl was trying to fulfill his contract for more than a week now. He was an assassin and had agreed to kill a Lord of Algoria for quite a big sum of money. Just that it turned out a lot harder than expected.

He had tired poisoning the Lord with continuously stronger poisons but either he had the best poison testers in the world, never ate anything or there was magic at play.

He had also tried shooting him with a bow and arrow a couple of times, but despite that he normally had a very good aim, he never hit the Lord. Furthermore, he had tried jumping him with a band of mercenaries, trying to stab him while the Lord was busy fighting with the mercenaries. But Carl was still trying to understand what had happened that time. With four quick punches he had either taken out or stunned all four attackers before anyone else could move.

 

Now Carl was sneaking into the mansion of the Lord under the cover of the night and was surprised at how easy it was and how little guard were posted, even if the Lord knew someone would be coming after him. This had to be a trap, right? Carl kept expecting an alarm would suddenly sound as he unlocked windows and doors, but he entered the bedroom undetected as far as he knew. And there was the Lord, sleeping peacefully just an arm length away from him.

Why was this so easy? Something must be wrong…

But Carl drew his dagger, stepped up and reached under the canopy of the bed. Still nothing happened.

Carl slit the Lord’s throat, red blood spilling out in a powerful wave, a gurgling sound emerging as the Lord took his final breath and –

A white skull flashed above the Lord’s head, the gurgling stopped and gave way to an oppressive silence.

Done.

Suddenly the Lord opened his eyes and looks at Carl who thinks he’s hallucinating.

»Run«, a deep voice emerged from the previously slit throat.

He couldn’t be alive! But obviously Carl’s job wasn’t done yet. He rushed forward and jammed the dagger into the Lord’s throat again, causing the body to fall limp.

Carl stumbled back terrified at what just happened.

The skull was a bit clearer this time and suddenly the body launched itself at Carl, making both fall to the ground together.

»You can’t kill me«, the Lord rumbled. Carl rammed the dagger into his chest. The body fell onto him lifeless once again. Carl managed to roll it off him, despite the fact that he was shaking violently. He had never seen anything like that and he was scared of the powers at play here.

For a third time, the skull appeared and it seemed to be smiling, laughing even.

The Lord picked himself up: »Run«, it sounded even darker than the first time and he reached for something beside his bed. Carl rolled onto his feet, dashing for the windows, breaking through them and launching himself into the garden. He RAN.

Until something jumped into his back, causing him to fall and land into the night-dew-wet grass face first. A white and black sword blade was pushed into the ground beside his face, one side reflecting Carl’s own bone white face. A punch on his spine paralyzed him even if he wouldn’t have dared to struggle anyway.

»Finally you show your face«, the same terrifying voice rumbled, a strange melody in his speech pattern. »Your futile attempts have gotten really annoying, I tell you. But you’re good. Any other person would have perished.«

Carl was too scared to do anything, let alone move or say anything. The Lord sat on his back with his full weight, the blade too close to his face and neck.

»You’ll be lucky if I let you live. You’re lucking that I haven’t already killed you. You will beg for your life soon enough. And until then I hope you won’t sully my cells with your excrements. You already made a mess in my rooms after all.«

Carl was shaking. The Lord words were true, he did make a mess there. But the being sitting on his back had to be a god in disguise. And he was horrifyingly calm considering someone had just attempted to take his life.

 

The sounds of guards hurrying over to them started to fill the air: »Lord Panagiotis! Are you alright?!«

»Fit as a fiddle. And I have our pesky assassin right here!«, he called back, his tone having shifted dramatically, being almost cheerful.

Soon after guard grab hold of Carl and the Lord got off. When Carl got pulled up, he gets a look at the Lord in the light of the torch, one of the guards had brought: His shirt was covered in a deluge of blood, a kind-of-closed wound on his throat. He looked like a death god. The sword seemed gone, but the Lord was holding onto something invisible with one hand. His gaze darkened as he looked at Carl, before he stepped over and pulled the mask off Carl’s face.

»I don’t know if you pray to a god, but if not, I’d advise you to pick a god, and pray to them. I’ll deal with you later.«

And with those words, the guards took him away. Carl wasn’t resisting.

Chapter 26: Retesodonth

Summary:

Pan visits the head of a group of half-dragons he helped settle in Millguard and reveals his identity to him.

Notes:

09 January 2024 - 1261 words

I wrote this chapter in my phone's notes app, which is why you have regular Quotation marks, instead of my typical ones.

Chapter Text

It's a couple of months later, middle of winter, the snow lays half a meter high. It's a few days before winter solstice. Someone wearing a big fur coat, buttoned up fully makes his way through the makeshift half-dragon settlement south of Millguard (city). The few people on the street might recognize him by the coat as one of the adventurers that helped get them settled here.

He makes his way to the house of Retesodonth and knocks.

"Ah, Pan. You're ..back? Come in, nobody should be in that cold outside", the half dragon let's him in.

They smalltalk for a bit. Eventually Pan says: "I have to apologize to you. Back in the day I haven't properly introduced myself. But otherwise things might have gotten a bit too complicated."

Reth looks a bit confused and Pan unbuttons the coat open from inside. It falls open and the young man wears the fine clothing of a noble man. A golden orb flies out as well. The sword by his side had a carved handle. A golden signet ring adorned his thumb. He bows lightly in front of Reth and speaks without jest: "Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd, my pleasure."

The half dragon stands there mouth agape. After a few moments Panagiotis chuckles, having straightened after his introduction.

"You probably also recognize me from Evonia ...where we fought ...because you were stealing mounts, my Ruffus included…"

"That dog hates me", Reth mutters still in stunned disbelief.

"For good reason I would say. But there are no hard feelings between us, I want to say", he still chuckles.

"Are you sure?"

Panagiotis shrugs: "From my side at least. Otherwise I wouldn't have helped you get settled in this land that I will take over one day. You were people in need of help and I am one of the last people that will refuse to give any."

The half-dragon still looks stunned and overwhelmed. After a few moments he speaks: "I am so so sorry for what I did when we first met. We are endlessly grateful for the kindness you, the Duke and dutchess have given you. I don't know how we deserve it, especially —"

Panagiotis waves a hand: "It was very much self serving. Getting you away from doing that ritual and instead getting a group of citizens grateful and in a way in debt to you? All strategy", Panagiotis speaks as if he was joking but Reth hears the truth in his words.

"Anyway, I came here to invite you and your people to the upcoming celebrations of the winter solstice. We celebrate the beginning of a new year, the returning of sunny days, and try to be blessed by the gods. There will be a big feast on the main plaza and in the noble’s mansion that everyone can attend after sundown and after the blessing light got received."

"Are you sure this is a good idea?", Reth asks.

"It's a celebration of new beginnings", the young lord smiles: "Following the example the snow makes, everyone is equal during the celebration. It doesn't matter who you are, where or how you were born. All of us have the right to start anew like the year. The people of Millguard would love to welcome your people and wish for a bright future by sharing the blessing with you. It is up to you if you want to recieve it. It's an offer", the last words were spoken soft.

"How does one receive the blessing?", Reth asks after a couple of moments of consideration.

Panagiotis smiles again: "Before sundown a representative of a group — a family, a circle of friends, a town — goes to the shrine circle with a candle. A cleric lights their candle on the sacred flame. Then the person can pray to the gods for whatever their group wishes for during the next year.

»It's the representative's task to keep the flame light through the whole night, from sundown to sunup. You can have a special candle able to burn through the whole night or just light a new candle on the old once it starts to run out, the only thing important is that the light doesn't die. And the representative needs to stay awake with the light through the whole night, otherwise the blessing won't take effect — or so it is said at least. Once the sun goes down there will be a feast for everyone, but you can also eat in your own circle, if you like. There is music and laughter until late at night, people connect and during that time – like I said – everyone is equal. At some point most of the families will head to sleep but the light keepers stay awake, keep each other company a lot of time, but not always. And they brace the longest and darkest of nights until the sun rises. The families often celebrate their success by a rich breakfast, then the light keepers are allowed to fall into bed with the knowledge they succeeded to bring the sacrifice the gods had asked for and the hope that the gods will fulfill the wishes."

Reth had listened intently: "It sounds like a nice tradition."

Panagiotis smiles: "It is. I will be the lightkeeper for my family and the whole of Millguard which includes you. So it doesn't need to be the head of family, even if it often is. If you want, you can represent the half dragons but it can also be anyone else. But again: there is no need to participate, it's an offer."

"I understand. I think we will be there. I will talk with my people."

"Great!", Panagiotis smiles wide. "I hope you will enjoy the festivities. And I hope you all can make some connections and friendships within the city. And furthermore, there might be smaller celebrations four days after. They aren't important for you, it's nothing you can or need or whatever participate, it's just that you're informed."

"What is getting celebrated?", Reth asks.

"My birthday", Panagiotis grimaces like the celebration were embarrassing to him.

This stuns the large half dragon again for a moment, then he says decisive and loud: "A day worthy of celebration!", and he laughs out. He other just keeps grimacing.

"Well then... that's all I came by to say. Anything you want to say before I get going?"

"Just again, how thankful I am for the help you have given us. Knowing ...that", he gestures at Pan's clothing, "I realize that I need to be even more thankful than I already was. Your city and people take good care of us and we have everything we need for now."

"That's good to hear. I sincerely hope you all integrate well and can become a real part of this community, in spirit as well."

Reth nods and Panagiotis stands and buttons up his coat again.

He stops at the door: "By the way, it's a half open secret in this town what my second life is. I'd appreciate it, if you guys keep it that way. For other, new people, let the adventurer who helped you get settled and the young lord be different guys. In a way they are", he winks at that last remark.

"I understand. I will tell the others too.", Reth says serious.

"Thanks. And see you around then. Take care!", Panagiotis arm appears through a slit on the side of the coat to open the door. Once he had closed the door, Retesodonth sits down again, feeling stunned and lost.

Chapter 27: Installment

Summary:

Pan gets installed as the new Duke of Millguard

Notes:

17 March 2024 - 8.110 words

Song Lyrics from the song »The Good Witch Sleep and the Old Man Grace« from »The Amazing Devil«, starting at minute ~5:22

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

The day Panagiotis was invested as Duke of Millguard was a happy day for many but him. The city hosted many high ranking noble guests from across the land and even the King of Algoria. It was a great honor that the king himself would hold the ceremony but Panagiotis wasn't rejoicing over that fact – on the contrary: Panagiotis had hoped the king wouldn’t come. In the end it didn’t matter if the metaphorical shackles were placed on him by the King personally or one of his proxies as he would still bid his freedom goodbye once and for all either way.

This caused the young Lord to go to bed with a heavy heart the evening prior. He slept badly, waking early with a feeling he was about to get hanged. Years of travel, of adventuring, of freedom came to an end and from there on out he would only travel for official functions.

He sat down to meditate, hoping to find some peace inside of himself, but the time passed and he didn’t. Eventually a knock on the door caused him to open his eyes.

»Good morning, young Lord. I bring you your breakfast«, his valet, Nathaniel, spoke as he entered. »You’re awake already, did you not sleep well?«

Panagiotis looked at him with a gaze like someone had died. Nathaniel just nodded, knowing how the other felt about the event to come.

»While you eat, a bath is being prepared for you«, he continued, »The ceremony starts at 11 o’clock.«

Like Panagiotis had forgotten. He had to rehearse the whole ceremony many times so that everything would go perfect on the day itself.

He didn’t say anything and instead sat down to eat, even if he wasn’t hungry. Not even the extra sour Sour Prince Candy could cheer him up – he actually didn’t touch it.

 

Once he was done Nathaniel and more two servants made sure Panagiotis looked as perfect as possible, bathing him, giving him a manicure and pedicure, and clothing him in outlandish robes fit for a king and that made Panagiotis feel both like a child playing dress‑up and a puffed up peacock – he hated it. And he hated how the servants insisted on doing everything for him on this day, as if he couldn’t care for himself.

Only when he put on his longsword he felt some comfort. It was stuck in an ornamental sheath and hung as a comforting weight on his belt. He also put on the silver necklace with a small pan flute on the end.
