Nathaniel watched critical as he put it on, covering it with the clothes as the necklace wasn’t supposed to be part of the outfit, but he didn’t say anything.

Panagiotis was about to become a Duke, but he was still a Kensei monk and a Bard blessed by the Patron of all Bards – and none of the servants would deny him this connection.

He adjusted the outfit one last time in the mirror and barely recognized himself. Even less when he put on the smile, that calm and content expression he had practiced so long. He didn’t feel like smiling, but he smiled anyway.

»It is time«, Nathaniel finally said. Panagiotis nodded and turned to leave the room. His step was confident and determined, like of a man on a mission. Not even Nathaniel could tell that Panagiotis’ heart was sinking into depths he didn’t know he had in him, but Nathaniel still knew that it was.

Panagiotis was on the way to Pan’s execution.

 

Minutes before the clock struck 11, the two stood in front of the throne room. From inside they could hear the damped mumbles of the people.

»Are you ready?«, Nathaniel asked slightly worried. Panagiotis nodded despite the stone in his throat that was chocking him.

The guards at the door checked their watches and with the first strike of the bell they opened the doors. The crowd inside fell silent and Panagiotis strode in.

Today, there was only one throne on the raised platform. Before it stood the current Duke and Duchess, Panagiotis’ parents, hand in hand, the duchess wearing the circlet as she had been the heir to the seat before Panagiotis. In front of them stood the King of Algoria.

Panagiotis walked up to him and knelt with one knee on the floor: »My king.«

»Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd, second of the name, born December 25 5459 after the draconic calendar, to Duchess Salixia Jadari Jazrueltehd and Duke Taraxacum‑Isteris Jazrueltehd, are you willing and able to take the oath of servitude over the lands and people of Millguard today, in front of this gathered crowd and the eyes of the gods?«, the king addressed him.

»I am«, he answered.

»Then rise and place your hand on the crest.«

Panagiotis rose. One of the king’s retainers held up the golden shield carved with the crest of Millguard. Panagiotis placed his hand on it.

The king spoke again: »Will you Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd swear to serve and protect the lands and people of Millguard not as if you rule them but they rule you?«

»I will.«

»Will you reign merciful and do everything in your power to bring prosperity and peace to the people of Millguard and Algoria as a whole?«

»I will.«

»Do you swear to make decisions based on logic, serving the greater good instead of your own gain when ruling Millguard?«

»I swear.«

»Do you swear to follow and uphold the laws and instructions given by the crown of Algoria and it’s representative in good faith, without breaking your prior oaths?«

»I do.«

»And do you swear to honor the gods and old traditions passed down from our ancestors?«

»I do.«

The king nodded and Panagiotis took his hand off the crest.

Then the king turned to the current Duke and Duchess: »Lady Salixia Jadari Jazrueltehd, do you judge your son, the man standing in front of us today, willing and able to uphold the vows, he just made?«

»I believe he will uphold his vows until the day he steps down from this position or takes his last breath«, she answered, smiling at her son.

»Lord Taraxacum-Isteris Jazrueltehd, do you believe your son, the man standing in front of us today, willing and able to uphold the vows, he just made?«

»I do with all my heart.«

»Are you two willing to step down and leave your reign to be taken over by Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd from this day forward?«

»We are«, they answered in unison. Then Salixia took off the circled and handed it to the king, who turned around back to Panagiotis. Panagiotis knelt again.

»With this, I, Mitnal Simonkari, King of Algoria, name you, Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd, the new Duke of Millguard so that you might rule this land as an extension of me and bring prosperity to your people.«

He placed the circlet on Panagiotis lowered head. Panagiotis had his jaw set. He felt like he was being choked, with the circlet being shackles instead of a crown. Over was his freedom. From now he wore the burden of leadership.

The king stepped aside and so did his parents, literally freeing the way to the throne. Panagiotis pushed himself up, walked towards it and then sat down.

»Long live Lord Jazrueltehd!«, the crowd started cheering as Panagiotis surveyed the people gathered: high ranking nobles from across the whole land cheered for him as well as important people from the region, friends and family. Lord Ramensos Lanzalot Junior of Kastanyard and his family were there, Junior nodding to him respectful. Retesodonth, the half-dragon leader was grinning meaningful. Xieni Tenspirit and her wife were cheering. And was that Myrkul in the back, the white skull hidden under a lavish hood?

The kings retainer reached Panagiotis the documents to sign and he quickly penned down his most extravagant signatures.

»The gods shall give you your blessings now«, Lady Salixia then spoke. Panagiotis got up and started heading out of hall, the others following him out of the mansion. Bells were ringing cheerful as he came out.

Ruffus, equipped with a fancy saddle and reigns barked excited as he saw Panagiotis who jumped onto his back. In other places the new Duke would have mounted a horse but Panagiotis was glad he could hop onto his furry friend. The two lead the procession that now started to make it’s way through the city. The citizens cheered for their new duke who smiled and waved, trying to be carried by their happiness. He didn’t allow himself to think of his own feelings in that moment.

 

They soon arrived at the shrine yard where Panagiotis jumped off Ruffus’ back and entered into the yard. He was followed by his parents and the king. Clerics of the different gods stood in front of their respective shrines, ready to give blessings to the new Duke. He passed down the circle and each Cleric spoke a prayer for him, his reign and Millguard’s lands and people.

Some were just prayers, others were more ritualistic, with him getting anointed. He knelt a lot, played honored and happy but he was all but happy.

Only one thing didn’t go as expected: he went to kneel in front of Oghma’s shrine to receive the blessing by the old Alius, but a gnome stood before him and told him: »Rise, my child.«

Panagiotis rose, looking at the ›Priest‹ and smiled – honestly smiled.

»Blessed shall you be by the Lord of Knowledge, the Binder of What is Known, Patron of Bards, our Lord Oghma, so that all the decisions you shall make be guided by him and his Wisdom«, the gnome spoke, reaching out. He touched Panagiotis’ chest, tapping him right onto the pan flute pendant hiding under his clothes. The pendant started feeling warm.

»Thank you, my Lord. I shall honor you always«, Panagiotis answered with a low voice, smiling softly. The gnome grinned back and signed Panagiotis to walk on. He did.

 

Once he had gotten all the blessings, he mounted up again and the procession continued through the city. He spotted many familiar faces: Moe and Brush, Brassy and Elas. But he also thought he saw ghosts of the past: Arledge, Arkaniest, Madame Kambera, even Equadorius – but he wasn’t sure he could trust those apparitions; there were too many faces.

 

The clock had already struck 1 PM when they arrived back at the mansion. Inside the ballroom a huge buffet had been set up. But before they could eat, Panagiotis had to hold his speech. He took a sip of the wine provided by him by Nathaniel and looked through the faces around him who were all waiting for him to speak.

»Friends«, he started loudly: »I thank you all for joining me on this momentous day. I am honored to have received the trust you all put in me today. I shall not disappoint it. I shall follow the example provided to me by my parents, their parents and all that came before them and make sure Millguard continues to prosper and thrive.

»I pledge myself to peace but will defend this realm should it be threatened. Long live Millguard. And long love Algoria!«

»Long live Panagiotis!«, it echoed from the gathered people.

»And now: let’s celebrate«, he smiled and gestured towards the food. People clapped and broke off to get some food. The formalities and rituals were over – mostly.

The now Old Duchess was the first to appear next to him, squeezing his arm: »I am so proud of you, son. You did great! You will do great.«

»Thank you«, he smiled at her – it was a farce as almost all the other smiles have been too.

»You’re never getting out of this«, his father joked as he came over too.

»I know«, he answered with a bit of a sigh in his voice. His parents laughed, like he had made a joke, but he hadn’t. He was still smiling as the King made his way over.

»Congratulations, Panagiotis. How are you feeling?«

»Great. Very honored, especially for your presence.«

»You show great potential, always have. I am sure you will be a great asset to the round of Lords. But your parents will be missed too, of course.«

»Thank you. I will do my best.«

The King patted his back and then too left for the buffet. As did Panagiotis’ parents.

Next came Junior: »I didn’t think I would experience this day. Thought you’d weasel your way out of this responsibility somehow.«

»That was never part of the deal. I always knew this day would come«, Panagiotis grimaced lightly.

»You never told anyone of the deal you made when you disappeared. And with so much time gone, people stared thinking you wouldn’t return.«

»That is fair, but you won’t get rid of me that easily«, Panagiotis gave him a little bump on the shoulder and Junior rolled his eyes.

»Now you only need to decide on a wife and produce an heir and you can step down again.«

»I repeat: you won’t get rid of me that easily. If I do something, I do it properly.«

»That is true. So: will you celebrate too?«

Panagiotis chuckled: »First I need to get through all those congratulations. So if you would move along...«

And Junior did. Immediately the next person came along, congratulating him and chatting him up for a bit. This continued for probably another hour and Panagiotis hated pretty much every second of it. For him it felt like everyone was congratulating him on a loss. At least Nathaniel kept his wine glass filled throughout.

 

Finally he was free for a moment to head over to the buffet. The chats were turning to other things, people were splitting off, going into the gardens, other areas or home. Panagiotis would have too, if he were allowed – but he wasn’t supposed to so he didn’t.

As he loaded his plate, he noticed his mother quietly arguing with Cleric Alius, the Cleric of Oghma, who should have given Panagiotis his blessing.

With his plate, he went over: »Is there a problem?«

»Not really a problem, no«, Salixia answered quickly.

»Lord, I am unforgivable – I was supposed to give you your blessings today. I have no explanation–«

Panagiotis smiled warmly and rests his free hand on the cleric’s shoulder: »It is alright. You did nothing wrong. I have received the blessings even in your absence – and that is the important part.«

»No, it isn’t«, Salixia disagreed: »Who was the gnome? What had kept Alius? That anyone could have taken over such a task in such an important moment is a serious security issue. Imagine what could have happened!«

»I don’t think anyone could have prevented this. And its alright, really. After all, nothing has happened«, Panagiotis tried to calm them.

»Either way, I can only apologize for my absence«, Alius spoke again, bowing deeply.

»You are forgiven. Now enjoy the buffet and the music instead of fretting over the past.«

»As you wish«, the cleric bowed again and left.

Salixia looked critically at her son: »Do you know who the gnome was?«

»I do«, he smiled conspiratorial.

»Who was it?«

Panagiotis bowed his head next to her ear and whispered: »Oghma.«

Salixia’s eyes widened and the glass slipped from her hand, but Panagiotis caught it before a single drop was spilled.

»You surely are joking«, she answered husky.

»Not at all, mother.«

She stared at him in disbelief. Panagiotis passed her glass back to her, then reached under his shirt, pulling out the necklace: »I have been blessed by him before and honor him everyday. I am happy he still places his trust in me.«

»Gods, Panagiotis«, she muttered and he hid the necklace again.

»At least you’re actually smiling now«, she noted. He noticed, that he was. The flute felt warm on his chest again.

»You have given me the right name«, he told her.

»Well, we will see about the second part«, she joked, »Now go back to your celebration. And don’t forget about the dragons.«

»Never. That is one of the few things I am looking forward to today.«

She rolled her eyes and pushed him back towards the middle of the room. He joined a new conversation.

People asked him for his plans now that he was in power but he had no answer – his parents had done a great job and he had had his hands in their decisions for a while already. Some of the Ladies were flirting with him hard as he was one of the most eligible bachelors in Algoria now, but he couldn’t have been less interested on this day. Many nobles also tried getting on his good side if they weren’t already. There was a lot of pea-cocking happening.

 

 

 

Hours passed until Panagiotis could finally get out of there as he had to go to the next event. He made his way out of the mansion and once again jumped onto Ruffus, riding north towards the dragon lake. This time he was alone and he was glad about it. For a moment he could relax and breathe. He was draped across Ruffus’ neck as the dog was carrying him to the lake.

This day had dragged itself on forever and he was so tired. He had to fight the urge to just take Ruffus and ride away to never be seen again. But begrudgingly he accepted the fate that his freedom was gone now. The circlet on his head was his golden shackle.

 

When they approached the clearing at the lake Panagiotis sat up again and gathered his composure. Eventually he stopped Ruffus and walked the last few meters into the middle of the clearing. The lake lay quiet and empty, not a single dragon visible.

He called out: »Dragons! I am here to make myself known! I am Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd, second of my name, new Duke of Millguard!«

As he called into the night a noise like strong wind through leaves rose up. Five fully grown dragons appear in the sky, circling the clearing, then landing around Panagiotis.

First up an ancient forest dragon that had more resemblance to a tree than some actual trees. Second a gray and black, feathered storm dragon with lightning crackling between its feathers. Thirdly a bright pink dragon, dangerous despite its appearance as they could make your ears scream with a screech. Fourth a giant ice dragon, white and blue, whose proximity alone made Panagiotis start freezing. And lastly a celestial dragon looking like the night sky had became a solid form.

»You are already known, young one«, the tree dragon spoke like leaves rustling.

»You were the one bringing peace back to this lake«, the ice dragon continued with a voice crackling like frozen clothes.

»The one defending our kind and our young when we were suffering«, the pink one said, bubbles forming as it spoke.

»The one running with the wind until you met their master«, the storm crackled thunderous.

»Friend of my children«, the celestial dragon finished anticlimactic.

»Zovranth, it’s an honor to meet you again«, Panagiotis bowed before the celestial dragon who lowered their head in a similar gesture.

»You haven’t just met him in days past. You have grown a lost since our encounter«, the tree dragon noted, »A mere wyrmling were you. Do you remember?«

Panagiotis thought for a moment then asked: »Were you the dragon I mistook as a tree and climbed?«

The dragon nodded: »Jalbuss is my name.«

»It’s an honor to properly meet you, Jalbuss. I can only apologize for my younger self’s behavior.«

»You have nothing to apologize for, young one«, he returned.

»So you are the new ruler of this piece of land. They change so fast«, the ice dragon spoke.

»From this day forward, I am.«

»Will you honor this land and swear to protect it?«, the ice dragon asked.

»I do.«

»Will you make sure the dragons here can live in peace and safety?«, the pink dragon continued.

»I will.«

»And will you honor our old traditions and dragon kind overall?«, the storm dragon went on.

»I will.«

»And will you and your people live with nature in harmony, not take too much and returning what you have taken when you can?«, the tree dragon asked.

»I will try my best to do all that.«

»Then we will accept you as the new ruler and work together with you, should you really need it«, Zovranth finished. All five dragons bowed their heads before Panagiotis and he answered by bowing himself. Then four of the five dragons rose into the sky, circling the lake a couple of times before leaving.

Only Zovranth remained, looking at Panagiotis: »Jalbuss is right: you have grown since last time. Even if that last time isn’t that far gone for us.«

»Still, we humans don’t have that much time before moving on.«

»Indeed. Know that I am still thankful for your help. Treat our cousins in the south of your city right and I will come when you need and call onto the stars for me.«

»Thank you. I will work hard to not disappoint you or anyone.«

»Don’t try taking the weight of the sky upon your shoulders or the world will loose a good soul.«

»Thank you for the advise.«

»It’s a warning, not advise«, Zovranth corrected Panagiotis. »Farewell now, young one.«

He spread his wings too. Once the massive body of the dragon had risen into the sky, it melted into the starry backdrop and vanished.

 

Panagiotis continued looking up, after him, while smaller dragons came out of their hiding spots, filling the lake and clearing with life again. He still stood there ten minutes later, as Ruffus was playing hideaway with a few dragons and whacked Panagiotis with his wagging tail. That pulled the newly‑minted Duke out of his endless thoughts.

»Ruffus! Let’s go home«, he called and Ruffus jumped next to him, offering him his back to ride.

Panagiotis smiled: »No. Let’s see who’s faster.«

And then he started running. Ruffus barked and barreled after him, back the way they came. The clothes Panagiotis wore weren’t meant for running or any stunts, being quite restrictive, but he cared little for that while he dashed down the path. He was actually faster than his mount.

When he reached the edge of the city, he stopped and whistled a short melody, making any signs of his run disappear with magic.

»Thought I catch you here«, a familiar voice said and stepped onto the path.

Panagiotis summoned fire in his palm and recognized the person in the dim light: »Elas!«

»My Lord«, Elas bowed. Panagiotis walked up to him and whacked him lightly: »Stop that.«

Elas laughed and hugged him instead: »If not today, then when? You look great. Noble.«

»One thing I did right then«, Panagiotis muttered as Ruffus barreled to a stop next to them.

»How are you doing, Tis?«

»I’m holding up.«

»That is probably all one could ask from you«, Elas answered with a slow nod.

»Probably. What are you doing here?«

»I wanted to check on my friend who I knew had a hard day. And this was the only hope I had of catching you. No way anyone lets me in that fancy mansion of yours today.«

»That is true. It’s good to see you. Thank you.«

Elas smiled: »Can I do anything for you?«

»No. This alone helped me.«

Elas pulled him into another hug: »You know where to find me – and Esbeth – if you need anything. Hold on, yes?«

»Elas, this was basically the first day of school – it’s not like I had to do anything hard today.«

»And yet your shoulders are as tense as ropes on a sailboat«, Elas rested his lower arms on Panagiotis shoulders, his hands clasped behind the other’s head. »I know how much you hate all of this. You probably do better doing the actual work than this. But you’re not alone. Remember that, please?«

»I will«, Panagiotis voice was husky. Elas hugged him again.

After a moment Panagiotis pulled back: »I should get back. I’ve already wasted some time up at the lake.«

»I know, and I won’t keep you any longer. Don’t forget to breathe, my friend.«

»Thank you, Elas«, he jumped onto Ruffus. Elas waved to him and Panagiotis rode back to the mansion, putting on the facade again.

 

 

»There you are. The banquet is ready to start. Did everything go well?«, Nathaniel greeted him at the entrance.

»Yes, it did. I was just savoring the free minutes.«

»I understand. Are you ready to get back?«

»I am.« Panagiotis wasn’t.

Nathaniel fixed some of the clothing on Panagiotis before they head inside.

About half of the guests from the Installment had left already and now only the most important people and closest friends remained.

Panagiotis took his seat at the head of the table, the king to his right, Salixia to his left.

»Did the dragons give you any troubles, my dear?«, his mother asked as they sat down.

»No, not at all. Some had already known me and apparently I made good impressions in the past.«

»That is good then.«

»You have met elder dragons before?«, the King asked interested.

»Yes, two of them. But one had been a long time ago, when I was still little.«

»You are a very lucky person indeed and from the sound of it always had been«, he said impressed.

»Indeed«, Panagiotis nodded.

The first course was served and for a few moments he could eat in silence. But it was a short respite as the soup was gone too fast.

»Lord Panagiotis, if you allow me one more question concerning the past – I know you don’t like speaking about it...«, the king started.

»Of course, my king, go ahead.«

»What were you doing in the years of your absence?«

Panagiotis smiled to himself, thought for a moment then answered: »I have been exploring the world, traveling to here and there. Goldrolitin, Esimarkill, the Feywild, other planes. And I faced beasts and monsters, spirits and gods. I was adventuring, one could say«, he explained but his words were laced with magic and he twisted the mind of the king, sowing doubt.

The king knotted his brows: »You jest, surely.«

»Of course, my king, if you say so.«

The king looked even more uncertain at Panagiotis who just smiled politely. Salixia was staring daggers at her son.

»You can still not talk about it?«, the king continued after a moment, »Not even to me?«

»Not with so many prying ears. Apologies.«

»Of course, I understand. I will ask again another time«, he smiled and Panagiotis bowed his head slightly.

The second course was served and again there was a moment of silence. Only the sharp, judging gaze from his mother remained, telling him ›You cannot treat our king like this! Don’t play with him like that!‹, but Panagiotis didn’t care much. When the conversation did pick up again and turned to other topics, she finally let it drop.

 

The rest of the dinner went by uneventful, eventually they move to the lounge and Panagiotis found himself between two nobles, both trying to convince him to take sides in an argument between them. Panagiotis knew from the start he would keep himself out of it but it was amusing enough to listen to them arguing if nothing else. Both were quite good at insulting each other underhanded and Panagiotis was taking mental notes. Had Junior been here he would have advised him to do the same but Junior was currently chatting with the king.

By now the atmosphere was relaxed and people were sufficiently drunk. After all wine had been pouring since lunch. More and more people were excusing himself to go to bed. Panagiotis might have left too, had he been actually physically tried but he was only emotionally tired. He wasn’t drunk either – he couldn’t get drunk after all. All the alcohol got burned away by the Ki surging through his body.

»Gentlemen, please«, he eventually interrupted the both of them: »If you two would pause the sub-textual insults for a moment and actually think about the situation for a bit, you might realize you could solve this drama quite easily. Although I doubt either of you want to hear that solution.«

Both looked at him dumbfounded.

»Lord Yanus produces the base material for the product Lord Hastor creates. This product is then again needed by Lord Yanus. If both of you stopped thinking short term you would realize working together would be much more profitable than taxing each other with every step. Make the different component tax free and then share the profit from the end product equally«, he explained.

Lord Yanus got a red head: »Working together with this–«

»Lord Yanus, your hatred of Lord Hastor is based on a fallout between your two grandfathers. A feud then carried on by your parents and now you. But neither of you are your parents or your grandparents. The only thing stopping you two to start anew is your misguided ego.«

»You are truly your mother’s son«, Lord Hastor huffed offended.

»I take this as a compliment«, Panagiotis smiled while he nipped on his wine.

Lord Yanus muttered »Insufferable« under his breath.

»Common ground, see? You can used your shared annoyance of me to find more like it«, Panagiotis continued smiling and got up: »If you excuse me now.«

Both nod him good night and as he left, they slide closer together to fill up the empty space Panagiotis was leaving between them. They were probably going to rant about him together and with some luck this would be the start of a blossoming friendship.

 

Panagiotis made his way over to his parents who sat in each other’s laps talking with Xieni and her wife. Xieni’s gaze was as clear as Panagiotis’.

»Would you mind if I joined you?«

»Not at all«, Taraxacum smiled.

»How was the talk with those two wranglers?«, Salixia asked.

»I’ll give them a week to become friends«, he smiled and sat down.

Salixia craned her head lifting lightly off her husband to look over to the two Lords: »How did you do that?«

»Once you listen to them fight for an hour you realize the solution. I told them about it, they both called me insufferable – as my mother – and I advised them to rant about me together. Then I left.«

Xieni laughed out, and so did Salixia. She noted: »They are right, you know?«

»I do. And I would bet you are proud of it too«, Panagiotis gave back. She continued to laugh and nodded.

»Your first act as Duke and you manage to pair up the two endlessly fighting families. And that on the same day of your installment. If you keep up feats like that you’ll become the best ruler Algoria ever had«, Taraxacum smiled proudly.

»Who says I’m not already?«, Panagiotis grinned and nipped his wine again.

Xieni laughed out again: »You’re as insolent as always. One would think you’d learn some humility over the years, especially with your travel.«

»Those years made it worse«, his father noted. Panagiotis kept smiling into his glass.

»Did you never get your behind whooped by an enemy?«, Xieni asked Panagiotis.

»Well, yes, but not enough to balance out the laurels I’ve earned in the same time.«

»Laurels like what?«, she raised an eyebrow.

»Defeating some strong enemies, solving some hard quests, meeting some … legendary people and making some powerful friends«, he answered vague.

»What powerful friends?«

»Gods...«

»Many adventurers have a close connection with a god, that’s not so special.«

»I’m a Bard, Xieni, not a Warlock, Cleric or Paladin.«

Xieni raised an eyebrow, at least a bit intrigued: »What god are we talking about? There’s still a big range.«

»A couple actually, but Oghma for example.«

Xieni’s expression shifted to being impressed: »How close are we speaking?«

»I can summon him.«

»I take it back. I am impressed.«

Panagiotis smiled conceit.

»Well, he topped you, Xieni. The student became the master«, Xieni’s wife chuckled, sleepy.

»You really have to tell me about your travels one day, Panagiotis. But not today. Sassa is about to fall asleep and I’m not gonna carry her to bed again.«

»That is fair«, Panagiotis smiled.

»We should also go«, Salixia told her husband.

»I agree. The day was long enough«, he answered.

Panagiotis nodded and looked around the room. Only a few people remained.

»I’ll follow your example as well«, he decided and all five got up, wished the rest a good night and headed to their rooms.

 

When Panagiotis has parted ways with the others Nathaniel joined him: »Will you require anything else?«

»No, not tonight. You can end it for today. I have kept you long enough.«

»Alright. I wish you a good night, Panagiotis«, he bowed as they reach Panagiotis’ quarters.

»You too. See you tomorrow«, he headed inside.

Panagiotis changed into his night dress and slumped on the bed. The day felt like three. He was tired, emotionally but his mind was running around his head like the wind. Again and again he replayed scenes from the day and colored the future he would have from this day forward: no more fooling around; no more easy jokes; no more adventures with nobody recognizing him. From this day forward he would be trapped in this golden birdcage called leadership and actual responsibility for decades to come.

Initially he had run from this to get away from the stuffy parties and from the nobles thinking they were so much better than everybody else but without an actual skill to their name. Now all of his life would be just that.

He was already done with it and wanted it over.

 

 

 

Panagiotis didn’t find any rest and eventually he gave up on trying to sleep and got up. His body was buzzing with the unused energy his mind set free.

He changed again, into some training pants and a loose shirt, and left the room. Barefoot and silent he made his way through the mansion, down into the training room. At this time of night it was completely dark but with light mumble of »Bring me light«, one of the torches flared up through magic. The silence got laced with the faint crackle of the torch, that had no chance of lighting the whole room.

Panagiotis went to stand in the middle of the room and was very still for a moment. Then he launched himself into a volley of punches, kicks, jumps and somersaults. He was moving fast, so fast his motion blurred. But everything was executed with perfect precision and control.

After a minutes he landed and was very still once again, except for his breath going fast.

He rested like this then did another burst with even more vigor, speed and length.

When he landed again, he was turned around and breathing heavily.

»Thought I would find you here«, he heard a voice and whipped around. In the door stood Junior.

»What are you doing here?«, Panagiotis asked disapproving.

»Following a hunch. Don’t let me stop your exercise though.«

»What do you want?«, Panagiotis’ tone was defensive.

»Do I need to want anything?«

»It’s the middle of the night and you decide to go to a not-so-public part of my home when everyone else is sleeping«, Panagiotis crossed his arms.

Junior shrugged, entered and sat down at the edge of the training area: »I had a feeling you wouldn’t be able to sleep and come here.«

»And what do you care?« They weren’t close enough to show worry for the other – much less act on it.

»I know how unhappy you were today. Will you deny that?«

Panagiotis was silent.

»I decided to be a friend and check on the person I’ve known for so long. Or would you prefer I wouldn’t?«

»No. It’s… Thank you«, Panagiotis answered. By now his breathing had calmed down enough he could do anther set. Junior was watching him with sharp eyes – when did he turn so perceptive? – and gestured to the middle of the floor again: »Don’t let me stop you.«

Panagiotis looked at him critical for a moment but finally he stepped over and performed another set. Silently Junior watched him.

When he stopped, Junior spoke up: »We know each other for so long but I barely know you. I didn’t know you could do all that. I would never imagined you were such a master… I’d ask when and where you have learned to move like this but that I know at least… But how did you do it?«

»What?«

»Go adventuring.«

»Fake names, promises to be careful, enough skill when going in«, he answered and went again.

»Ten years, Panagiotis. And you never bothered to tell me...«, Junior said, when he came to a rest.

»You hated me back then.«

»We both know that’s not true. And you proved that at that masquerade ball.«

Panagiotis chuckled, then looked serious at him again: »Is that why you’re here?«

»No. It was fun back then but today I don’t have any interest in that. I am married«, for a moment he was silent: »I was envious of you, Panagiotis.«

»I know«, the other husked.

»Everything comes so natural to you. So easy. The star child. Do you have any idea how often I had to hear how much better you were doing compared to me?«, Junior started ranting, »I was so glad when I heard you were struggling with magic – it was the one thing I had over you! That and the fact you hated court. That’s it. With everything else you were better.«

»Where are you going with this?«

Junior ignored the question: »And still, you were better in court things than me. I still remember the lesson you gave me about being kind to servants. It was so simple but I only understood it much later. Even then you were so much wiser! And now look at you! Spinning through the air like an actual tornado. Total control over your body! Never loosing your senses no matter how much you drink! And speaking every language if you can trust that rumor?! Most of the ladies would marry you in an instance and the only reason you aren’t married yet is because you’re so damn picky! It’s a miracle you put up with me at all with how perfect you are!« Junior was shouting now and gestured widely at the other. Panagiotis was letting his head hang, ever so slightly turned way from him.

»You’re drunk, you should go to bed«, he mumbled.

»No! I’m not letting you off the hook this time!«, Junior rose and stepped towards Panagiotis, stabbing his finger into the other’s chest: »You have had everything handed to you forever! Even if you say you hate it, I know you will be such a great Duke! You can get everything you want! I am even sure you would find a way to take the stars from the sky if you wanted to hang them in your bedroom! How is that fair? How are you fair?! I would trade places in an instance with you just to get a taste of your perfect life! The king himself installed you! Do you have any idea how many people he has installed personally?! None! Not a single one! Only you! And you were grinding your teeth through that!«

Panagiotis had his fists balled otherwise he wasn’t moving. Every single word hit him and they all hurt.

»You think I’m perfect«, he asked with a low voice.

»I mean: just look at you!«, the other shouted.

Panagiotis looked at him, then mumbled: »Yeah, look at me«, and he pulled his shirt off.

Junior gasped and stumbled back. Panagiotis’ body was covered in scars – small and large; old and new; cuts, claw marks and elemental damage.

»This is what adventuring brought me, Junior. Pain upon pain upon pain. Others less lucky would have been dead many times over. I know Myrkul – intimately. And still – despite everything – this is the day I hated most so far. Not one of the many days I almost died. Not the day we faced literal motherfucking Tiamat, not the day I was this close to turning into a void creature. No: this. And you know why?«, now Panagiotis was becoming louder, stabbing his finger toward Junior in a similar fashion. »And you know why? Because I never wanted any of this!«, he gestured at everything around them. »The one thing I actually want is gone now, for the next few decades. Maybe once my successor takes over I’ll get it back but then I’ll be old and the world won’t be the same. And until then… Until then I don’t have the one thing I actually want.«

»What is that?«, Junior asked, his own anger fully gone.

»Freedom! The ability to do whatever I want without having to think of the consequences! Being whoever I want. Going wherever I want! I went adventuring to experience that freedom ONCE! I never wanted to become a Duke! If I had siblings I would have happily passed the position on to them. But my life was all planned out and unlike you I was never happy or okay with that plan! And I neither have the guts nor the cold heart to deny my fate because I know the consequences and I keep thinking of them, whatever I do. Even when I was the whole way across the continent and nobody would have recognized the name ›Jazrueltehd‹!«

Junior had a faint smile on his lips: »There we have the truth.«

This time Panagiotis ignored him, not hearing the mumbled comment: »You were always jealous of me? You would have hated being me because I hate being me! You know I was always running away! And for good reason! Oh, it’s such a great honor that the king himself was here – guess what: I wish he weren’t! Part of me wishes some monster had killed me so that I wouldn’t need to be here today but they didn’t and I am! So I don’t need your resentment today – or any other day. And if you only came here to make me even more miserable: good job, but be glad for the self control I have because I would love for you to be at the receiving end of my training routine«, seething hatred poured out between the teeth of the new Duke.

Junior stepped back, raising his hands defensively: »Calm down, Panagiotis, I am not your enemy.«

»Are you sure? Because you’re behaving like it!«

»You’re always so composed, never talking about what is going on inside of you, what you think and feel, the only way to get it out of you is to make you angry«, Junior explained.

Panagiotis lunged forward, grabbed Junior’s shirt and flipped him over his shoulder, slamming him into the ground behind him. He put a knee on his chest while Junior was coughing from the sudden impact.

»You are truly annoying«, Panagiotis grumbled.

»I know. Are you ready to talk about your feelings now?«, Junior asked.

Panagiotis let him go and stood up. He grabbed his shirt from the floor, put it on quickly and said: »I’m leaving.«

Quickly Junior came to his feet and grabbed his wrist: »No, you’re not. You don’t need to do this alone. We were always stuck with each other, for better or for worse. Let it be for better tonight – and from this day forward.«

Panagiotis was letting his head hang, silent for a moment, before he mumbled: »Let’s bury this.«

And then he starts singing, in a low grumble at first:

»I’m all yours / but you’re all mine.

Let’s dance together / you and I.

Cause I’m not trapped / with you, you see.

You’re the one who’s / trapped with me.

Cause I’ve been here / so many times before.

Don’t you think I look pretty / curled up on this bathroom floor?«

And as he sang, a silent image appeared on the ground in the middle of the room: the curled up figure of Panagiotis, laying completely still.

»Where you see weakness / I see wit.

Sometimes I fall to pieces / just to see what bits of me don’t fit.«

Shards broke from the figure like a crumbling vase.

»Cause when I stand, oh / All those folk will run«

The figure started getting up, broken still but morphing into something else as it rose unsteady.

»And tell the tales of what I’ve become.«

The figure turned to the adventurer, fur coat, two colored sword, ready to fight the creature forming from the shards.

»They speak of me / oh in whispered tones.

And say my name / like it shakes their bones.«

The creature morphed into a giant bear, then a hydra, a dragon, a worm – shifting endlessly, no set form.

»Cause we dance together / so close we’re sharing breath«

Panagiotis ripped Junior around suddenly, grabbing his waist and hand like one would do for a waltz. Junior’s eyes widen with fear and a bit of amazement as he sees the crazed glint in Panagiotis’ eyes. Panagiotis forced Junior to some clumsy dance moves, never stopping singing:

»But now I’m leading / doesn’t that just scare you to death?

Cause I’m all yours / You’re all mine.

Let’s dance together / You and I.

Cause I’m not trapped / With you, you see?

You’re the one / who’s trapped with me!«

Finally Panagiotis let the other go, pushing him backwards into the illusion that whirled up like dust. Junior lost balance and fell to the ground stunned from the display of emotion and power.

Until this point he hadn’t known that Panagiotis had learned to do magic. And he hadn’t known of all these feeling buried in his childhood friend and rival.

»Panagiotis…«, he husked. Panagiotis was turned away from him, his head lowered again, motionless.

»You… That was… You… All those years ago«, Junior stuttered together a few thoughts and rose. »And you’re a bard...«

»I’m so much more than you think. Than everybody thinks. But I had to bury this person today«, Panagiotis said with a saddened voice. The illusion vanished as he spoke. »I was happy traveling. Despite the scars it gave me. But I can’t be that person anymore. I need to perform my duties now.«

Junior let himself fall against Panagiotis’ back, hugging him silently for a moment: »But you’re not alone in this. You can do all of it alone, yes, but you don’t have to.«

Panagiotis blinked through tears.

»I am you ally not your enemy. I can imagine you find it hard to talk to anyone about who you really are but I’ll be here if you want me to. We’re on the same level – socially at least – but I don’t want any favors from you. And you know me too well for me to be able to hide that from you anyway. Let me be your friend.«

Panagiotis inhaled and Junior could feel how shaky he drew the breath and the tension in his muscles.

Eventually Panagiotis exhaled and softened: »Why today of all days?«

Junior let go and Panagiotis turned around, actually looking at him.

»You once taught me there is a right time for everything. Today was the right time for this.«

Panagiotis chuckled at that: »You’re a bastard, but gods, you’re right. Defeated me with my own weapons.«

Junior smirked for a moment, then his gaze turned soft again. Soft and worried: »How are you feeling?«

Panagiotis nodded: »Better. Thank you. You know I hate to admit it, but you are good. You were right on the money.«

Junior chuckled: »I also grew while you were flying the nest. You’ll see soon enough. But maybe not anymore today. Are you ready to go to bed?«

Panagiotis nodded again: »I think I am. Thank you, truly.«

Junior clasped his shoulder: »Always, old friend«, then he pushed Panagiotis towards the exit.

Panagiotis complied, whistling a bit and the torch died.

»I hate that you can do magic now. The one thing I had over you«, Junior admitted, half laughing as they walk through the dark and silent corridors.

»You’ll hate it even more when I tell you how I’ve gotten the magic«, Panagiotis gave back with a chuckle.

»I am sure I will.«

Notes:

Iiiii love this chapter so much~

Chapter 28: Bathhouse

Summary:

Returning to Wolkrim years later, Pan inevitably runs into an old friend

Notes:

02 April 2024 - 4117

Back then, our group had really gone back to Wolkrim and this one became canon! Pan might have subsequently accidentally stolen Equadorius' name - thanks fey rules.

Chapter Text

I head over to the bath house after agreeing to meeting the party back at the castle gates in 2 ½ hours. We had just arrived in the city and negotiated an audience with the ruler and since I was tense, somewhat dirty from the track and smelled of horse, I decided to freshen up and relax a bit. The bath house had left good memories when I was here the last time: it was a beautiful large building with a couple of grand pools of water. I was pretty sure it was heated by runes and not an actual hot spring, but I couldn’t confirm yet, and it didn’t matter much to me either.

When I head inside, I see a familiar face: a Spring Eladrin, who had been working in the building last time too.

»Good day«, I greet him.

He looks up and smiles widely: »Oh? A returning customer, if I am not mistaken. Welcome, welcome!«

»It’s been a long time, but yes. I would like a bath and a clothing cleaning please.«

»Clean clothes service and a nice bath: coming right up. I do have to tell you, all our private baths are currently occupied.«

»That is alright«, I nod.

»Do you like for all your clothes to be cleaned? The shoe cleaning is on the house if you take the all inclusive deal.«

I shrug: »Yeah, sure.«

He smiles and a key flies from a board behind him into his hand: »This will be for your valuables in the meantime.«

»How much do I owe you?«

»The all inclusive is a gold worth. And the shoes will be on the house. Since you have been here before, you know where to go?«

»As long as you didn’t rebuild in the last four years, yeah I do.« I hand him a gold and an extra silver: »So the shoes don’t actually end up on the house again«, I wink.

He grumbles lightly. »Thank you.«

And I smile and head off to the changing rooms. Inside were a gnome and a tabaxi who both give me short, but pleasant nods when I enter. I nod back before putting all my things, including my weapons in my bag of holding and lock it away in my locker. A towel is provided and when I undress, I wrap it around my waist. The key goes on the necklace with my other key and all the clothes and the shoes go into the chute. Then I head into the bathing area through the showers where I clean myself before going into the big main pool.

I see a few people through the mist of the hot water, four shapes to be exact. The two closest were two people quietly talking about schools of magic. I slip into the water and find myself a spot on the edge of the pool, that was as far away from everyone else as possible, where I hang myself onto the side with my head resting on my crossed arms on the pool wall.

Slowly the hot water started chipping away at the tension in my body and I allow myself some time to relax, to let go. Time where I didn’t have to perform or pretend. I close my eyes and just listen to the soft tickle of the water and the hushed conversation from across the room – I didn’t understand what they were saying anymore.

 

»Sir Minister! I get FOUR HOURS of free time PER MONTH!«, a voice starts yelling a solid five minutes into my relaxation. »And I loose most of that when leaving the Feywild. I am NOT giving any of that to you! If you don’t immediately stop stealing more of my time, you will serve as my footstool for the rest of the month! Is this clear enough for you?!«

As the man was yelling, I open my eyes and peer through the mist towards him. There was a sinking feeling in my gut and any relaxation, that had already happened, vanished in an instant.

Through the mist I can see a pair of magically glowing, furious eyes and a small creature, maybe a halfling Loxodon quickly bowing and then swimming away. As the Fey sighs and the glow fades, I let my arms slip off the edge and sink into the water, so that only my eyes are above the waterline. Slowly and silently I move through the water, bringing distance between him and me. Through that I get closer to the two mages who are talking in even more hushed tones now. I hang myself at the edge and try to relax again, but I had a feeling of being watched – being observed and seen – that I just couldn’t ignore for once. Maybe it was just the two mages having spotted me, looking at the scars on my back, arms and shoulders. Or maybe it was the Kitsune that was sitting outside of the pool close by and staring at me. I raise an eyebrow at it and stare back. It mirrors the motion.

For a few minutes we have a staring contest until the voice from the Fey spoke again, this time in a much softer but still slightly scolding tone: »Ayaka, why are you interrupting someones time to relax? I can feel their tension through the water.«

›I am not tense because of the Fox‹, I think and decide to close my eyes again, considering, if I should be leaving or submerging myself again.

»He smells like home but also foreign lands. A traveler who doesn’t stay in a place for long«, Ayaka answers.

»Ayaka, please come over here. It’s rude to intrude on strangers«, the Fey speaks again.

My mind was basically screaming at me: ›What was he doing here?! Why was he not in one of the private rooms?! Why here at all?! What kind of luck was I having?!‹, but I don’t betray a motion.

The kitsune stands with a light hummed melody. The two mages relax as four tails move above her head, as it starts walking around the pool. The humming also affects me relaxing my body and calming my mind.

»Pardon my companion. She’s still a child and doesn’t always know how to act properly«, the Fey now talks to me.

»It’s alright«, I just answer quickly, without turning. I hoped so badly, he would leave me be and not recognize me here and now.

But Savras was laughing at me. »Hmm. Your voice seems familiar. May I know where I know it from, Sir?«

I shrug in response and bite my tongue: ›Just don’t say anything.‹

»It seems like you have something to hide, not speaking up when someone is asking you something. I will inform you that you are in my home and the fey don’t like being disrespected in their homes. You could even be punished«, I hear him stand up in the water.

I bite my lower lip and turn around as I answer: »I did answer: non-verbally. I don’t know what you may or may not know, Sir, Lord, Prince– I am not here to disrespect you. I am here to take a bath. But if I somehow impede on your ability to do the same, I can leave.«

He was moving towards me, his walk still as elegant as back in the day even if the water was up to his hips right now. He had grown a bit and his hair was now in a bun, longer obviously as back in the day. And as I speak I watch his face melt with a longing that was finally over.

»Ayaka, please bring the other visitors out. Tell them their visit will be reimbursed«, he says offhandedly, not taking his eyes off me and coming over. The two mages get out of the water and head to the changing rooms without any protests.

I hold his gaze steady, my face almost defiantly blank. I don’t say another word.

He stops an arm length away from me, reaches out with a trembling hand but hesitates.

»May I touch you? I only wish to check something...«

I nod silently. At least Serina’s fear of him being angry and starting a hunt for me didn’t turn out true – not yet. Slowly he reaches out, his soft hands touching my shoulder careful like I would break at a touch too strong. His hand slides over to my back as he was searching for something. His touch spread goosebumps over my whole body.

Finally he finds the – now old – scar across my shoulder that he had noticed back in the day already. His face flushes and goes through all the emotions in rapid fire: Worry, happiness, sadness, relief. Tears were forming in his eyes and he only manages to husk out: »Thanis.«

»Equadorius«, I answer, my throat tightening.

He was still moving his hand over my shoulder, back and arm, stroking the scars he came past: »You have new ones«, he notes, lost for words.

»It’s been a few wild years. What– Why–«, I wasn’t sure of my question either.

»I thought… I thought you were gone. Your name circulated but it had no power anymore...«

»I didn’t use it for a while«, I shrug, »What are you doing here?«

»Relaxing«, he smiles crooked, »Most people don’t recognize the prince when he’s obscured by mist and naked. I hate throwing out the people from their private rooms, so I just… mingle… At least as long as people don’t try talking politics during my downtime.«

»That is fair.« I wasn’t moving much myself, going with his strokes, but not actually initiating anything myself. His fingers eventually leave my hand and reach up to my face as if to touch it. Again he hesitated, many emotions flashing through his eyes.

Again I hold his gaze, barely daring to breath. I blink slowly and nod ever so faintly. And he lays his hand gently on my jaw, tracing where the wound had been below my eye so many years ago with his thumb. No trace remained now but he still remembers. His thumb moves down, soon resting by my lips. He was moving closer too, kneeling beside me in the water again, his knees touching my hips.

»You’ve been on many adventures since we met.«

»And you still hold onto that night«, I note.

»All too much«, he whispers, taking up his other hand, cupping my face fully and gently guiding it to his. »You were the best thing to ever happen to me.«

For a moment he could feel the tension in my neck as I hesitate at these words, thinking about pulling back. But in the end I couldn’t: too mighty was the part of me that wanted to kiss him again.

»One of us is a fool. I am just not sure who«, I husk, his face so close to mine, our noses almost touching.

He answers by leaning forward, kissing me with so much love. Like a fine wine the memory of our first meeting had aged and intensified in the years apart and he had held on to it for so long. It was a soft kiss, gentle like he was still scared I could vanish, should he push too hard. Yet, I could basically taste the longing and feel him holding back.

»We’re probably both fools. But I rather be a fool than continue to long for a dream of someone who I thought was dead«, he says when he eventually pulls back.

I smirk: »I don’t die that easily«, and push forward to him, giving him back the kiss, passionate, wild compared to the first kiss, leaning in until only my hand on his chest and a bit of water separated us. His grip was on my shoulder and it got harder, demanding while the other hand was finding my hip to never let go of that.

»Thank the Archfey for that. You are a blessing after all«, he eventually says when I draw a breath.

»You sure I’m not a curse?«, I ask with a chuckle.

»Either way I wouldn’t want to li–«, he pauses mid sentence, then answers: »Maybe you are a curse: coming at a time like this...«

»Why?«

His hands let go of me, he looked around the pool for a second but we were alone.

»In the first year we’re supposed to be monogamous.«

»You’re married?«, I ask with a raised eyebrow, surprised.

He looks away: »It wasn’t my choice. It’s to empower our court. We got hit the hardest three years ago...«, he explains.

»It’s certainly not my place to judge you for getting married, Equadorius. I’m just some random adventurer after all.«

»You are so much more than that to me, Thanis. Or what name do you use now, if I can ask?«

»You can still call me Thanis.«

He smiles: »Still using counterfeit names I see. How intriguing«, he starts reaching out to me, wanting to touch me, to trace my scars, to be close to me, but he stopped himself. He could no longer justify it.

»I’m sorry we meet like this.«

»Me too. I probably shouldn’t have come here. But I didn’t expect you to be here of all places«, I give a crooked, saddened and apologetic smile.

»You couldn’t have know. And I won’t regret meeting you. I missed you, Thanis.«

»I was afraid you resented me.«

»A part of me at first yes, but it became smaller and smaller while the longing and curiosity grew. I wanted to know where you were after all these years.«

»I suppose normally normal people don’t come into town with a fake name, steal a night from the fey prince and leave not to be seen again for years«, I chuckle.

»No, normally that doesn’t happen. Yet it did with you.«

I nod: »And what shall we do now?«

He is silent for a moment. »Go back to our lives and pretend nothing happened here? …Or we could sneak off together. I learn what it is like to be adventurer. And the court can work out their problems on their own.«

I pull a face: »I don’t think things will be so easy.«

He gives a vague smile: »One could always hope. You could also return in a year and a half, when we will open our relationship. But either way, now that I met you I fear I will loath my upcoming husband.«

I give a somewhat disapproving head shake: »No, things aren’t that easy. You should probably ask me why I’m here.«

»I– You’re not just looking for some quests?«, he asks but immediately shakes his head, muttering more to himself: »No, of course not. How could it be that simple with a figure as mysterious as that?« He looks back at me: »Why are you here?«

I give a smirk: »I think you will see once your break is over. Because the breakfast club and I are having an appointment at court in like an hour...«

He smiles intrigued: »Now that changes things. I would have let the substitute handle that adventuring group if I didn’t know it were you.«

»You can still do that if you rather not have anything to do with me. But either way I doubt this will be the last time we meet this week.«

»Interesting. And please, Thanis, even if my wedding is coming up, I will do everything I can do to be close to you. Even if it’s just for talking.«

I chuckle and roll my eyes: »This will end so badly«, then I sigh a bit: »But if you say you’re not married yet...«

»The wedding is planned for when the court joins; in the six days when winter and spring collides«, he explains.

I nod: »That makes sense. But I should probably stop speaking now.«

He shakes his head: »You can talk as much as you want.«

»It’s a really bad idea, Equadorius.«

»It’s only a bad idea if we get caught«, he smirks and gently strokes over my shoulder: »Nothing bad will happen just by me hearing the idea.«

I chuckle again: »I doubt that but...«, I shrug. »If you’re supposed to be monogamous the first year of marriage… And you’re not yet married...«

There was a glint in his eyes and he nods: »Go on. You may say it. There is no one listening.«

»I think you know where I’m going with this«, I take his hand in mine.

He leans in closer and whispers into my ear: »I do but I want to hear you say it, my brave adventurer.«

»Until you marry you can still kiss me without breaking any rules«, I answer and move in for a kiss. He takes the offer and is even more passionate than before. As we were making out, he slides over until he was sitting on my lap. One hand held my face, the other my shoulder. I just sling my arms around his waist and draw him in closer, feeling every part of him, drinking up the kisses. The first went long, then a lot smaller ones happened over the whole face and neck. We were fully making out.

We only break once we’re both out of breath. He grins like the fool he was: »I wanted you for such a long time. And you don’t disappoint.«

»You don’t either, Equadorius«, I grin, »We are both fools.«

He smiles: »Happy and excited fools«, then he kisses me again. We resume making out, my hands gliding over his back, arms and chest, caressing everything softly. His fingers were doing the same to me, dancing over my body even more skillful than back in the day. He manages to fully relax me and at some point I moan: »Gods, you are beautiful«, fully forgetting anything and everything that I should or shouldn’t be doing.

»And you are such a handsome warrior«, he gives back, nibbling on my neck.

»Your word are still as lovely.«

»I had to learn to be elegant – but loving someone like you was something I was destined to do.«

»Someone like me?«, I laugh lightly, »But you don’t really know me, do you?«

He looks up: »I know you in all the important aspects. And the rest? Maybe you will let me know you more this week – when we see each other again?«

»Yeah. Speaking off… We should probably get back to the castle. I have an appointment – and from the sound of it you too. And I should meet the others beforehand...«, I start grinning as an idea sprung up in my head.

He leans back now, smirking too: »How about you don’t meet up with them? And instead come with me through the back entrance of the castle? I’m sure the group will be happy to meet my body double...«

»Or you could come with me to meet the group...«

He smirks: »I could… But I will need some other clothes. All I have are my royal robes.«

»I have a second set...«

He chuckles excited: »So you want me to meet your group before they go into the castle? This will be very intriguing.«

»Just another bad idea of mine. You shouldn’t listen to me.«

»No no. I want to meet them. And you don’t need to worry about one of them stealing me from you. I’m sure they can’t beat you.«

»You will meet them either way: Question is just how they react to you. In one option you will meet them – us – and all is official and proper. And the other is a bit more fun.«

»You should know Fey love trickery and shenanigans since we can’t lie directly.«

»You think this is my first time dealing with the Fey?«, my grin was stretching over my whole face.

He grinned too: »Of course not. You destroyed our economy after all – and you survived the spring festival.«

»Oh, you know that was me?«, I laugh out at his first mention: »Gods. It was an accident, I swear.«

He boops my nose with his: »Your name has a specific taste whenever it is used. I know it since you first gave your name to me. It had the same taste when I took it out of the currency pool. And it tastes the same now.«

»How do I taste?«

»Like spice and earth and a bit of lemon. A very sweet lemon«, he smiles, gently stroking over my shoulders again.

»Interesting. Very interesting«, I give him another longer kiss. »So, should we go annoy my group?«

»I would love to meet your group. But I still need to–«, he cups my face and kisses me again. »Alright, now we can go.«

I grab him tighter and stand, picking him from the water pressed against me as I carry him. He yelps a bit, his face flushes red: »Oh, oh my...«

»Should I let you go or do you like this?«, I grin at him sideways.

»This is all good«, he relaxes against me almost like a child being carried by their parent, »It’s nice.«

I climb out of the water and carry him back to the changing rooms. I was grinning the whole time.

 

The changing room is empty except fro Ayaka. Her tail twitches with questions but she doesn’t speak.

I put Equadorius down on the bench next to her.

»So, where are your extra clothes? Are you sure they will fit?«

»Patience. Give me a second. They are in my bag«, I say as I take off the necklace to get the key. »You can also have the freshly washed clothes and I put on the backup.«

My clothes – and shoes – had appeared in my locker magically, all clean and dry.

»No no, I’ll take the backups. You’ll get them back once we’re back at the castle«, he says, petting Ayaka and watching me grab my bag. It was a messenger bag like couriers would often use but as I pull out the other set of clothing, it doesn’t seem to get any emptier from the outside. I hand Equadorius the clothes and put on my own, freshly washed.

Once he is done, he comes over and leans an elbow on my shoulder: »How do I look? Like a real adventurer?«

»You look like you put on my clothes«, I chuckle and ruffle through his hair, before pulling out my longsword – that shouldn’t have fitted into the bag from the looks of it. But it was a bag of holding after all. I fix the sword on my right side.

»The same sword as four years ago. Is it still treating you well?«, Equadorius asks.

»It is the best there currently is«, I smile, »You have a really good memory.«

»Well, I sure hope so. I do need to remember all the people I have made deals with – or that I have cursed.«

»That is fair, although I guess you have a proper record of that somewhere too.«

»Very much so, but that is not a secret that I can give to any handsome adventurer willy nilly.«

»But I am not just some adventurer, am I?«, I laugh.

»No, you’re not. But even you don’t have full access.«

»For now. Should we go? Will Ayaka join us?«

»Ayaka is bound to me so she will come along. I hope that is okay?«

»Taking her is no problem although you will be more recognizable with her. Do you have your own clothes?«

Equadorius flicks his hand and his own clothes appeare on him – then he waves his hand in the other direction and he wears mine again. »And don’t worry, Ayaka has her own tricks, don’t you, Ayaka?«

»Yes, master. We’re going undercover?«, the fox asks.

»Indeed we are.«

The fox jumps on his shoulder and as she jumps her form shifts from the white furred, four tailed fox into a tabby cat with orange eyes that laid itself across his shoulders.

I laugh: »Great! This will be fun. A warning: the others are all insane.«

Equadorius chuckled quietly: »Says the man who dared to ghost the prince of Wolkrim.«

»Why do you think I hang out with them?«, I grin.

»You really are adorable in the wildest and most adventurous ways there are.«

»And you have fully fallen for me«, I note and pull him to me by the collar, kissing him again. When I let go, I start heading out. A hint of red is in his face and he jumps after me, taking my hand.

We were both grinning like the fools we were.

Chapter 29: Equadorius revisited

Summary:

Millguard gets an unexpected visitor and Pan suddenly has to deal with a past he has long thought to have left behind.

Notes:

08 April 2024 - 6669 words (nice)

The song used here is "Mensch aus Stahl" by "Peter Maffay". Since Pan has the tongue of Sun and Moon, you get the translation as well. I did change a singe line, because in the original he sings "[Watching] TV at night", but since TV doesn't fit into the setting at all, I decided to use "Reading" instead.

Chapter Text

Claiming Lord Panagiotis, eleventh Duke of Millguard, was overworked would have been a massive understatement. He hadn’t eaten anything in three weeks which – to be fair – wasn’t necessary for him. Almost every night his valet Nathaniel would find him fallen asleep over his work. And the only reason he got out of his office was the weekly open day and Ruffus.

»Lord, Junior is asking if you really cannot make it to the meeting tomorrow«, Nathaniel confirms one day.

»I don’t have time«, Panagiotis answers without looking up from the paper he was writing.

»Are you sure you don’t want to take it? It might be beneficial-«, Nathaniel starts.

»I don’t have time«, Panagiotis growls, interrupting him, »I still need to write up answers for...«, he looks over a stack of papers on the desk: »Four Lords, think about three new trade offers, give Estanio another offer and incentive to move here, check the guild reports and potentially react to them, go over the plan for the town extension and probably a bunch of other things I’m currently forgetting. I. Don’t. Have. Time.«

»Yes, Lord«, Nathaniel bows lightly and leaves Panagiotis alone again. Panagiotis hands were moving quickly but still not quick enough for his liking. He did appreciate his talks with Junior, but he really didn’t have any time for any frivolous activities.

 

Hours later Nathaniel knocks again on the office door: »Lord, a messenger just arrived: a delegation of nobles from Askain is on it’s way here. They are scheduled to arrive tomorrow.«

Panagiotis looks up confused and quite annoyed: »Askain? Tomorrow? Who-? What do they want?«

»They are traveling from Wolkrim. The messenger said there should be a letter explaining the situation. He didn’t say more.«

»There was no correspondence from Wolkrim!«, Panagiotis yells out irritated.

»No. That letter must have been lost. I did inform the messenger about it. He is asking if you will still accept the guests.

Panagiotis looks pained and makes a similar sound as he is thinking. Eventually he grumbles: »Alright. Since they traveled this far. Have rooms prepared for them. I can meet them for dinner tomorrow – or lunch the next day if they wish to recover from the journey first. And I want to know who I am dealing with as soon as possible.«

»Of course, Sir, I will update you as soon as I get any information. Is there anything else I can do for you?«

»These are done«, Panagiotis reaches him two papers.

Nathaniel takes them with a nod and leaves again.

 

At night Nathaniel finds the Duke fallen asleep over his work once again. Silently he summons a bit of magic to at least move him to the couch and covers him with a blanket. And when Panagiotis wakes at first light, he goes straight back to his work. If he had to waste time on those unannounced visitors, then he had to get even more done in whatever time remained. At least he didn’t spend much time wondering who was arriving.

 

Panagiotis is surprised to see the clock was already past noon when Nathaniel knocks: »The delegation from Wolkrim has arrived.«

»Who is it?«

»It is Lord Equadorius, third Prince of Wolkrim, fourth Prince of the Early Spring Court, as well as seven servants of his.«

Panagiotis pales, his face slips and the pen drops from his hand, splatting ink onto the page. Nathaniel quickly waves his hand and prestidigitationsit away.

»What does he want?«, Panagiotis finally finds his voice again.

»He wishes to speak with the Duke of Millguard, It’s about some kind of trade business.«

»Does he… know?«, Panagiotis slowly asks and picks up the pen again.

»Know what?«

»That I’m … you know.«

»I don’t think so, but I don’t know.«

»Good«, he speaks but immediately shakes his head.

»He has requested to meet you before dinner so he can tell you about why he is here«, Nathaniel continues. Panagiotis looks sideways at the stack of work still undone. His heart was beating unsteady, scared.

»I can… squeeze him in, an hour before dinner.«

»As you wish, Sir. I would recommend you take some time to make yourself… more presentable.«

Panagiotis pushes a hand through his hair: »You’re probably correct.«

»I’ll let a bath be prepared for you. You can finish up what you are doing.«

Panagiotis nods thankful and tries to go back to focusing but he is unable to do so. Eventually he gives up and heads to his quarters to freshen up. It had been almost a week since he had last bathed himself, only keeping himself clean with Prestidigitation. It was really good that Nathaniel had talked him out of further reducing the frequency of open day.

Panagiotis makes himself presentable and puts on the whole Duke outfit. He was scared of meeting Equadorius. How high was the chance that he would recognize Panagiotis despite the setting? Judging from their last meeting: high. But that had been years ago! Decades even.

But Equadorius had a great memory and had recognized Panagiotis immediately the last time, despite years having passed back then as well. Curses.

 

A couple of minutes before the meeting was supposed to start, Nathaniel comes in knocking again.

»You look great. How are you feeling?«

Panagiotis answers by raising an eyebrow at him.

»I understand. They are waiting in the negotiation room.«

»They?«

»Yes, the Lord has two of his scribes with him. Do you wish for Iselle to join you?«

»Have her on stand by. First I want to hear what he wants.«

Nathaniel nods, fixes some detail on Panagiotis’ clothes and claps his shoulder.

Panagiotis exhales and gets going.

 

With the clock striking for the full hour, the Lord Panagiotis enters the room where his visitor was waiting.

»Lord Equadorius, I apologize for having kept you waiting. It is a pleasure to host and meet you«, he announces as he enters, quickly going over to Equadorius and shaking his hand in greeting. Panagiotis hopes to overwhelm Equadorius by the sudden introduction and it works.

Equadorius takes a moment before he answers: »All is well. I have been informed you are very busy and that the preceding letter did not arrive. You have my thanks for still receiving me on the short notice.« And Equadorius immediately gestures to his two scribes who open up a wooden box on the table: »It’s a couple of fey spring glasses, forged in the fey wilds. Any liquids filled in them will be purified when one drinks from it.«

»That is very generous of you. I hope your trip wasn’t too draining. Wolkrim is quite a long way, so it must be quite important«, Panagiotis motions for him to sit again and they both do. Equadorius didn’t seemed to have recognize him.

Equadorius chuckles: »Right down to business I see.«

»I apologize. Since I haven’t received the initial letter and since you have traveled this far, I am very curious on your reasoning.«

»That I can understand. In short: I am interested in your craftsfolk.«

»My craftsfolk?«

»As you and your predecessors have gathered some of the best guild people here in Millguard we, the Fey Court of Early Spring, would like to use their services in a larger project. With your consent of course.«

Panagiotis nods: »We are honored. But if you allow me the question: why come here yourself and on this rather short notice?«

»It is much easier and quicker to get the results, we wish for, when talking to the craftsfolk directly. The projects are slightly time sensitive too. And, if I am being honest, I have heard many interesting things about Millguard, I wanted to take a look myself«, he smiles.

»I envy that you have the time for such a travel«, Panagiotis smiles too, crooked and a bit sad. A flicker of confused recognition passes over Equadorius face but he seems to dismiss it quickly.

»Well, yes and no. After all it’s not just for pleasure. I can only express again how grateful I am you agreed to host us.«

»Of course. Since there aren’t any trade relations between Millguard and Wolkrim – or the Fey Wild for that matter – I could not let this opportunity pass. I am quite intrigued on the projects you have. If you don’t mind, I would have my scribe join us...«

»No, of course not.«

Panagiotis picks up a small bell and rings it – but no sound comes out, as it had no clapper. Still, Nathaniel enters a moment later.

»Send in Iselle«, Panagiotis tells him. Nathaniel bows and leaves again. Barely any time later, the scribe enters. She bows before the Lords and sits to Panagiotis’ side in a similar style as Equadorius’ scribes were doing.

Equadorius starts telling Panagiotis about the projects. The Fey court was rebuilding one of the gathering halls and wanted to incorporate some complex design elements that needed a combination of glass-blowing, smithing and rune-crafting. In return, and additional to the regular payment of the craftsfolk, the Fey Court was offering different Fey Wild goods, a trade agreement and a portal connection between Millguard and Wolkrím.

It was quite a good offer at first glance, but since they were asking for a rather quick delivery, and the craftsfolk would need to push back other planned projects, the offer was appropriate over all.

 

The hour passes by quickly and soon Nathaniel knocks again: »Lords, dinner would now be served.«

Panagiotis nods: »Iselle, please contact the required people and inform them about this opportunity. Ask them to gather jointly tomorrow at … two in the afternoon.«

»Of course, my-lord.«

»Lord, might I remind you that tomorrow is Wednesday?«, Nathaniel speaks up.

»Tomorrow? Ah. Well, then let’s gather on Thursday.«

»What is on Wednesday?«, Equadorius asks curious.

»Once a week anybody who wants has an opportunity to step in front of – well – me and bring forth any grievances or wishes. It’s a great way to stay in contact with my people and make sure they stay happy with the current situation«, Panagiotis explains, »Therefore I will be busy for most of the day tomorrow. Do feel free to explore the city or lands should you wish. Or even meet up with the crafters as they will be informed about your request in the morning.«

»You are very generous, Lord Panagiotis.«

»I try my best«, he smiles, »Shall we go eat?«

»Certainly.«

They both get up and Panagiotis leads the other to the dining room. The scribes don’t join them as they would be eating in a separate room.

 

And suddenly Panagiotis is alone with Equadorius – ignoring the sole servant standing by to refill their glasses. They chit chat for a while as they eat.

At some point, Equadorius notes with a laugh: »You know, sometimes you remind me of someone I knew long ago. But it can’t be...«

»Yeah? Why?«, Panagiotis plays the interested bystander and has to keep himself from angling his head when asking. His heart had immediately sped up.

»Long ago I have met an adventurer going by the name ›Thanis‹. You share some of his looks and mannerisms. But you can’t be him, can you?«

»Probably not, no.«

Equadorius chuckles again: »No, I didn’t think so either. He was an interesting character to say the least. Vanished into nothingness for years both times we met.«

»Well, that sounds like the typical adventurer. Sometimes, some come by here and from what I can say, they are all special in their own way.« A short pause: »It does sound like you had a deeper connection with that man.«

The lightest pink blush spreads over Equadorius’ face and he focuses on his plate before he answers: »One could say that. Maybe I shouldn’t have brought it up.«

»It is alright. We can change the topic. How about you tell me more about the Fey Wild?«

Equadorius seems thankful for the understanding as he starts talking again.

 

The rest of the dinner passes by without more bumps and Panagiotis apologizes himself to do some more work. He was glad for the distraction it provided, but his foot was drumming in the air the whole time he was writing and reading endless amounts of papers. He does manage to go to bed for once, but it was still late at night.

The next morning, he gets woken by Nathaniel, he bathes and spends most of the day speaking to his citizens and helping out with their colorful problems. He liked open day, even if it took up a lot of time that could have been used differently. But in the end, it was a tiny bit of relaxation, a change of scenery and it did help a lot with the public relation and how well liked he was by his people.

 

Still he is found in his office at the end of the day so he could catch up on the work, he hadn’t been able to do, because of open day. It was late in the evening and he was deep in concentration, brow furrowed and shoulders tense, when the door gets thrown open with a BANG causing him to scratch the pen over the paper startled.

Equadorius was standing in the doorway panting.

The first burst of anger over the interruption is quickly replaced by worry and Panagiotis half rose as he asks: »Is everything alright?«

Equadorius comes into the room with furious steps. The air fills with the smell of ozone, rain, wet mud and faint blossoms – an upcoming storm. His eyes were glowing green as he commands, rumbling like thunder: »Tell me the truth.«

Panagiotis emotions now shift to concern for himself and he puts a hand on the invisible Eclipse by the table.

»Truth about what?«, his voice is hard.

»You are Thanis! Show me your shoulder!«, the Fey demands and the energy from him almost overwhelms Panagiotis who feels compelled to follow his order. But he steels himself and the compulsion passes.

»You are a guest in my house! You will behave yourself!«, Panagiotis yells out angry and the Fey freezes in his track. He blinks stunned and the glow of his eyes fades – as did the smell in the air.

A couple of guards push through the door while Equadorius’ brain processed what just happened – what he had done – and he starts stumbling back. Panagiotis shakes his head towards the guards and says with a hard tone: »Close the door.«

They pull back and do. Suddenly the two men were alone in the office, the desk between them.

»Have my deepest apologies, L-«, Equadorius starts, half stammering.

»SILENCE!«, Panagiotis commands and immediately the other shuts up. Panagiotis steps around his desk and leans against the front, crossing his arms: »Your kind would enslave a guest for behaving like that. And you. You are an Archfey. You should be better.«
