Equadorius looks like he had been kicked in the groin. Shock and horror, fear and sorrow fight each other in his face, his eyes pleading.

»There is no explanation you could give that would make this better«, Panagiotis continues. »Even Non-Fey would agree that I could – and should – throw you out point blank.« At these words confusion enters into the mix in Equadorius face – and after another moment some hope. »I do like the deal you put on the table though. After this I might need to get more out of it for Millguard.« Panagiotis looks critical at the Fey: »What can you say for yourself?«

»I can only apologize for my behavior. I was overcome with emotion I could not control«, Equadorius speaks with shaking words. »You have already shown so much generosity. I can only ask you to show more and forgive this offense. I will make it up in multitudes if you do. If you proceed with our business after this.«

»You will be indebted to me.«

»Yes, I will«, Equadorius admits reluctantly.

Panagiotis is silent for a moment, then nods: »Then we share an understanding.«

»Yes, Lord«, the other lets his head hang.

For a moment both remain silent in this exact position. Panagiotis was waiting for Equadorius to leave. Equadorius was waiting for another word from Panagiotis.

»Anything else?«, Panagiotis eventually asks sharp.

»If you would grant me one more request«, Equadorius looks up hopeful and fearful at the same time, like he would get slapped the next moment.

»And what would that be?«, Panagiotis’ tone is degrading.

Equadorius dares a half step forward: »Let me touch your shoulder. I need to know.«

Panagiotis pulls a face. He considers the man before him. Considers his feelings. And he considers the position Equadorius was putting himself into.

Why did he have to have a soft spot for that damned fey?

»I’m a fool«, he mumbles to himself after a moment and undoes the collar of shirt so Equadorius could reach inside. The other watches in disbelief and motionless as Panagiotis jerks his head in a calling motion.

Slowly and oh so scared, Equadorius steps forward. His hand trembles as he pushes it under the cloth, over the shoulder and to the back. Panagiotis feels his fingers bump over the scars he had. The touch was gentle, warm, soft. Equadorius is standing so close, he could almost feel his heartbeat. He could definitely feel his faltering breath. And then Equadorius traces the scar, that he had traced so long ago, when they first met.

Tingles seem to rush through Panagiotis, sent from where the fingertips followed the grove in his skin. And before he knows it Equadorius had grabbed the base of Panagiotis’ skull and had pushed him back into the desk with the hips and pulled him into a kiss. The teacup on the desk clatters and falls over as they bump the table.

The kiss was breathless and they have to break for air too soon. Equadorius was shaking and as he moves back ever so slightly, his knee buckles. With the reflexes of a Master Monk does Panagiotis grab the other even before he begins to fall and Equadorius clings himself into his clothes.

»You’ve been mine for so long already«, Panagiotis whispers, his voice raw, »How did you find out?«

»The smith. He had your sword. It confirmed my suspicion«, Equadorius answers husky.

»I told Brassy a million times to melt it down«, Panagiotis chuckles.

»You never came back«, Equadorius begins, judgmental.

»I was never good for you.«

»You are the best there is for me!«, Equadorius looks up at him, pleading, clinging.

But Panagiotis takes him by the shoulders and pushes him back a bit: »I’m a drug for you. And you are deeply addicted.«

Equadorius looks back exactly like an addict who had been denied his drug.

»You aren’t-«, he starts but Panagiotis stops him by shaking his head.

»I am and always were. Every time we met you were doing something you weren’t supposed to do. Sleeping with a low life adventurer? Sleeping with him again while you were engaged? Why would this time be different?«

»But you’re not a low life!«, Equadorius protests.

»You didn’t know that!« Panagiotis starts turning away, rounding his desk again.

»Then: why tell me?«, Equadorius pleads and steps after him.

»I hoped it would give you some peace of mind: to know where Thanis ended up. Ah- shit-«, he had noticed the spill that had flooded half his desk and soaked a good amount of papers.

»You cannot tell me, you don’t want it too!«

»And even if I would, this is not a world where I can allow myself to give into this temptation. I have work to do; duties to perform. I cannot chase this fantasy. You should let it go too. And this should clean itself up«, the last part is spoken to the spilled tea and through the words he casts Prestidigitation to clean it up.

»Thanis...«

Panagiotis exhales heavy: »I have not used this name for so long. Thanis got buried the day I became Duke.«

»One night. Let’s get drunk and-«, Equadorius pleads.

»No!«, the word is a sharp knife cutting through the air, hurting Panagiotis as much as it was Equadorius – but Panagiotis didn’t show it. »I can’t. Not in good conscious. And I cannot get drunk to loose the ›good consciousness‹ either. It’s physically impossible for me.«

»How-?«

Panagiotis leans heavy on the table with both hands. When he looks up at Equadorius he looks so tired: »Tell me one thing: what language are we speaking?«

Equadorius blinks confused: »Common«, but he sounds unsure. »No. Sylvan…«, he furrows his brows confused by his own mouth and brain.

»It wouldn’t matter either way. I can understand any and all languages. And everybody understands me. You know I’m a Monk. I control the energy in my body to a degree only few people do. I do not need to eat. I do not age. And I am immune to poisons. Alcohol is a poison: it does not affect me.«

Equadorius looks at him stunned again and finds no words.

Panagiotis sits down and repeats: »I cannot give you what you like. You should let it go.«

»Is there no way?«

»No. And if you can’t let it go, I will ask you to leave«, he starts ordering the papers, checking them for tea-damage.

»I am so sorry«, Equadorius mumbles.

Panagiotis looks up and sees tears sparkle in his eyes. »Me too«, he says through a tight throat.

And Equadorius leaves. And Panagiotis remained in the deafening, sickening silence of the rejection he had given the other.

 

Soon there is a soft knock.

Nathaniel enters and knows.

»Heavy is the head that wears the crown«, he speaks soft. Panagiotis looks up at him and nods. He palms his eyes dry.

»Why don’t you?«, Nathaniel asks.

»It’s not proper«, Panagiotis’ voice is the dry husk of someone choking.

»Since when do you care about proper?«

Panagiotis doesn’t answer.

»We servants don’t speak about what happens behind closed doors...«

»But you whisper.«

»Maybe, but that doesn’t matter. Panagiotis...«, he puts a hand on the desk. »Panagiotis, look at me.«

Panagiotis looks up into the serious face of his long time servant and, most importantly, friend.

»Don’t deny yourself this bit of pleasure. You deserve it. You are way to hard on yourself.«

Panagiotis exhales.

»If you prefer, I’ll send him somewhere to meet you.«

»You think this is a good idea?«, Panagiotis asks unsure.

»Since when do you care if I think something you do is a good idea?«, Nathaniel seems annoyed: »You have barely been listening to my advise for weeks, months even. Why start now?«

Panagiotis clucks his tongue – Nathaniel was right.

»Take a break«, Nathaniel continues, »Go somewhere you like. I’ll send him after you.«

Panagiotis looks still unconvinced.

»I’ll put a blanket into the glade. If you go there, I’ll make sure he’ll join. And if you don’t we will never speak about any of this again. Does that sound like a deal?«

Panagiotis gives him a tired look. Nathaniel smiles: »I take this as a yes. Give me ten minutes. Or take your time.«

Nathaniel’s step has just a bit too much bounce as he leaves.

Panagiotis lowers his forehead onto the table, trying to sort out his thoughts and feeling but to no avail. Eventually he continues working, finishing the letter he had been writing, despite the tea stains on the paper. He could barely concentrate, his shoulder still seemed to tingle from the touch and his heart is screaming.

 

Once he finishes he puts the letter on the ›finished‹ stack – the stack was empty otherwise. A scribe would create a clean copy from it later and they would send it out.

With a sigh he rises, torn between lust and logic. Should he go to bed or the grove? In his head Nathaniel’s scolding echoes, as does the warmth of Equadorius’ lips on his.

When he finally he moves, he grabs the invisible Eclipse and just before he leaves the room, Oghma’s pan flute.

Panagiotis shakes his head and pushes the basket aside, laying Eclipse beside it. Instead he holds the flute in his hands and looks at it for a while. He hadn’t played it in so long. There just wasn’t any time. Would the music still accept him?

Scared he blows the first notes. The sound was soft and clear. And it felt good. It felt right.

He closes his eyes and begins playing. Simple at first but as he gets lost in the music more and more, the melody gets more complex too. Panagiotis doesn’t notice how he adds to it by summoning faint tunes from magic and he doesn’t see the colors and shapes sparking around him. He also doesn’t notice when he starts crying.

The song breaks off as the tear fluid runs down the back of his throat and the clear tones break off. Sobbing Panagiotis raises his hands to cover his eyes. He was crying over a life given up; wishes unfulfilled; a love lost; dreams broken.

And someone gently wraps their arms around his lowered head and pulls it to their chest. They smelled of flowers and rain: Equadorius.

He doesn’t speak.

Neither do.

And Panagiotis lets himself feel for the moment. All the emotions bottled up inside of him, they tear our. He had been working so hard just so that he didn’t need to feel. It had been clear since the beginning: he would be miserable should he allow himself to feel.

But now Equadorius was back. The man made with disruption and new beginnings; the bringer of spring. The man that had fully fallen for him for so long; that had been ready to give up everything in a heartbeat to run away with him back in the day.

Now Panagiotis was the one who wanted to run away.

 

Hours pass. Or maybe it was just minutes. Equadorius holds Panagiotis who was silently spilling tears. And Panagiotis wasn’t trying to control himself until he finally runs dry; until his tears stop on their own because his body didn’t have enough liquid anymore.

»I-«, he tries speaking, but his voice breaks.

»Don’t say anything«, Equadorius whispers, stroking over his back.

And Panagiotis doesn’t. But he does pull his head out of the hug and he reaches upwards, pulling Equadorius’ head down to his. Their lips meet. The kiss tastes of salt.

Equadorius pushes into Panagiotis hungry since he had just waited for this moment. Panagiotis lets himself be pushed over, coming to rest in the fur below him and with Equadorius above.

»Let me take your pain«, Equadorius whispers.

»You can’t«, Panagiotis turns his head away.

»I can. If you let me, I will make you think you see the gods.«

This makes Panagiotis laughs out: »Been there. Done that.«

Equadorius pulls back confused: »What?«

»Seen the gods. Met them even. A couple of them at least.«

»Wait. Slow down. What?!« Equadorius was now fully sitting upright over Panagiotis.

And Panagiotis starts laughing when he sees the confused face of Equadorius.

»You think the guy who has caused the collapse of the fey economy TWICE didn’t meet a god?«

Equadorius chuckles at seeing him laugh: »There is the man I know.«

But the laugh from Panagiotis lips fades at that. Before he can say anything Equadorius descended on him again silencing him in a warm kiss. Panagiotis wasn’t giving it back.

»Stay with that man. You are him«, Equadorius whispers between caresses: »Imagine we are still young. Young and free.« And as he speaks and kisses Panagiotis lower jaw, his eyes start to glow, as he weaves a charm. »Let me take you away. Back to a time where you still allowed yourself to dream. Focus only on me.«

The air begins smelling of blooming flowers. Panagiotis isn’tresisting the charm, closes his eyes and lets himself go. He noticeably relaxes under the wanting touch and lets Equadorius pull off his shirt.

Panagiotis basically melts as Equadorius starts massaging him, gently working out all the tension in his sore body. He kisses every scar like they were all treasures to be valued and he brings back memories of better days so lively that Panagiotis forgets they are just memories.

 

Equadorius holds onto the base of Panagiotis’ skull like he is raising a goblet, stretching out the others neck while fondling and kissing his jaw and neck passionately. He is seated on Panagiotis waist to make sure Panagiotis wasn’t going anywhere – but Panagiotis wasn’t planning to. He is lost in the touch, in the memories, in the charm, in the moment.

»Finally I know your name«, Equadorius speaks breathless.

Panagiotis smiles, eyes closed: »Know it. But you don’t have it.«

»Panagiotis«, Equadorius whispers and Panagiotis feels drawn towards the fey whether or not he possessed the name.

»Equadorius.«

»Panagiotis.«

»You are mine«, Panagiotis smirks.

»I won’t let go of you, Panagiotis.«

»There is no need to.«

»Panagiotis.«
»Endo«, Panagiotis speaks and goosebumps run over Equadorius’ whole body.

»You remember-«

»Every bit of it«, Panagiotis smiles and topples over the fey in a quick, strong motion causing Panagiotis to sit on top. He smiles down at Equadorius who was covered in sweat.

»I remember it all like it was just yesterday. Like not a single day has passed. And you are all mine.« He was holding on to Equadorius wrists, the weight on his waist – Equadorius wasn’t going anywhere.

Equadorius shivers. And Panagiotis bows down to kiss him.

 

Eventually they lie side by side. Panagiotis feels dried out and high. Sweat clings to both their bodies and an exhausted silence rests over them.

Panagiotis is looking up into the starry sky; Equadorius is looking at him.

Finally Equadorius pushes himself up and takes the bottle of wine from the basket: »Drink something.«

Panagiotis sits up too and drinks. The wine was heavy and sweet. For a moment he can feel the alcohol stuff his brain with fog but the effect vanishes as quickly as it had come. He passes back the bottle and lays down again, a sigh escaping his throat.

»What are you thinking about«?, Equadorius asks.

»I’m thinking about the things I gave up. I have lost.«

»Don’t think about those.« Equadorius lays his head on Panagiotis’ chest and traces the muscles of his stomach with a finger.

»Tell me about your music instead. It was beautiful.«

»I learned to play from Oghma himself«, Panagitois says and Equadorius stops mid-trace.

»The Patron God of Bards? Lord of Wisdom?«, he asks in disbelief, raising his head to look at Panagiotis.

Panagiotis laughs lightly: »Yeah.«

»How in the worlds did you manage to meet him?!«

»I didn’t do anything! He was just kind of there!«, Panagiotis raises his hands lightly.

Equadorius lets his head drop again: »Are you sure you are not an Eldritch being instead of a human?«

»Pretty sure«, Panagiotis continues chuckling.

»You are truly a wonder of nature«, Equadorius kisses him again. »Would you play something for me?«

»If you like...«, Panagiotis starts sitting up.

Equadorius does too and hands him the flute: »Please.«

Panagiotis looks at him, then the flute. Again he was reluctant to start playing. He didn’t know what to play. He doubted he would be any good.

»Play what you feel in your heart«, Equadorius smiles encouragingly as he sees the other’s doubt. He lays his head on Panagiotis legs.

»...what my heart feels like… That’s a hard question.«

»To answer?«

»No, to express it like that...«

»If somebody can, it’s you.«

Panagiotis still hesitates, thinking for a bit longer. He eventually finds something in him but he doesn’t take up the flute: »I need a different instrument...«

»What do you need?«

»There is a harp in my room...«

Equadorius smiles and pulls himself up. They both throw over their clothes again – at least one layer each, Panagiotis grabs Eclipse and Equadorius the basket and Panagiotis’ hand before they starts to run over the dew wet grass back to the mansion.

 

The mansion was silent, yet they still climb the wall up to the balcony of Panagiotis’ chambers. Panagiotis opens the doors and heads into the darkened room, knowing it well enough to navigate only by the faint star light.

He sits down by the harp, taking it between his legs as Equadorius lays down on the couch. They were both silent and again Panagiotis hesitates.

Finally he raises the flute and blows a deep note, letting it hang in the room for a moment. He adds to it slowly, eventually reaching out with one hand to pluck a few strings. And in those first moments, he casts some Prestidigitation to continue the tones of the flute, even as he puts it down and plays on on the harp.

It was a slow melody, a dark one. Rolling tones dragging out and yet plucked so lightly on the harp.

And then Panagiotis sings – and it wasn’t Common but Equadorius still understood his words through the magic of Panagiotis’ Ki:

 

Dunkelheit kommt / Lang schon das Träumen verlernt

Darkness comes / long unlearnt dreaming

Und die Zärtlichkeit / Scheint einen Halbmond entfernt

And the affection / seems a half moon away

And my cages is / out of pure gold

Mensch aus Stahl

Panagiotis picks up the flute again, playing the end of the song on it again and lets it fade out eventually.

Equadorius is mesmerized, looking at Panagiotis like he was the most beautiful thing in existence. And in Equadorius’ eyes he is. But the endless sadness, Panagiotis had sung of, didn’t pass by him unnoticed either: he has tears sparkling on the edge of his eyes.

»The only reason I’ve been working so much is that I don’t have to think about this«, Panagiotis says eventually, breaking the silence that had formed in the room.

»Wake up, work. And work until I literally fall asleep. Just so that I get away from all of this.«

»Golden cage«, Equadorius repeats from the song.

Panagiotis nods.

Silently Equadorius gets up and goes over to Panagiotis, gently guiding him into another kiss: »But you are not alone.«

»I basically am«, Panagiotis pulls away. »Nobody from this life should know of my old life – and nobody from my old life should know who I am. That was the price I agreed to pay when I first went traveling. Some people of the city know, some servants. You should have never found out.«

»But I did.«
»And you’re still married.«

»In an open relationship«, Equadorius corrects, »And you are unwed.«

»We cannot have anything, Equadorius!«, Panagiotis says pained, »Nothing that lasts.«

Equadorius pulls away with a sigh. He sits down again.

»I know. In the end I know that… But that doesn’t mean we can’t have an affair. Many nobles have one«, Equadorius says.

Panagiotis gives him a pained look: »I still need to marry and produce an heir. An affair won’t benefit that.«

»Neither will you drowning yourself in work.«

Panagiotis sighs: »I know.«

»Then how about I help you find a good wife? One you will like in all ways – I know your taste. And while I’m around I can let you forget your life for a while.«

»Why would you do that?«

»Two reasons: one, I owe you and it’s really bad news for potentially everyone when an Archfey owes someone. And two is more simple: I love you, Panagiotis. If I could, I would remain by your side for every moment of your life. Any and all.«

»Endo...«

»No, you don’t get to speak right now«, Equadorius moves onto him again, pushing him back and kissing him. »You are too amazing to make yourself waste away in this life forced upon you. You could become a legend with your fighting skills, your magic, your wit. You have met gods and slept with a real powerful fey. You are the Duke of Millguard. You can forge your own path.«

Panagiotis had started to avoid Equadorius’ gaze for the last couple of sentences. Equadorius gently pushes his chin back to the middle, guiding Panagiotis’ gaze back to his eyes.

»I will make sure you will find your happiness again even without you deserting your post here. You will feel alive again. And that is a promise«, his eyes flash up with a green glow again but it was already fading as Equadorius descends on him for another kiss.

 

 

The next morning, they are woken by a knock and Nathaniel entering.

»Good morning, Lords«, he speaks formally like it was totally normal to find another Lord – and an Archfey for that – in Panagiotis’ bed. But as Panagiotis starts sitting up, he notices Nathaniel was fighting back the biggest grin.

»Do you wish to have breakfast served in the east lounge or shall it bring it here?«

»I don’t need-«, Panagiotis starts speaking but Equadorius pushes him back down.

»Bring it here«, Equadorius half glares at Panagiotis.

»Certainly, my lords. Will you require anything else?«

»No«, Panagiotis says and Equadorius too shakes his head.

Nathaniel bows and leaves them again. Panagiotis watches the corners of his mouth spring up into a grin, just as he was turning. Equadorius was already focused on Panagiotis again, climbing onto him again.

»I am not sure if I like or dislike your valet«, he tells Panagiotis.

»You should like him. Very much.«

»Why?«

»Because he told me to go to the grove? To allow myself this? And because he told you to go find me there somehow.«

Equadorius breaks out in a grin too now: »In that case I like him very much« and he kisses Panagiotis passionately.

»You can’t imagine how happy it probably made him to find you here«, Panagiotis tells him afterwards.

»Probably as happy as I am being here«, Equadorius grins and another kiss happens.

Panagiotis just smiles through it. He felt good too – a rare event since he became Duke.

Chapter 30: Revelations

Summary:

Arkaniest confronts Pan about why he doesn't want to date her.

Notes:

10 April 2024 - 709 words

Chapter Text

»So, do you not want to date me because I am a mere mortal or what?«, Arkaniest asks, finally cracking.

»You make it sound like I am not«, Pan answers.

»I am not sure you are sometimes! How many people can summon gods just by talking about them? How many have seduced an Archfey? Like what the fuck, Pan? Maybe Iron was right when she was about to ward the smithy against ghosts because of you.«

»I am human, Arkaniest. And I’ll happily confirm that in a Zone of Truth.«

»Then why do you want to date me? What’s the actual reason?«

Pan looks over to her. She was leaning against the windowsill, arms crossed. He lay on the bed, his head hanging off the side upside down.

»Because it’s too complicated«, he says after a moment.

»How do I make it complicated?«

»Not you. Me.«

»Explain.«

He closes his eyes for a moment. The blood was gathering in his head and it was making him stupid – which was the exact reason why he was laying upside-down.

»Because I cannot date anyone. I have to court people.«, he spat out the world like it was disgusting. »Dating can have no strings attached, even if there is potential for them. But if I would date anyone, they would immediately get strings tied to them – whether they like it or not. Whether they know of it or not.«

Arkaniest slid down the wall and sat cross legged. »You really are noble, aren’t you?«, she asks slowly.

He nods: »The theory you had in Millguard was correct.«

»But why…?«

»Why I lie about it? Because I want to experience the string-free life for one. If I would tell people about who I am then I would have to behave like that too«, he explains.

»You lied to me«, she mutters in disbelief.

»You and everyone else. Not even Kambera knows.«

»No! In Millguard, when I asked you about it. You promised you would tell the truth!«

»I literally said ›No promises‹ and you were alright with it. And I didn’t say I wasn’t him either – I just sowed enough doubt for you to believe I did say that.«

»You are a real pain«, she says with a hurt undertone.

»I know. And you have every right to despise me for it.«

Arkaniest is silent for a while as she considers Pan. »I don’t.«

He just nods.

»Can I ask… what would the strings be that you would attach to your courted lady?«

»I don’t attach them. They get attached by the nature of who I am.«

»Tomato, tomato. What are they?«

He looks at her again: »You really want to know, huh?«

She nods and he sits up, leaning against the head board of the bed next. »Well… It would become a public relationship pretty fast. That means people – you might not even know – start judging you; who you are, where you come from, what do you. You would need to attend official functions with me and behave correctly there too. And over long or short there’s the expectation of marriage. And that would mean you would become Duchess and would have to do Duchess things for the rest of your life.«

»You say it like it’s a bad thing.«

»If you ask me: it is.«

»Is that also the reason why you risk your life adventuring?«

»Yeah. It took me half an eternity to convince my parents to let me go.«

»Your parents… I did meet them after all«, Arkaniest says pensive.

»They liked you a lot«, Pan smiles, »They actually wanted to invite you to the ball after meeting you.«

»What ball?«

Pan groans: »They host a ball each year to find me a wife. It’s a real pain, trust me.«

»Hm...« She fidgets with her nails and doesn’t look at him when she continues speaking: »Maybe it wouldn’t be so bad if I were there.«

Pan raises an eyebrow but she doesn’t see.

»Are you serious?«

She looks up and smiles shyly as some hair falls over her face.

»Are you really sure you want to do that?«, he asks again.

She shrugs: »It’s a chance to see you all dressed up. And why not?«

He humpfs.

Chapter 31: Young Panagiotis

Summary:

Panagiotis, 12 years old, gets a new caretaker and is a nightmare to deal with.

Notes:

15 April 2024 - 1978 words

Chapter Text

It was in the middle of autumn and the young Lord Panagiotis of Millguard was a nightmare of 12 year old preteen with an inhumane well of energy. He had recently achieved his long term goal of getting his longer-term nursemaid Tessa to quit.

»Never, in all my years, did I have to deal with a child as rambunctious as this!«, she had yelled at Taraxacum after he had escape her watchful eye for the fourth time that day and they had finally found the pile of stones he used to climb over the walls surrounding the mansion.

»Not even the triplets were that bad and they had three prepubescent nightmares as brains! You can be glad that that child isn’t malicious otherwise Millguard would be on fire every other week!«

And despite Taraxacum’s pleads, Tessa packed her bags afterwards and left.

Panagiotis was stoked about it. He had despised her for many years now: she was old and slow and no fun at all. She kept yelling at him when he showed her some of his ›achievements‹ and always expected him to sit still and behave.

Now Panagiotis was free from her and made it everybody else’s problem. Whenever he wasn’t getting lessons – and even when he was supposed to get them – he got all up in the servants’ business hindering their work or he was just straight up running off into town.

The servants eventually resorted to giving him some of their own tasks, making him work, which was as undignified for someone of his birth and status as it was effective.

Nevertheless, they were still begging the Duke and Duchess to find a replacement for Tessa quickly. And the Duke and Duchess were trying to find one, receiving half a dozen to a dozen of applicants each day despite their busy schedule but finding them all lacking when it came to dealing with the nightmare that was their son at the moment.

In the meantime they just ramped up all lessons in which Panagiotis was moving, since those were the ones he escaped least from and sometimes he got powered out enough to actually sleep for once.

 

It was a rainy afternoon and the young Panagiotis was having a sword fighting lesson with Mister Hemos. Despite Panagiotis young age and small size – causing shortswords to have the proportions of longswords in his hands – he was already quite a skilled fighter who had long mastered the basics.

It was towards the end of the lesson, Hemos and him were having a training fight, when Salixia and a new face entered the training room. The new face belonged to a well dressed, comparatively young looking elf with pale skin and long white hair with beads braided into it.

Panagiotis became momentarily distracted by the entrance but not enough that Hemos could break through his defense. Parrying the two attacks pulled his mind back to the fight at hand and he goes into offense himself. Hemos deflected his wooden sword once, then swiped his legs, making the boy trip. Panagiotis finds himself sitting on the ground.

»What did you do wrong this time?«, Hemos asks and stretches a hand out to help Panagiotis up.

He takes it and sounds bored as he answers: »I forgot to defend my legs while attacking.«

»And?«

»And I got distracted.«

»Very much so. You always need to keep your focus on the enemy in front of you.«

»Wrong!«, it sounds from Panagiotis immediately. »You could get ambushed if you don’t pay attention to your surroundings!«

Hemos keeps himself from exhaling tired: »That is true, but-«

»I defended your attack. You can’t say I was too inattentive«, Panagiotis interrupts him again.

»That is true«, Hemos sounded a bit defeated. He turns towards Salixia: »Lady Jazrueltehd, how may I be of service?«

»I will need to cut the lesson short. Panagiotis, would you come over, please?«

Panagiotis jogs over to them and continues looking at the new man like he was a threat. Said man bowed before the sweaty, out of breath child.

»Might I introduce Mister Thethyra?«, she starts as Panagiotis picks up a towel disinterested and wipes some sweat off his chin.

»He will be your new guardian from this day forward.«

This lets Panagiotis pause and he takes in the elf more closely. Panagiotis’ sharp, judging gaze pierced unlike anything a 12-year-old should be capable of doing.

»What’s your first name?«, he asks critical after a moment passes.

»Nathaniel, young master. It is a pleasure to meet you.« He had a soft voice that told a tale of endless patience. Panagiotis raised an eyebrow doubtful.

»My name is Panagiotis«, he sounds annoyed.

»Panagiotis, be nice«, his mother warned soft.

But Nathaniel only smiled: »I look forward to serving you, Panagiotis.«

»Let’s see when you loose him for the first time«, Salixia jokes, clasping a hand on Nathaniel’s shoulder.

»Is that a challenge?«, Panagiotis asks lighting fast.

»No!«, she answers equally quick and sharp.

»Now, Panagiotis, I believe you have your next lesson in law soon?«, Nathaniel speaks up.

Panagiotis looks at him again, doubtful for a moment, then he sprouts a smile: »Yeah! I should change before, tho.« And the throws the towel up to Nathaniel who catches it while Panagiotis was already bouncing away, throwing the door closed behind him.

Salixia just raised an eyebrow at Nathaniel.

He smiled at her: »I know.«

 

Panagiotis ran to his room with a huge grin on his face. He entered, throwing the door closed behind him, barely slowed down as he crossed the space and stepped onto the balcony. He hauled himself over the railing on the side and used on of the banners to slide down to the ground floor before dashing through the garden and around a hedge, out of sight.

»That is an interesting way to the school room«, Nathaniel speaks, arms crossed, as the boy ran past.

Panagiotis got so startled he tripped and tumbled over. But he was a child and basically made from rubber so he barely lay a second in the dirt, as he looked back at the elf, before jumping up and running on.

He ran right into Nathaniel’s arms who somehow stood on the path before him.

»How did you-?!«, Panagiotis almost shrieks as Nathaniel picks him up, throwing him over his shoulder like a sack of grain.

»Your parents chose me for a reason, Panagiotis. I can keep up with you. Now then, you wanted to change?«

Panagiotis grumbles but doesn’t resist being carried back to the mansion.

 

The next few days the servants were breathing a sigh of relief as Nathaniel took over the care of that rascal. Nathaniel was indeed more than capable of keeping up with Panagiotis and he kept him on a somewhat tight leash. At no point did he loose control of Panagiotis and with a single metaphorical tug on the leash he could get him to fall back in line. Nathaniel was always a step ahead of Panagiotis and the boy hated it as much as he loved it. Finally he had gotten a real challenge. Panagiotis wouldn’t have admitted it, but it was fun to have a sparring partner who was better than him – and Nathaniel was better than him (for now).

 

It took almost a full week before Panagiotis managed his first escape. Beforehand Nathaniel had always anticipated his moves, had always appeared in just the place that Panagiotis was passing during his escape or foiled his plans in another way.

The maneuver Panagiotis pulled that day was so stupidly simple, he was surprised Nathaniel hadn’t managed to stop him. Feeling high on the adrenaline of a successful coup, he ran and jumped in full speed through the city to find his friend, Elas.

As always, Elas was found at the sawmill and Panagiotis only had to dash past him for Elas to drop the broom and follow. They didn’t need words to know where they were going: up into the mow where they always hid in.

»Tis! It’s been foreeeever! Where were you?!«, Elas yelled as they threw themselves into the hay.

Panagiotis groaned: »New nanny.«

»So your parents found one?«

»Yeahhh.«

»She’s a pain?«

Panagiotis pushed himself up and faced Elas. He had a huge grin on his face: »He’s great! He’s a pain! But he’s great!«

»What?«, Elas looked back dumbfounded.

»He’s a real pain. Annoying. I think he might be psychic and reading my mind? At least he always knows what I am thinking. He always predicts what I’m about to do, where I am about to go! It’s so annoying! But gods, he’s great! He can take a joke! He knows what I want! And he delivers! Maybe not like I was hoping but in the end, I am content.«

Elas chuckled: »You finally found your match, eh?«

»I wished he were a little less attentive. I want to get out more! I never get to see you anymore!«, Panagiotis threw himself on his friend who groaned under the sudden impact and weight. Elas tired pushing him off and this caused a playful scuffle to break out, where Panagiotis tried to lay himself over Elas and Elas tried to keep him off. Soon they were both laughing so hard they had to stop wrangling each other.

They turned to telling each other stories. As always Panagiotis was listening more since Elas had all the gossip of the city and Panagiotis had been trapped in the mansion.

 

At some point, Panagiotis interrupts Elas by raising his hand. They both listened. Someone started climbing the ladder.

›Hide‹, they mouthed to each other and dug themselves into a small cave in the hay.

»Panagiotis, it’s time for your harp lesson«, Nathaniel called. The two boys froze.

A minute later, a hand reached into the hay, grabbed Panagiotis’ arm who shrieked and grabbed Elas with the other hand.

A short tug-of-war ensued over the boy but since Nathaniel was twice the weight and size of Elas it didn’t take long for both to topple out and sprawl in front of his feet on the hay.

Nathaniel had his hands on his hips and looked down disapprovingly at Panagiotis for a moment. Then he turned to Elas.

»You must be Elas«, he spoke cheerfully and reached him a hand to help him up.

»I- I am, Sir«, Elas stammered and got up.

Panagiotis was starting to crawl towards the ladder.

»My name is Nathaniel. It’s nice to finally meet you, I have heard a lot of you. And Panagiotis, I can see you!«

Sulky Panagiotis stopped, put his chin in his hands, elbows on the ground and silently mocked Nathaniel’s scold.

»Come on, is that how you thank me for letting you see your friend?«, Nathaniel tapped Panagiotis’ side lightly with the tip of his shoe.

»You-«, Panagiotis scrambled to his knees in disbelief.

Nathaniel grinned lightly: »Thought you had one on me?«

Elas started laughing at Panagiotis’ shocked face.

»Now, you will need to clean yourself up before the lesson«, Nathaniel reached Pan a hand too.

Admitting defeat, Panagiotis takes it: »You’re a pain.«

»You are great!«, Elas yelled, wiping some tears of laughter from his eyes.

»I aim to please«, Nathaniel smiled at Elas, »But you should probably also get back to your duties, young Elas. Your father is already looking for you.«

Elas cursed on Elvish and scrambled past them towards the ladder: »Byeee, Tis! Till next timeeee!«

And he was gone.

»Bye, Elas«, Panagiotis answered in a saddened, low voice.

Nathaniel tugged his hand: »Come on. If you’re a good student I’ll let you run off more often to see him.«

Panagiotis huffed, pulled his hand free and started walking to the ladder too. But he didn’t run from Nathaniel. He even waited at the bottom for him.

Chapter 32: Arledge

Summary:

Pan gets thrown overboard during a fight and a familiar merfolk takes care of him

Notes:

16 April 2024 - 8455 words

Warning: abandoned / open ending

(See the end of the chapter for more notes.)

Chapter Text

Pan was traveling by ship down south. He had been home for his 21st birthday and was ready to explore new horizons now.

The ship was sailing past Qrante at the moment. It was the middle of the day and only a light breeze lay in the air, barely capable of moving the galleon. Therefore the sailors used the downtime to throw out some nets and catch fish.

Most of the crew mates and travelers were lounging on deck, enjoying the sun, when something big got caught in the starboard nets and caused the ship to shake.

Some sailors jumped up to pull out their haul, only to yell »Cut the lines!« moments later. More jumped into actions to cleave at the lines of the net but it was too late. Endless amounts of tentacles pushed themselves over the side of the railing and dragged a creature up.

Pun jumped into action at that, drawing his sword, ready for the fight.

The creature resembled an octopus most closely, just that it was twice the size of a bear, had more arms and where the top of the head normally was, a huge, circular maw opened with rows and rows of teeth.

Shouting broke out as the creature dragged itself into the view of everyone, slamming sailors away with it’s arms. Whoever wasn’t fighting, ran to hide. Pan dashed forward and cleaved his sword into one arm, slicing it clean through. He ducked under another arm, stabbed up and danced past another attack in the rhythm of the fight. The crash of the waves against the shaking ship was the beating heart of this one. The beast attacked Pan and others, but Pan just cleaved into the mass of arms with his swords, getting soaked with the ink black blood of the creature, before dodging out of the way of more arms.

He severed the next tentacle and the creature screamed out. It didn’t swipe this time, but it was still writhing endlessly and Pan continued attacking merciless.

Then a saber tore over his abdomen, ripping deep into Panagiotis’ flesh. The saber was held by one of the sailors, who had a crazed look in his eye and stumbled past Pan. Pan gritted his teeth, punched the guy’s lights out and continued attacking the monster.

But because of the distraction, he didn’t notice the tentacle that pushed towards him on the ground, until it slammed into him and threw him into the air. Pan’s trajectory was stopped by the rigging up the mast. His limbs became tangled up and he hung upside down, before managing to get at least three out of four limbs back under his control and out of the rigging, only one leg remaining stuck.

Pan involuntarily made the mistake of looking down. It was too far for comfort. Especially since he would most likely fall headfirst and break his neck. For Pan that meant: Hold on.

He twisted around, grabbed the rig and started trying to pull his leg free, but it was stuck worse than initially suspected. In that position he neither saw, nor had any hope of avoiding, the tentacle coming for him and it slammed into him, pressing him into the rigging, causing his knee to twist so far it jumped out of it’s socket. Pan blinked through tears: ›Just hold on‹, he though, not noticing that he had let go of his sword, which by now had dropped into the endless ocean.

But the monster wasn’t done with Pan yet and hit the rigging two more times, until the ropes ripped under the stress and he got catapulted out, away from the ship. Pan was flying for just a moment, then the ocean greeted him ice cold and rock hard. His neck and shoulders were the first to enter, the impact shocking him to the core and he screamed out. Just that now he was sinking and had no air to inhale. The impulsive inhale caused his lungs to fill with water, he started coughing, tried to get up towards the water surface. But this only causes the rigging to entangle him more and more until he had no hope of breaching before he passed out. For a moment, Pan wasn’t sure if the light was dimming because he was sinking or because he was loosing consciousness, but moments later he didn’t have the mind to wonder anymore.

 

With the head start, Pan’s sword had already sunken deeper into the ocean, where it passed a merman who was gathering kelp fruit. Curiously he grabbed the sword and started swimming up, leaving his basket behind.

Soon the merman saw a body, entangled in a large-meshed net that was attached to the dark shadow of a ship. The body was motionless. Blood was seeping from a wound in his mid and filled the water around. The body had an empty sheath on his belt and the merman looked at the sword in his hand. If his suspicion was right, he probably wasn’t down here for too long – was he still alive? The merman pointed and an air bubble formed around the head of the man but he stayed motionless. Quickly he dashed up and grabbed an arm of the man, soon finding a weak pulse. Hope wasn’t lost for him, so the merman started cutting him loose with his own sword, catching the body against his. And as their skins were touching, the merman noticed his felt strange: slimy like one was touching an amphibian instead of a human. The merman looked up at the shadow of the ship with worry. Clearly something big was causing a ruckus up there and from the signs this man showed it was a creature one didn’t want to meet: an Aboleth. No matter how he turned the situation in his head: the man wouldn’t survive if he tried to return him to the boat. Either because he would be eaten by the Aboleth or because of the disease the Aboleth had caused in him.

So the merman sheathed the sword in the empty scabbard and took the man into the depth.

 

Pan woke coughing and spitting, his body trying to get the water out of its system. He felt horrible, weak and sick to such a degree that he hadn’t felt in a long time.

While he was coughing, he instinctively sat up but that only caused an agonizing pain to shoot through his body originating from his stomach and chest. He curled up further, trying to draw his legs in, but one of his knees was held in a rigid fixture.

»Panagiotis!«, he heard his name being called through the daze of his pain and someone pushed him back into a lying position by the shoulders. It took a moment for Pan to let go of the tension and to relax, but finally he opened his eyes and looked around. Next to him swam a merman, watching him with worried eyes. The room around them was formed by curving walls of natural clay, bio-luminescent corals growing over pretty much every surface and creating a dim twilight. The curtains of the windows were made of strips of kelp, seashells decorating the interior. Outside more houses formed of low domes covered the seafloor, fish, merfolk and tritons swimming past distantly.

Pan focused back on the merman next to him who was still watching him worried. He seemed familiar. His sickness-slowed brain didn’t process yet, what the guy had called him.

Finally Pan found his voice, even if it was hoarse from coughing – and screaming: »What-«

The merman looked relieved as he spoke and started talking himself: »Thank the gods, you are alright. You got injured pretty badly. Can you remember what happened?«

Slowly Pan nodded: »There was a fight… a creature… I got knocked off the ship...«

The merman nodded: »An aboleth, I think. It got you pretty bad. You had luck I found you when I did or you would have drowned.«

Only now Pan realized that he was still, in fact, underwater and at the bottom of the ocean from the looks of it. But he breathed normally: air, not water. He reached up to his face and his hands passed into an air bubble. He looked down on himself. His clothing got removed mostly, he only still wore his pants but one leg had been cut off as to give access to his injured knee. A kelp bandage was wrapped around his stomach. His skin looked sickly, half translucent and was bruised in a lot of places. He couldn’t see any of his belongings he had on him when he fell into the water – neither sword nor coat.

Pan looked back at the merman and finally his brain processed the name that he had been called before.

»Who are you?«, Pan spoke slowly, worry and fear knotting his heart.

»You don’t – No, of course not«, the merman muttered before his face shifted into that of a human. Pan watched confused, stared into the face that was familiar.

It took a couple of seconds before he found the name to it: »Arledge?«

»Yes… my lord«, the other answered slowly.

Pan pulled a face: »Don’t – don’t call me that.«

»What?«

»Just call me Pan. And forget who I am otherwise«, Pan said disapprovingly.

Arledge looked confused and worried: »Are you… sure? Are you feeling alright?«

»Yeah. I am sure. And I am feeling horrible. But, please… do me that favor.«

Pan didn’t find the energy to explain more. His eyelids were fluttering closed with exhaustion, his head was lolling back.

»Alright, Pan«, Arledge finally spoke. »You will need to rest. Your injuries are pretty severe. And that Aboleth infected you.«

»What does that mean?«

»It’s a disease. You will need to remain in water until it’s cured, otherwise your skin dries up and you are wounded by it. A lot of people who have encountered an Aboleth have died just from that. And you can only get rid of it by some strong magic.«

»I don’t suppose you can...?«

»No. But I know a person who can. I have already sent for them but it might take some time for them to arrive. I will take care of you in the meantime.«

»Thank you«, Pan breathed out, fully closing his eyes now.

»Of course«, Arledge answered soft. He reaches into the air bubble and stroked some of Pan’s hair back: »Rest now. I’ll keep you save.«

Pan nodded and stopped his futile attempt at staying awake longer. He felt too broken and weak to do so and it didn’t take long for him to slip into a restless sleep.

Arledge kept close to his patient, watching him toss and turn for hours.

 

The dim twilight on the seafloor hadn’t changed much when Pan woke again. He wasn’t sure how much time had passed since he felt pretty much the same as before. His body still felt shattered and traversed by sickness.

Arledge wasn’t in the room.

Slowly Pan started feeling out his body. Apart from bruises, his arms seemed unhurt but when he carefully patted down his chest, two ribs flared up painful – broken. He hissed at his own touch, at the pain.

He skipped his abdomen that was tightly wrapped with the underwater bandage, slowly sat up and reached for his legs. One knee was immobilized and he remembered it getting twisted when he hung in the rigging. The other leg seemed mostly unhurt, as long as he continued ignoring the bruises.

Finally Pan did probe the bandage around his stomach but the moment he touched it, he gasped in pain and groaned as while curling up.

»Pan – what-?«, Arledge had appeared in the doorway. Pan looked up at him, frozen.

»Do you really feel good enough to sit up?«, Arledge continued, disapproving, came over and pushed him back down.

Pan didn’t resist. »No«, he admitted with a low voice.

»Were you up for a reason?«, Arledge raised an eyebrow.

»I… I checked myself for injuries. Didn’t know you were around.«

»I’m not gonna leave you unattended while you’re in this state. You can just call. Is there anything you need?«

Pan thought for a moment: »Do you have my things?«

»I have your coat and the gear you wore. I even came across your sword – at least I think it is yours.«

»Can I see?«, he propped himself up again a bit.

Arledge nodded and headed into the other room, coming back with the coat and gear. He handed the sword to Pan. Pan drew it out a bit, and relief spread over his face as he mumbled: »Thank the gods.«

Then he looked up at Arledge: »It is mine. Thank you.«

»It’s alright. I’m just glad I could help. That you didn’t die.«

Pan nodded and was silent for a moment. »Do you know what happened to the rest of the crew?«

»Not really. A few bodies were found but the ship has sailed since then. It seems at least some have survived.«

»That is good«, Pan muttered pensive.

Arledge sat down on the edge of the cot and looked at him worried: »Were you traveling with someone? Should I keep an eye out for anyone?«

»No. I was alone. You don’t need to worry about that. I just – I just need to inform my parents I’m alive still. If they hear the ship was attacked and see I went missing...«

»I’ll make sure they are informed you are alright. Did you not have any bodyguards or-?«

Pan interrupted him by shaking his head sharply.

»I will make sure your parents are informed«, Arledge said again after a moment of silence.

»I would appreciate that. And… if you could do me another favor… I had some things on the ship. If you could find out where they landed...«

Arledge nodded understanding: »I will see what I can find out. And Pan?«, he laid a hand on Pan’s healthy knee: »It’s no issue. I am really just glad you are alright. You were really lucky.«

»I am lucky you found me. Again: thank you«, Pan looked serious at him.

Arledge smiled lightly and shook his knee a bit: »Just get better. It won’t be as easy with that infection, so I’ll make sure you don’t need to worry on anything else. Do you want to eat something?«

Pan hesitated a bit at the last question. He considered how he was feeling – it wasn’t particularly hungry. But he still nodded: »Maybe a bit. And something to drink.«

»Give me a few minutes«, Arledge smiled and got up before swimming out. Pan just looked after him with too many thoughts in his head. He still held his sword in one hand and didn’t even notice the time pass as he stared into nothingness.

 

Eventually Arledge returned with a tray in his hand that was surrounded by an air bubble. Pan’s mind got pulled back to the moment and he sat up further, holding his hand over broken ribs and clenching his teeth. The movement and effort made him nauseous.

Arledge watched him worried: »Are you alright?«

»Holding on«, Pan muttered, trying to control the pain by deep breaths.

»The broth should help a bit with the pain. It won’t be perfect, but...«

»Better than nothing«, Pan nodded. Once he had settled, Arledge put the tray down in his lap and the air bubbles connected. The tray had a bowl on it with a soup of sea food and a spoon made from fish bone.

»Thank you, again«, Pan picked up the spoon, »I’ll make it up to you.«

»When you are better, you can. After you have eaten, I would like to check your wound again.«

Pan nodded and started eating, slowly. He still didn’t feel like eating, but made himself do so anyway – if only for the possible pain relief.

Arledge watched him quietly and worried. He didn’t like how sickly Pan was looking despite the rest and when factoring out the translucent skin. He didn’t like how slow Pan was moving; how out of breath he was; how glazed his eyes were. And Arledge didn’t like how many scars the body already sprouted, even if most of them weren’t new – what had Pan done, that he had ended up beaten up like that?

But Arledge kept quiet. Only when Pan put down the bowl, he spoke up again: »Would you like some more?«

»No, thanks. It was good.« He leaned back, clearly exhausted.

»Would you like a break before I check the wound?«

»It’s fine«, Pan said breathless, eyes closed.

»Then I’ll just put this away and be right back.«

Arledge took the tray and left the room again. He deliberately took his time in the other room, cleaning up and gathering his tools so that Pan could get some rest.

When he came back, Pan had sunken into himself, still half upright, mouth ever so slightly hanging open: asleep. Arledge chuckled at the sight and put his tools down.

He tried to move Pan down so he was laying down again, careful not to wake him. But Pan grabbed Arledge’s arm tightly and startled Arledge’s head whipped around: Pan’s eyes were open.

»I didn’t want to wake you«, Arledge explained soft.

Pan let go and pushed himself up again, slightly growling at the pain: »I didn’t intend to fall asleep.«

Arledge stroked his shoulder even if Pan’s skin still felt slimy: »You can sleep whenever you need to. We don’t need to do anything right now.«

»It’s fine«, Pan gritted his teeth.

Arledge chuckled: »You are said to be restless. But you’re not my first patient like that.«

Now Pan narrowed his brows: »Aren’t you and your family traders? Pearls, if I remember correctly?«

»Well...«, Arledge avoided his piercing gaze, »Let’s call it a side business.«

»Then what are you?«, Pan crossed his arms, his tone sharp and judging.

Arledge exhaled, closing his eyes for a moment: »We’re druids.«

»And?«

Arledge looked a bit confused: »No and?«

»And you’re shape changers? Or something similar. Because the first time we met, you did not have a tail.«

»Ah, yes… That… Yeah. Something like that.«

»How exactly?«, Pan’s tone was still sharp.

Again, Arledge exhaled, then Pan watched as the face lost almost all its features and turning white: a changeling.

After a moment of stun Pan started laughing out, pushing his hands through his hair. The laugh quickly turned into a pained wheeze: »I thought you were some kind of Vampire! That was the only explanation I could come up with! That you didn’t want to endanger me because of your nature!« He was laughing despite the pain it caused him.

»I- I suppose that is fair«, Arledge eventually said, adopting his merman face again.

Pan’s laughter finally died down, ending in pained yelps. An awkward moment of silence developed from it.

»Shall I look at your wound now?«, Arledge eventually asked.

With shallow breaths Pan nodded: »You can.«

Arledge guided him to lay down, moved his tools closer and started to explain: »When I take off the bandage, the water will probably cause some hurt. But I am rather worried about the cut getting infected so I need to check. And maybe I need to stitch it up too.«

Pan nodded and Arledge cut away the bandage careful not to cut Pan. He removed the other bandaging material cautiously, but some of the scab still pulled off and the wound began bleeding again. Pan hissed slightly as the seawater seeped into the wound, making it feel like fire ants were crawling all over his bare flesh.

Arledge waved his hand and created a current to carry the blood away before he started inspecting the wound closely. But no matter how tenderly he touched it – or the area around it – jolts of pain shot through Pan’s system. Ever so often he gasped for air, clenching his fingers into the sea-moss bed, but he still kept remarkably quiet overall.

»So far it looks alright but I can’t say you are out of the woods yet. It’s still a fresh wound and the infection you already have is slowing down any healing you do naturally. I do think I should stitch it up though, especially considering your nature but...«

»Do it«, Pan growled between clenched teeth.

»You-«, Arledge brought out stunned, »It will hurt.«

»You think it’s my first time? Do it! Or wrap it up again!«, he commanded.

»Yes, yes. I’ll get to it«, Arledge quickly said and picked up a shark tooth needle. His hands were shaking lightly, while he was threading the needle, because he didn’t want to hurt Pan and this would certainly hurt him. Pan was forcing himself to breath steady, steeling himself for what was about to come.

Arledge floated up a bit over Pan to have a better angle and hesitated again: »Are you sure you want to do this?«

»Get on with it or I’ll do it myself!«, Pan barked like a commander.

»Yes, Sir!«, he called and got on with it, stitching up Pan. Pan gritted his teeth, balled his firsts, tense all over but he kept pretty quiet. Some pained gasps and small whines did escape his throat but he never screamed. He would have been drenched in sweat had he not been underwater. Now only his face surrounded by the air bubble was.

By the end Pan’s breaths were short and labored. Quickly Arledge put on a thick salve that was supposed to disinfect and soothe the wound. Then he wrapped it again.

Pan barely noticed the change in behavior, trapped in this world of pain. He had his eyes squeezed closed, body still tense all over.

»Rest, Panagiotis«, Arledge mumbled and wiped sweat off the other’s forehead. He remained at the edge of the bed, watching Pan worried until Pan finally slipped into an exhausted slumber, going limp. Only then did Arledge gather his tools and left to clean everything up.

He returned to check on Pan every couple of minutes, eventually finding him clutching his belly with one hand. Quickly Arledge swam over and pulled the hand off. It wasn’t good to put any more stress on the wound and even this much should be avoided. Arledge took Pan’s other hand as well and remained by the bedside, making sure Pan didn’t end up clutching the wound again.

 

When Pan woke, Arledge was sleeping next to him. His head was resting on Pan’s lower arm and he held on to both of his hands. Otherwise he was sprawled out over the edge of the bed.

Pan turned a bit, pulled his arm from under his head and thought about how he could best free his hands without waking Arledge.

And as he thought, he looked at the man – at the changeling. He thought of when they first met: the ball in Millguard where they had ended up in Pan’s room for a bit. Arledge had enchanted Pan only to get uncomfortable himself and pull back before anything between them could happen.

Now, five years later, Pan had ended up in his bed and basically helpless. If Arledge didn’t re-summon the Air Bubble he would die. If Pan left the water he would die. And he would also possibly die from the cut on his stomach. He had to trust that Arledge didn’t take advantage of him in this situation – and that he wasn’t lying about sending messages to the healer and his parents.

Carefully Pan started pulling out his hands from Arledge – Arledge didn’t wake. Pan reached out, hissing as stress came onto his midst, and started to pull Arledge onto the bed. Since they were both somewhat floating underwater it was definitely possible for Pan to do so. Pan shifted a bit further towards the wall to give him space, pulled him closer by the waist and, still asleep, Arledge reached out and puts a hand against his chest. Gently Pan took the hand and slid it off, but Arledge held on, pushing his face towards Pan and their lips meet.

Surprised Pan pulled back, but he was already flattened against the wall. He wasn’t necessarily complaining but it still came too sudden for him to not be taken back. And there was one other problem: Pan’s lip had busted during the fight and had yet to heal, so the pressure against the wound caused it to hurt. It was barely a mosquito bite compared to his other wounds but it did add another reason to Pan’s retreat.

»What are you doing?«, Pan breathed lightly, pulling his head back a bit more.

Arledge was looking in his eyes mesmerized for a few moments. Then suddenly his eyes grew wide and he shoved himself away from Pan, swimming in the middle of the room, his head red like a tomato.

»I’m- I’m sorry! I’m-«, he stuttered, swimming a circle as if he was looking for a way out.

»It’s fine«, Pan said with a laugh over his embarrassment, »I am just surprised...«

»Either way, I shouldn’t have done that! I apologize!«, he bowed.

»It’s fineee«, Pan repeated and pushed himself up from the bed.

Before he could do anything Arledge had already swum to him and pushed him back down: »Don’t!« And to emphasize his point he pushed his hand on Pan’s chest: »Don’t get up.«

Only that he accidentally pushed on one of the broken ribs and Pan flinched hard and gasped, pain immediately flooding his mind.

»Got it. You don’t need to push the broken ribs for it«, Pan groaned and grabbed the other’s wrist, pulling the hand off. Arledge became pale of shock. Pan’s grip was painfully strong. Arledge he hadn’t noticed the broken ribs yet. His face turned worried again and Pan let go off him.

»Broken ribs? I- I am sorry. Will you let me check it out?«

»It’s those two«, Pan pointed at a dark bruise on his chest.

»How do you-?«

»Know? Not my first broken rib. Not my last«, Pan half explained.

»How do you keep getting hurt like that?!«

»I am an adventurer.«

»But- you’re-«, Arledge stuttered, »Noble?«

»Yeah? Are they mutually exclusive?«

For a moment Arledge was silent: »No, I suppose not. It’s just uncommon.«

»Are you really surprised given what you know about me?«

»If you put it like that, no.«

»Great. Can we go back to the problem at hand?«, Pan’s voice was harsh.

»Sorry, yes. Let me...«, Arledge swam above him and lightly touched him. He muttered some words of magic and a warmth spread under Pan’s skin.

When Arledge moved back, Pan tenderly tested the ribs. They were still sore but certainly better than before: »Thank you.«

»I hadn’t noticed they were broken yet, I’m sorry.«

»It’s not that bad. Why aren’t you using more magic to heal me?«

»I can only do that a limited amount of times. I already healed your knee, even though it’s probably still sore, and I did help the cut along. But the infection hinders healing magic as well. And once Oenethera arrives to heal you proper, it will become irrelevant anyway.«

»That makes sense.«

»I just want to make sure you’re not needlessly suffering«, Arledge started to reach out towards his face, but stopped even before he reached the air bubble.

»Thank you. Again.« Pan took his hand and guided it to his cheek, smiling lightly.

There was more than a hint of Want in Arledge’s eyes, then he turned red and pulled his hand back without resistance from Pan.

»I shouldn’t-«

»Shouldn’t or...«

»Shouldn’t«, Arledge swam back a bit more. Pan nodded quietly, letting him go.

»If you need something, call«, Arledge told him and then hurried out of the room to escape the awkwardness.

Pan sighed and let himself lay down again, closing his eyes. Again he tested his ribs but now they were just bruised. His hand remained on them, when he relaxed. He thought about Arledge, if he had acted wrong but apart from the situation being confusing, he didn’t find a fault in his behavior.

For a while longer he just laid there, listening to the sounds at the bottom of the ocean, before he eventually fell asleep once more.

 

 

»I have brought you food«, Arledge’s words pulled Pan from his slumber. »Sorry, were you sleeping?«

Pan pushed himself up: »Just a bit.«

Arledge handed him the tray. There were more dishes now: a bowl of the soup, some cooked grain and a small cooked fish.

»Thank you.«

Arledge nodded and left again for Pan to eat in peace. It was still a light meal, but more substantial and Pan was glad for it. Yet, he did end up having problems finishing it, as his body was still weak from the strain and not ready to eat this much.

Eventually, Arledge returned, checking up on Pan: »Do you want anything else?«

»No. I’m alright. It was good.«

»I’m glad«, he smiled, takes the tray and left again.

Pan looked after him in silence.

 

Now having slept and eaten once again, Pan became restless. He still felt sickly but his natural instincts to explore caused an itch: he was in a new place after all. He knew he should stay in bed and not move. He knew it would cause him pain to do so. And Pan knew Arledge would be worried sick if he would come back and not find Pan, but Pan couldn’t fight that urge for long.

He fixed his sword on his belt, pushed himself up and swam towards the window. Since one of his knees was still in a brace and he tried to keep his mid rather stable swimming wasn’t exactly easy or graceful but he managed to move.

He reached the window and pushed himself out and up, slowly pirouetting to take in everything around him. The village consisted of many low domes that rose between the over-grown seafloor. Corals, kelp and sea grass created a warm-feeling environment and if one didn’t know the domes were houses one could have missed them entirely. A couple of fishes were dashing between hiding places. Distantly larger creatures swam around: merfolk and tritons, doing their day’s work, but also larger fish and whales. It was alien and beautiful.

Amazed Pan sat himself onto the top of the dome and continued looking out and around. He loved watching the going ons of busy places and even if this wasn’t particularly busy, it was all still new and therefore interesting. Most curious for him was the fact that it was quiet. The water wasn’t carrying the sounds too far and whatever was reaching Pan’s ears, was muffled. It was peaceful.

 

For a good while Pan got lost in the moment. He was calm. He was happy. He didn’t mind if he had to remain here for a while for the healer to arrive.

Pan was mid inhale, when the air bubble suddenly vanished. Naturally he inhaled a bunch of water before he could stop himself. Then he started coughing – and panicking. Quickly he grabbed his mouth and nose, squeezing both shut trying to suppress his urge to cough and inhale as he scrambled to return to the room. And with the little air he had left in his lungs he squealed, hoping Arledge would hear.

With only one hand and one leg, swimming turned out even more difficult. He desperately needed air. He reached out to the window frame with the hand that had held his airways and just pulled himself through. Desperate he tried yelling Arledge’s name, but didn’t get further than the first letter as water flooded his mouth. His lungs were empty, he needed to breath in but there was just more water. And despite his better judgment his instincts took over and he did breath in, his lungs filling with water.

Arledge came through the door and dashed towards Pan, who was coughing and convulsing again, as a new air bubble formed around his head. Arledge grabbed his shoulders, steadied him and Pan grabbed onto him, gasping and coughing, spitting out more water until his body finally calmed down.

»I’m so sorry. I didn’t realize how much time has already passed«, Arledge pleaded when he started to calm down. Pan still clung onto him, again exhausted from the stress.

He couldn’t yet speak, still breathing heavily. After a moment Arledge pushed his hand into Pan’s hair and with that his head up: »Are you alright?«

Pan didn’t answer. He just pulled himself closer to Arledge and hugged him. He was shaking.

Arledge held him, stroked his back for a few moments, muttering quietly: »You are alright. You are alright.«

It took a solid minute before Pan finally managed to pull himself together. He put his forehead down against Arledge’s shoulder, muttering: »I was so scared.«

»I am sorry. I shouldn’t have let this happen. I am so incredibly sorry.«

»It was an honest mistake«, Pan still breathed heavily but he wasn’t angry at all.

»Come on«, Arledge gently guided him back to the bed. Pan didn’t resist and let himself be pushed down again.

»What were you even doing?«

»Just… sitting on the roof, looking around...«

»You- you shouldn’t be leaving the bed. But I can understand you«, Arledge spoke slowly, sitting next to Pan, an arm still over his shoulder, »I won’t scold you. I think you were punished enough.«

Pan let his head hang: »Thank you.«

There’s a bit more quiet between them, until Pan started laying down.

Arledge looked at him worried but let him go: »How are you feeling?«

»Exhausted. I-«

»I’ll let you rest«, he raised but Pan grabbed his hand.

»Stay please.«

Arledge hesitated a moment, but the pleading eyes of Pan moved him to sit down again.

»Can I ask you something?«, Pan started slowly.

»Of course.«

He spoke uncertain: »How do you breath underwater? Changelings can’t-«

»No, they can’t. I have a ring of water adaption. It also allows me to shift into this form.«

»That’s… fair. And practical.«

»It is, yes.«

Pan was still holding on to his hand.

»Are you alright, Pan?«

Pan looked down: »No, I don’t think I am...«

»Talk to me«, hesitantly Arledge laid beside him, looking at Pan fondly.

Pan still avoided his gaze and it took a moment for him to speak: »I… I’m not used to feeling this helpless. Without your help I would be dead twice over. Even if I would try to leave just as you recasted the air bubble, there is no guarantee that I would make it back to the surface and to the shore. And even then I can’t leave the water. You-«, Pan’s voice caught.

»I’m taking care of you. You don’t need to worry«, Arledge reached out and laid a hand against his cheek.

»I know. But- What if...«, he stopped, lightly shaking his head.

»What if what?«

»If you were anyone else. You would just need to be a bit more malicious and you could take advantage of this situation so much.«

Arledge paled at the words, pulling his hand back like Pan was burning him: »I would never-!«

»No, not you. But I can’t help but worry. Especially after this.«

»Pan… I-«

»No, I know. I’m sorry.«

Arledge reached out to Pan again, cupping his face: »You have every right to worry, Pan. And you are right: probably every other person would take advantage of this. But not me. I swear. I don’t know how long until Oenethera arrives but until then I will be here. I won’t leave your side, if you don’t want me to.«

»Thank you, Arledge. I-«

»You don’t need to say anything«, Arledge whispered and pulled Pan closer to him.

This time Pan saw the kiss coming and he didn’t pull away. And even if the touch still hurt thanks to the busted lip, it was still filled with so much warmth and comfort, he could ignore that hurt easily. Pan wrapped his arms around Arledge’s neck and held the back of his head.

After a couple of second Arledge pulled back, muttering to himself: »What am I doing?«

»I think you’re doing the thing your heart wants«, Pan whispered.

»I’m sorry, Panagiotis«, Arledge backed away further.

But Pan didn’t let him go this time: »No. Explain. Please. I want to understand what is happening. You keep enchanting me and then pulling away. Why?«

»I don’t have enough self control...«, he said avoiding Pan’s eyes.

»You’re not supposed to love me. You said that. But why not?«

»You’re my patient. You’re so much higher up in the hierarchy than me. You… shouldn’t know our secrets...«

»Well, that ship has sailed.«

»I know«, Arledge’s tone was pained.

»And you know my secret: that I’m an adventurer. So I think it’s fair if we both keep quiet about the other’s. And about the other points...«

»Pan, please. I am not worthy.«

Pan kissed him.

»My mother married a farmer. Do you really think we care about social status?«, Pan asked once he let off.

»You’re still my patient.«

»And we are both smart enough to make our own decisions and differentiate two situations, right?«

»You’re a pain.«

»I know«, Pan smiled and Arledge pushed into him for another kiss.

Arledge was kissing Pan passionately, like someone drowning gasping for air – Pan had experience in that now. He ate up Pan’s lips fully like they would escape him should he not be quick enough.

Subconsciously Pan thought ›I also am in pain‹ as stress came onto his lips, but he tried to ignore that. Yet at some point neither couldn’t ignore it anymore: Arledge’s teeth had caught on the scab and pulled it open, causing it to bleed once again.

»Arledge«, Pan pulled back.

»I can fix that«, he whispered, gently stroked with his thumb over the lips and a warmth spread from it as the wound closes.

Pan smiled: »Thank you.«

»Always. Maybe I should keep you here«, he chuckled

»You can’t. And you know that. I’m nothing one can hold on to for long.«

»I know. You are too special to be trapped in one place.«

Pan stroked down his neck and both got lost in the other’s eyes.

And as Pan’s body was at rest now, the exhaustion returned. He didn’t really want to sleep again, but the sickness was still taking it’s toll.

Arledge noticed how tiredness crept into his eyes and gently he stroked over his hair: »Sleep, Pan. Tomorrow will be another day.«

»Will you stay here?«

»Gladly.«

Pan closed his eyes and soon drifted away.

 

Pan got woken when Arledge freed himself from his arms and swam out of the room.

»Mytian?«, a distant voice called.

»Coming! Coming«, Alredge called back.

The two voices started talking with each other and Pan couldn’t understand them anymore. But he was curious and maybe, since he was still half asleep, he forgot his manners, so he pushed himself off the bed and followed slowly.

As he came closer to the doorway, the voices became clearer again.

»...find the Aboleth. The ship has landed in Qrante. They have a couple more injuries, the amount of people they reported missing fits with how many we found but all in all they were quite lucky.«

»Thank you. Did you send out the message?«, Arledge spoke.

»I did. I also spoke with some of the sailors and informed them about the survivor you have. They have handed me his belongings but I had them stashed in the postal office for him to gather once he’s better.«

Pan stopped in the doorway. The person reporting was an older Triton equipped with a coral spear.

»Good. Have you heard about Oenethera?«

»No, not yet. How is your-«, the triton looked over and spotted Pan, »Oh.«

Arledge turned too: »Pan! You should remain in bed!«

»Sorry, I-«, Pan started but didn’t know what he wanted to say. He looked at the Triton: »Thank you for securing my things. I appreciate it.«

»Of course. It’s good you’re doing better«, he nodded but his eyes still scanned over Pan critical. Pan became painfully aware once again that he was just wearing a pair of pants and his sword by his side. And that he had a thick bandage around his abdomen, the brace around his knee as well as too many black and blue bruises.

»Do you need anything?«, Arledge asked him.

»Maybe some water.«

»I’ll bring it over in a moment. Please, go back and rest.«

Pan nodded and left them, returning to the bed again.

 

After some time, Arledge returned with a glass of water in an air bubble.

»Here«, he said dryly.

»Sorry for intruding.« Pan took it and drank.

»It’s alright. I’m not mad at you or anything. Just annoyed by what he told me.«

»What did he tell you?«

»Not much, that’s the problem. They didn’t find the Aboleth or heard from Oenethera.«

»Are they normally hard to find?«

»They are. I just hope they aren’t too far away and will be found soon. For your sake.«

Pan nodded: »And about the Aboleth?«

»Nothing about it«, he said dismissively.

Pan raised an eyebrow but stayed silent. He handed Arledge back the glass: »Thank you.«

Arledge nodded and left again. There was something he wasn’t telling Pan, something to do with the Aboleth. But Pan wouldn’t be pushing, instead he laid down again. He felt cold and started shivering. The only thing remotely like a blanket was his coat but since the water got into everything even inside of his coat he still was cold after wrapping it around him. He curled up, as far as he could without pain from the wound setting in.

 

A little while later, Arledge found Pan shivering inside the coat.

»Pan, are you alright?«

Pan was half asleep and didn’t react to the question.

Arledge moved closer and gently shook his shoulder: »Pan?«

Pan groaned lightly and moved his head towards him. There was a dazed look in his eyes and Arledge wasn’t sure if it was from being half asleep or from the sickness.

»Pan?«

»What is it?«, his speech was slurred.

»Are you alright? You look worse again.«

»Just a bit cold«, another shiver ran over him.

Arledge reached out and touched his arm – Pan wasn’t warm, he was as cold as the water which is to say very cold. Immediately Arledge’s pulse flared up with worry.

»How long have you felt like this?«

»It never was warm down here. Now it’s just… worse...«, he muttered, still not fully there.

Arledge pulled him up and to his side: »There’s a thermal vent not that far from here. You need some warmth. Why didn’t you say something?«

Pan groaned, his head lolling against Arledge’s shoulder.

»Dammit, Pan!« Arledge swam as quickly as he could while Pan was like dead weight in his arms once again.

For Pan the world rushed past him. He still felt half asleep and wasn’t fully sure why Arledge was panicking. Just because he was a bit cold? Wasn’t that better than him burning up with fever from an infected wound? Either way, he had neither the mind or the strength to resist.

Arledge was going as fast as he could. But it still felt like it took forever until the thermal vent came into view and then still longer for him to actually arrive there. But finally the water became warmer and the panic slowly subsided. Arledge took Pan closer and after a bit he finally stopped shivering. He eventually also pulled away from Arledge and moved closer to the vent.

»Be careful. Don’t get too close or you’ll get burned.«

Pan nodded and laid himself on the seafloor, looking up. Arledge sat beside him after a couple of seconds.

Finally Pan said: »I didn’t realize just how cold I was.«

»I should have thought about it. Normally I don’t have non-aquatic patients down here. The water is pretty cold and can cool you down in a bad way.«

»I’m not blaming you«, Pan said soft.

»I am. After the air bubble ran out and this now, I must be a really bad caretaker.«

»Shut up«, Pan’s voice was suddenly harsh.

»What?«

»Shut up. I don’t need your self-pity. You saved my life. You are still saving it. You are doing great even if you are messing up once or twice.«
»Pan...«

»Or just shut up because I am not doing well and can’t deal with another person’s problem right now.«

»That is fair. Can I do anything else for you?«

»No. I just need to stay here for a while.«

»Of course. However long you need. If you want to sleep more you can do that.«

»Maybe a bit.« Pan put his arm over his eyes, but he did not sleep.

 

»I hate feeling like this«, Pan eventually spoke up, his arm still over his eyes.

»Like how?«

»Helpless. Out of control.«

»I get that. I’m sorry.«

»You don’t need to apologize. I’m glad it is you. But I still hate it.«

»I wish I could do more.«

»You are doing everything I could ask for.«

»Trying at least«, he exhaled, »Are you feeling better?«

»Yeah. Can I stay here?«

»It’s not save around here.«

»I can stand my own in a fight«, Pan almost sounded like a sulky child.

»Do I need to poke your stomach?«

He grunted.
