»I trust that you can normally do that but not right now. Right now it would be better if you would stay in a save environment. But we can return here anytime.« Arledge stroked through Pan’s hair.

Pan swiped his arm up and hit Arledge’s as he barked sharp: »Don’t treat me like a child.«

»Sorry.«

»I started adventuring to get away from people who treat me like I am incapable of wiping my own ass. Just because I am sick and hurt now doesn’t mean I loose all my abilities.«

»Sorry… I just like your hair.«

That made Pan laugh lightly and his expression softened.

»I am insufferable when I’m in pain«, he muttered to himself.

»Are you in pain right now?«

»A bit. But I’m also just stretched out from being in constant pain.«

»Should I try to give a bit more healing?«

Pan shrugged: »I don’t think you can bring it far enough.«

»Probably not. But some relief might still help you«, he floated up and over Pan, clearly fretting.

Pan shrugged again: »Go ahead and try.«

Before he could reach down, a pod of orcas swum up to them, singing. Arledge looked up to them, mumbled something again and then sang himself like the orcas were doing.

One peeled off closer to them and they seemed to speak with each other. Arledge swam up a bit too and as they talked he became visibly excited. A smaller orca peeled off the group too and approached Pan curious who pushed himself off the seafloor too. The animal was already as large as Pan and it chattered before swimming towards him quickly and pushing into him – right into his chest and stomach. Pan grunted, pushed himself away sideways but the baby returned for another charge. Pan tried dodging but in the water, with the coat dragging after him and against an excellent swimmer like that, he had no chance. Again the orca pushed into him and swam a circle before Pan could push himself off again. The baby chattered excited, which made Arledge look over to see Pan clutching his stomach once more. Arledge seemed to yell in the orca’s language, shooing away the baby and coming over to Pan, laying an arm around him protectively: »Are you alright?«

»Right where it hurt«, Pan grumbled.

»It just wanted to play. It’s sorry«, Arledge spoke and Pan noticed the baby swimming up again, but much slower now.

»It’s alright«, Pan answered and forced a smile, before reaching out a hand towards the orca who approached slowly and then swam past Pan’s hand letting him stroke it.

Arledge chattered something and the baby chattered back, then it quickly dashed off towards it’s family who was already leaving.

»They informed me that Oenethera was found. They are some distance away, but will probably arrive tomorrow«, Arledge said after a moment of looking after the pod.

»That’s good to hear«, Pan’s voice was filled with pain.

»Let me...«, Arledge softly put his hand on the bandage and mumbled some words. A warmth spread from his fingers into the wound and the pain receded. Pan allowed himself a full breath.

»Thank you.«

»Always. Do you want to stay here longer or should we head back?«

»We can head back, I think.«

»Alright. But tell me the moment you start freezing again.«

Pan nodded and together they swam back. Again Arledge did most of the swimming but at least Pan was trying this time.

 

Arledge returned him to the bed: »Can I do anything else for you? Would you like to eat something?«

»Not right now. I think I’ll try to sleep a bit again.«

»That’s a good idea: plenty of rest. I’ll be in the other room. Just call if you need anything.«

Pan nodded and laid down again as Arledge was leaving the room. But he stopped in the doorway and looked back at Pan who had put his arm over his eyes again.

»Pan?«

Pan raised his arm: »Yeah?«

Arledge hesitated but eventually did ask: »Are you upset with me?«

»What? Why would I be?«, Pan asked back confused.

»Because I left in a hurry before? I woke you and then rudely sent you away when you came looking what was up.«

»No. I’m not. Not at all.«

»Then why didn’t you say you were that cold?«, Arledge returned a bit into the room again.

»I don’t think I realized it was that bad. I was hoping my coat would help as it always did but...«

»Not down here.«

»No. Not in the water«, Pan nodded, »I realized that.«

»And you are sure I did not upset you?«

»I am sure. And if I were, the repeated rescue would have made me forget it«, he gave a dry laugh.

»Alright. Good. Would you prefer if I stayed here?«, Arledge asked a bit scared.

»You can go do your things. I don’t mind.«

Arledge nodded and after another moment of hesitation left. Pan put his arm over his eyes again and suppressed a sigh.

Notes:

Sorry, this story got abandoned. I promise I wanted to finish it but the muses left me.

Chapter 33: Revenge

Summary:

Equadorius takes revenge on Pan stealing and using his true name

Notes:

18 May 2024 - 1247 words

Chapter Text

I went to bed feeling depressed. I had hurt Equadorius in a way I had never intended to and worst of all, I could fully understand his hurt. I had used his name to force him to let go of the feelings he had for me and then I had been stupid enough to summon him into the Fey Wild, letting him know what I had done. I had taken the tiny bit of freedom Equadorius had and had taken that away too.

Later when Equadorius’ mother had appeared to take back her son’s name I had given it for a cheap prize and instead had asked her to deliver my apologies to him. I hoped desperately I would be forgiven but I knew I had no right to it.

I had tried meditating, but it hadn’t helped with my mood so soon I bedded down in the tent that he had summoned. I Normally liked sleeping under the stars but I didn’t have the nerve to face anyone, not even my group.

 

I got ripped out of sleep as my limbs were pulled apart by hard grips. For a moment I struggled, tired calling out but roots wrapped around my jaw and mouth the same way they were wrapping around my wrists and ankles. A smell like a thunderstorm laid in the air and a person with glowing green eyes stepped over me.

»I don’t want a word out of you«, a familiar voice said. I wasn’t sure if I should panic more or less over his appearance. »I should be ripping you apart. I’m not someone anyone can boss around. Your treatment of me is nothing that can be easily forgiven.«

I looked up, my gaze turning sad. I nodded slightly, after all I couldn’t say anything.

Equadorius sighed: »You say you did it because it’s better for the both of us. Make me understand why. Maybe then I can attempt to grant you your ask.«

I looked at him, still unable to speak.

»But how could I even trust you? Every word you have spoken had been a lie«, he muttered desperate.

The gag lifted but the roots laid themselves around my throat instead.

»Most of my words had been the truth, I swear on my real name«, I spoke slowly.

He scoffed.

»And I swear, anything that I will say tonight won’t be lies either.«

»Go on then: explain yourself«, he demanded sharp.

»You see freedom in me and my life, but I am barely freer than you. Even if I would like to: I cannot give you what you want or wish for. You would just move from one cage into the next.«

»Speak clear.«

»I wear the reminder of my own shackles around my neck. The key… It doesn’t hold my freedom though.«

»Speak clearer!«, he commanded again and the roots around my throat tightened.

»Back when we first met, I told you to pretend I was of your status… It wasn’t pretend.«

The roots tightened more.

»I will reign myself one day«, I pressed out, but they continued to tighten.

»You swore to tell the truth«, he growled.

»I am noble. Unwrap my sword hilt«, I chocked out. Finally the roots eased up and I could breathe properly again. They stayed tight enough to be an active reminder of what he could do though.

Equadorius bowed down and pulled my sword, opened the knot of the wrapping and after a few moments the beautiful carved handle appeared.

»This proves nothing. You could have stolen it«, he noted sharp.

»P T B J. The initials in the handle. Those are my initials: Pan Thanis… and so on.«

»Liar«, he put the tip of my sword against my throat: »If Thanis were your real name, it would have held more power.«

»It’s… It’s not the full name, yes. But they are parts of my name. That’s how I managed to pick up the same name multiple times and destroy your economy.«

He considered for a moment, eventually removing the sword from my throat: »Who are you then?«

»I can’t tell you.«

»Can’t or don’t want to?«, he asked strident.

»Don’t want to. It’s for my safety and my family’s.«

He scoffed again and said with a dark note: »I could force you to tell me.«

And again the roots were moving around my throat – but they weren’t tightening this time.

»But you won’t«, I said like it was a fact.

»I should hate you«, his voice was still dark.

I just looked at him: there was no need to speak words as blatantly obvious. Yet the silence left me scared.

Equadorius was motionless like a cat waiting for the right time to pounce. It was the silence before a storm; the distance thunder rolling before hell broke loose.

And then everything happened all at once: The roots gagged me again, he tossed my sword away and descended onto me, clawing at my chest with fingernails sharp as razors. He ripped open my shirt, tore into my chest and a muffled scream escaped me.

He dug further and further in blind fury. The amount of times I had experienced pain on a similar scale had been few. Tears were rolling from my eyes unstoppable. He could be ripping out my still beating heart and watch it stop in his hands – had he wanted to.

And for a moment I feared he did want to. I felt my last breath escaping, the world going dim around me as tension left my body. But I wasn’t done with life yet and wouldn’t be giving up so easily.

A white light flashed above me and I sucked in air sharply again after the moment of stillness. I didn’t try to free myself from the bindings. Part of my brain was convinced I deserved his wrath. Part of it was even glad for it.

But my sudden stillness and return to life had knocked some sense into Equadorius. He stopped and inhaled shakily, looking at my open chest for a moment. Then bowed forward hurriedly, putting his palms on them. Warm, soothing healing magic coursed over it and closed all the wounds he had just caused. The roots binding me dropped away too, but I didn’t dare to move.

Still pressing his hands onto my chest he leaned down further and kissed me.

I turned my face away, but I could still taste the salt of his tears – and mine.

»I am sorry. I am sorry. I am sorry«, he repeated breathless, his lips brushing over my cheek.

»You should leave«, I told him, my voice dry.

»Thanis, please.«

I still didn’t look at him: »Leave, please.«

He kissed the base of my neck: »I love you.«

I recoiled from the touch and finally reached upwards, grabbing his shoulders before pushing him away gently. He resisted.

»I told you to let go of the feeling you had for me. Can you understand why I did that?«

He started kissing my wrist instead, in a desperate attempt to get me back.

»If you don’t go I will steal your name again and make you«, my words came out sharp. Finally he paled and pulled back.

»I am sorry. Truly«, he husked, tears streaking his face.

»Go!«, I pushed him away.

He looked at me sad, stood up and vanished in the smell of the first spring bloom.

I turned to my side and curled up, sobbing.

Chapter 34: Fluff

Summary:

Pan invites Arkaniest on the ball of his birthday.

Notes:

04 June 2024 - 5533 words

Chapter Text

Quietly did Pan unsling his sword and put it down beside the bed. Arkaniest was almost asleep on it and he tried not to disturb her as he crept into the bed. But he couldn't stop himself from leaning down for a kiss, gracing it onto her temple.

She smiled softly at that and without any malice she teased: »So you can be soft.«

»I know how to treat a lady right«, he almost seemed to purr as he answered and smiled. Of course he smiled. He always did. But tonight it was honest and soft: the smile of a person perfectly content.

He laid down beside her, putting an arm over her as she turned to face him.

»I am not a lady«, Arkaniest objected but smiled too.

»You are when you are with me«, he gave back and moved in for a kiss. His touch was gentle, his lips sweet and his body warm. And as Arkaniest stroke over his chest, shoulders and arms, she realized how relaxed he was. He, Pan, who always had his guard up and his sword in reach. Who was always ready to fight or run or lie. He was relaxed now, not a tense muscle in his body and his eyes closed lazily.

Arkaniest moved a hand into his soft hair that hung into his face. It was a bit longer on one side: a natural wave hiding the difference normally but so very obvious once you ran your fingers through it.

Pan smiled and kissed her wrist softly. It tickled her lightly but it was a good tickle.

»I would have never thought you could be this gentle«, Arkaniest admitted while he traced her hairline with a light touch.

»Would you want me to not be gentle?«, he teased, his brown eyes seeming endless in the dim light.

»No. You just keep surprising me.« She paused for a moment, then noted: »You are like Rosend Lake«

»A kraken hides inside me?«, he chuckled.

»Yeah, in a way. Right now you are so still. The surface like a mirror, capable of reflecting the stars. But I know what hides inside.« She traced his chest, her hand catching on the necklace that held the key.

»And it is impressive how you can be so calm with all that hiding inside of you.«

»It’s you. You calm me«, he smiled and it was the kind of smile that made you feel like standing in the warm morning sun.

»Why me? What makes me different?«, she asked.

»I don’t know. But when I am with you I know someone will catch me should I stumble.

You might not know everything, but you still support me. You make sure I don’t burn myself up. You somehow know how to deal with the monster inside of me.« He lowered his gaze almost ashamed but he added: »And I love you for it.«

Arkaniest smiled, pushed forward and kissed him softly. Her hand remained on his chest and she felt his heartbeat: strong and assured.

He continued: »I had stopped hoping I would find someone who would make me feel like that. I felt passion with a lot of people: passion like an oil fire. But I never felt like this…

»I feel safe when I am around you. For the first time, I care less about fooling around and more about just being beside you, holding you and hopefully never having to let go.«

»You don’t have to let me go. I will stay by your side, if you let me«, she said.

He exhaled and it seemed heavy. A trace element of fear crept inside his eyes.

»Tell me what scares you«, Arkaniest offered. But as she did, she could feel something inside of him tensing up.

»Not yet. One day, you will know. But let me hold on to the fantasy that things will be easy like this forever.«

»Alright«, Arkaniest put her hand on his cheek and he smiled, relaxing again.

»I do love you, Arkaniest«, he smiled.

»I love you too, Pan Thanis.«

He chuckled.

 

 

It was early winter when Arkaniest received the invitation. It came straight from the Duke and Duchess of Millguard and it was for a ball in their home.

The invitation confused her: it had been over a year since she had been in Millguard and she had talked with the two rulers a total of one (1) time(s). And there was no letter attached to explain anything.

When Pan came back from a missing a couple of days later, she could finally ask him.

»Look what I got«, she said and showed him the invitation.

He read through the invitation and started grinning: »You have made quite the impression last year it seems.«

»Do you know anything about that ball?«

»I think it’s the one where they try to match their son and find him a wife?«, he shrugged, »But I wouldn’t bet on it.«

»I’m not noble, why would they even consider me?«

»Desperation? The ball is held every year for like ten years now? They must have gone through every noble bachelorette in the land.«

»Really?«

»I don’t know. It’s just what I think I heard? Do you want to go?«

»It might be exciting even if I am not looking to be matched with a Duke-son.« She smiled at him and laid her hand on his neck tenderly.

»If you want to go, I will accompany you. And should that Duke-son take a liking in you, we can fight over you«, there was a twinkle of shenanigans in his eyes.

»That would hardly be a fair fight, would it?«

»I guess you have nothing to fear then«, he grinned and kissed her.

After it, she stepped back and looked at him doubtful: »Why would you want me to go anyway? What if I do find someone better than you?«

»As if there is anyone better than me«, he teased. »But maybe I just like it because it gives me a reason to travel with you.«

»That is indeed a good reason«, she smiled back and put her arms around his waist. He did the same with her and for few moments they both just enjoyed the other’s presence.

 

 

It was the day of the ball and Arkaniest awoke by the smell of breakfast and the sound of a door being pushed closed.

Pan came over and pushed her gently on the cheek, he had a tray in his hands: »Good morning, sleepyhead.«

She smiled, stretched and sat up. He placed the tray on her lap: Pancakes, bacon, coffee.

»You are too good too me«, she smiled and gave back the kiss.

»I need to make sure no noble can steal you today.«

»Can’t you join me? Be my plus one?«, she asked not for the first time.

»I don’t think that is how it works«, he repeated the same answer he gave her every time.

Arkaniest pouted playfully and bit off a piece of bacon.

»I will go over to Carleone today while you are busy. But we will see each other tonight, hopefully.«

»I will be there«, she assured him. He smiled. Again, there was that twinkle of mischief In his eyes but she hadn’t been successful in tickling it out of him.

This morning, she didn’t try. And he took off the necklace with the key and put it around her head: »Hold on to that today. So you don’t forget you already are the keeper of the key to my heart«, he told her and placed a kiss on her forehead.

Arkaniest blushed hard at those words but before she could even begin to think of an answer, he had already pulled back. Only now that he picked up his bag, did she notice he was already fully dressed.

»Are you leaving already?«, she asked disappointed.

»You will be fine without me«, he answered.

»At least let me give you a good-bye kiss«, she said a bit sulky.

He returned to her and they shared a long, passionate kiss during which Arkaniest took his hand. She didn’t let go, when he finally pulled back. But after some more moments, he pulled free and left her alone.

She had to eat alone, then took her sweet time with getting ready. One of the previous days they had bought a wonderful dress for the ball and despite being happy to finally wear it, she was mostly happy about having Pan’s key. He had never told her what it was for but since he always wore it, it had to be important to him. And now he had entrusted it to her.

 

 

In the evening, Arkaniest headed to the mansion. She arrived between carriages delivering well dressed nobles, often from two generations. She reminded herself of the noble family her mother had served: the parents would barter while the children courted. Why then had the young Duke not already been married? From what Arkaniest had learned he was well over 20 – old for a human Duke to be unmarried.

Despite her arriving on the foot and her low birth, she had no trouble entering the party when she showed the invitation. She entered a nicely decorated ballroom where two dozen people already mingled – not even half of them were the age to marry the young Duke: probably the parents.

The Nobles were of all races and there even the ›young‹ ones where of all ages. There was an Aarakocra Lady and an Elf Lord – they must have been a hundred years apart. But Arkaniest couldn’t really speak herself. She herself was older than the current Duke and Duchess.

Arkaniest was looking for those two. She hoped she could get some answers from them: why they had invited her for example. And she also looked for any other familiar faces even if she doubted she would see any.

 

But before she had luck with either of those searches, a well dressed gentleman approached her: »What a beautiful decoration«, he told her, looking at Ticker who was sleeping over her shoulders like a shawl.

She gave the man a light curtsy: »Thank you, although it is no decoration.«

»No?«

Ticker cracked open an eye and looked at him. The Lord laughed impressed: »Marvelous! Who has made it? Maybe I shall commission something similar too.«

»I made Ticker«, she smiled and scratched the metal dragon who stretched.

»Oh, it’s a clockwork animal! And you really made it? Might I know your name?«, he asked enthralled.

»Arkaniest Karnista«, she offered him her hand to shake. But instead of shaking it, he took it and bowed down to kiss it.

»Edwillas Sontill. A pleasure«, he smiled. »I haven’t heard of the Karnista family yet. Where are you from, if I might ask?«

»I am not actually from a noble family«, she admitted, »I am an adventurer.«

»Curious. I didn’t know the Jazrueltehd’s have started to invite commoners.«

»It was per my own request«, a familiar voice spoke behind Arkaniest.

Edwillas bowed quickly and deeply: »Lord Jazrueltehd! I apologize. I did not mean any offense.«

»Of course you didn’t«, the Lord answered but his tone was disapproving.

Arkaniest had turned in the meantime. It took her brain a solid minute to understand what she was seeing: before her she stood possibly the best dressed man of the whole ball. He wore something akin to a dress uniform in deep green and gold: the colors of Millguard. The sword by his side had an artfully carved handle and on the man hung different pieces of jewelry, some almost looking like medals on a frock.

But despite the get-up, Pan’s familiar and mischievous grin sat on his face.

After the full minute, all Arkaniest could manage was: »What?«

Pan bowed before her: »Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd. It is my pleasure. Might I ask you for this dance?«

He offered her his hand and overwhelmed with the situation, but also shackled by the formalities of this event, she took it. He brought her over to the dance floor and they started with the slow waltz.

»You liar!«, Arkaniest finally whisper-yelled at him as they were dancing.

»You are my first dance of tonight. You look magnificent and beautiful«, he ignored her accusations, talking quietly. She blushed.

»Don’t- I’m angry at you.«

»I don’t think you actually are. You suspected the truth for so long already«, his words were soft.

»And you lied in my face every time I asked you about it! Up to and including this morning«, she hissed. Then she suppressed a sigh while exhaling: »No. I suppose I’m not. But I’m still annoyed with you.«

»That much is fair«, he chuckled. And it was the same chuckle he always had, even if he didn’t look at all like he normally did. His longer strand of hair was braided into a short braid.

»Did you really want me here?«, she eventually asked.

»Yes. My parents technically wanted to invite you last year already after you met them, but I told them not to. I hadn’t been sure I could trust you with that secret yet.«

»When did you change your mind?«

»When I started wishing you were here to support me. And when we then became serious. Arkaniest, you don’t know how glad I am that you finally know. I want to kiss you so badly right now«, he whispered and she blushed again.

»So many things make actual sense now«, she muttered after a moment and put her forehead against his chest.

Pan was painfully aware that basically everyone was watching them and therefore witnessing the intimacy between them. And they would be whispering how atypical that was for him. But he just closed his eyes and continued dancing with her, giving her time to process.

When the song neared it’s end he spoke up again: »You will need to make a decision...«

»A decision? What do you mean?«

»If the young Lord Panagiotis starts to court Lady Arkaniest – or if this just stays between Pan and Arki.« He paused for a moment: »But you don’t need to decide now. Or soon for that matter. Well, maybe soon… But not right now.«

Arkaniest nodded slowly.

And the dance came to an end.

»I will need to keep up appearances tonight«, he told her as he lead her off the dance floor. »But you have my key and nobody else, so you don’t need to worry about anything.«

He bowed down to kiss her hand. It was a soft, warm touch, honest and truthful but still proper in these circumstances. Her heart fluttered as it did so often with him. But then he let go and left her behind.

A passing servant basically put a glass of wine into her hand while she was still caught in the stun of being left behind.

She didn’t really have much time to be stunned though. After a couple of moments she basically got swarmed by a gaggle of bachelors all wanting to know this mysterious lady who seemed to have caught the young Lord’s eye.

It was a good distraction from Pan but then she got a glimpse of him laughing with a young Lady and a sting of jealousy went right through her.

Arkaniest composed herself: she knew better than to trust appearances like that. She new Pan better.

 

Later on, Arkaniest took a break from socializing on the balcony. It was cold and the moment she stepped outside she knew she couldn’t stay long but for the moment she enjoyed the peaceful silence.

When the noise level increased for a moment, she knew someone had joined her and she hoped it was Pan. But the person stepped beside her and didn’t put his arm around her waist like he normally did.

»I swear, I’m coming out here every year. And every year it’s freezing.«

Arkaniest looked over and recognized the Duke.

»Lord-«, she curtsied quickly but he waved her off.

»There is no need to be so formal. I once was like you.«

»How do you mean?«

He turned around and leaned against the stone banister: »I was born a farmer. There is not a drop of noble blood in my veins.«

Arkaniest had to laugh: »Pan always said his father was a farmer. He was actually serious.«

The Duke just smiled and nodded. After a moment of silence he said: »I never saw him so calm with anyone before. It’s like all the noise in his brain quiets down when he’s with you.«

Arkaniest smiled: »He did say that once.«

»My son hates this event. He hates having to become Duke one day. But he will do it because he has the strongest sense of duty I’ve ever seen.«

Arkaniest hummed agreeing.

»He kept this life from you this long, not because he didn’t trust you with it but to keep you from that burden. He can’t imagine you – or anyone – would want to enter this life if they haven’t been born into it.«

»I have a choice to make«, I said, remembering Pan’s words from before.

The Duke nodded: »But you have the choice. Either way will bring hardship: stand with him and become Duchess, become noble with all the advantages or disadvantages. Or stay away from that but then you will need to leave him eventually.«

Arkaniest grunted. Neither option sounded truly appealing.

»You won’t need to decide now«, the Duke also said: »Not today, not even this week. Panagiotis will continue to travel for a few years. But one day you will need to make it and I can tell you: it’s not an easy one...«

»Did you ever regret your decision?«

»No. The only thing I regret is not having more children to give Panagiotis that choice too. But the gods didn’t bless us with more.«

The Duke noticed Arkaniest shivering and softly said: »Let’s return inside before you freeze to death.«

»I am far from that, but yes«, she chuckled and they went inside.

The Duke headed to his wife who received him lovingly. They exchanged some word and as Arkaniest watched him, she noticed many of Pan’s mannerisms in them – including his gentleness. Pan had often told her, his parents truly loved each other and she could see that in their gentle touches too.

The same gentle touches Pan had been sharing with her.

The Duchess looked over at her and gave her a warm smile. She returned it but then got distracted as someone stroke over her lower back lovingly.

She looked back and Pan smiled as he walked past.

»Are you alright? You can leave if you want«, he whispered.

»I’m alright.

»I would leave if I could«, he gave her pained look but his smile never wavered.

»Go back to your duties, my lord«, she teased him and gave him a light push.

»Maybe just one more dance?«

»Later tonight«, she smiled and his eyes light up at that. But then he move away again.

 

The rest of the evening, they both talked and danced with the different guests. Another time Pan stole her from her current dance partner receiving dark glances from him. But Pan was obviously the highest ranking bachelor here and nobody dared to speak against him.

 

 

 

Eventually the ball came to a close and more and more people left. Some who remained had started to annoy Arkaniest, so she too decided to leave. She headed over to Pan, putting a hand on his shoulder: »I’m going home«, she told me.

He turned around, took her hand and kissed it in a bow: »It was an honor to have you here.«

»Thank you for the invite. It was somewhat fun.«

He chuckled and winked at her: »Have a good evening.«

»You too.«

She curtsied before him and then left him. As she was heading out, a servant approached her, speaking quietly: »Panagiotis asks if I should guide you to his chambers.«

Arkaniest blinked at him. She realized it was the same servant who had handed her the glass: a tall winter elf with long, white hair and beads braided into it.

Then she broke out in a smile: »I would like that!«

»If you would follow me then«, a light smile played on his lips as he gestured her away.

She did follow him and the man guided her through the mansion, upstairs, and eventually opening a door. Arkaniest entered a large room with a seating area, a few bookshelves, and a large bed. Two more doors led away from it, one wall was closed off by a heavy curtain.

»To the right, you will find the bathroom«, the servant told her, »Will you require anything else right now?«

»Was it Pan’s idea we stay the night?«

»I believe so.«

»Would it be possible to borrow a nightgown then?«

»Of course, I can bring you one. Alternatively...«, there was the same mischievous glint in his eyes she knew from Pan: »I won’t be here to stop you looking through his wardrobe«, he gestured at a hidden sliding door in the wall to the right.

Arkaniest chuckled: »That is actually a good idea.«

»Anything else I can help you with?«, he asked, formally again.

»No, not at the moment.«

»If something does come up, you can ring that bell other there and I will come.«

»Who are you? If I might ask.«

»My name is Nathaniel. I am Panagiotis’ valet.«

»Personal servant«, Arkaniest muttered, »That makes sense.«

He laughed out at that, his wit obviously as quick as Pan’s.

»Well, if you don’t have anything else, I will head back to the ball.«

»Of course. Thank you«, Arkaniest gave him an honest smile and he returned it. Then he gave a light bow and left her alone in Pan’s room.

 

Arkaniest started to look around closer. Over one chair hung Pan’s coat, sword scabbard and bag. Otherwise the room seemed cold and barely lived in. It made sense: Arkaniest knew where he had been after all.

She shook her head: Pan actually was the Duke-son, heir to these lands. So many things made sense now: his avoidance of certain people and topics; his wide-reaching education; why it sometimes seemed like he had the world on his shoulders. But in return: other things made less sense now.

But either way, she wanted to get out of the dress and lay down. She went over to his wardrobe and opened it. It had rather typical wardrobe contents for a noble, she thought, many pieces in Millguard’s green and gold. After a bit, she found a white shirt and headed into the bathroom to change. It was an almost pompous room with running water and marbled fixtures. It too was overwhelming, but she chose not to get stunned again and instead took advantage of it. She washed herself and changed into Pan’s shirt. But it wasn’t particularly warm and the rooms were rather cold.

Arkaniest dashed out and put on Pan’s coat too. It had always been beautifully warm but it rarely left his person when it was actually cold outside. Now it was hers. She smiled.

 

Back on the ball, Nathaniel gave Panagiotis a nod and Panagiotis brightened up instantly. It was unusual for Nathaniel to see his master this happy, but it just made him happy too. Nat knew how much Pan had suffered under his inability to find a connection as deep and honest as that but now Pan seemed to float through that ball that he normally hated with all his heart.

Nathaniel looked over to Panagiotis’ parents who also watched their son with delight. They not only saw a source of happiness in Miss Karnista but finally a possible wife and future Duchess; and with that: security of their bloodline and reign.

 

 

When the evening finally closed out fully, Pan said good night to his parents and got excited thinking about who was waiting for him. He was slightly afraid of her reaction to his revelation but that she had agreed to stay in his chambers was a good sign in his mind.

Naturally Nathaniel followed him upstairs to help him undress and take worn clothes away to be washed.

»Nat, I won’t need you tonight«, Pan tried sending him away.

»But there are still open questions«, Nathaniel grinned, teasing him.

»What open questions?«, Pan raised an eyebrow. There was a hint of a sign in his voice but he still grinned: Nathaniel was just as bad as he was.

»W§hat would you two like for breakfast for example. And where would you like it be served?«

»Something with bacon. In bed. But don’t you care wake us.«

»Of course not. Shall I send someone to the inn to retrieve both of your things?«

Pan considered for a moment, then answered: »I’ll tell you in the morning.«

A moment later they reached Panagiotis’ chambers and Pan stepped through the door quickly, stepping into Nathaniel’s way. Nathaniel still tried to follow but Pan put a hand on his chest and stopped him. Pan was grinning broadly and quietly told him: »You are done for today. I will be fine.«

»As you wish. Have fun and sleep well«, Nathaniel chuckled and pulled the door closed.

Panagiotis looked through the dimly light room. Arkaniest had snuggled herself in the bed and Pan wasn’t sure if she was already sleeping or not. He decided not to risk waking her, and started to undress himself silently. He threw the uncomfortable robes over the armchair by the wardrobe and noticed movement behind him. But he didn’t turn yet, instead continued buttoning open his shirt jacket and when he took it off, hands reached up to take it from him.

»You know, I can take my own clothes off«, he said with a low voice and chuckling.

»Since when do you dislike it when I undress you?«, Arkaniest put her arms around his waist, leaning against his back. Pan could feel her warmth and the edges of his coat fell around him. Arkaniest started to open the latch on his belt. Pan put his hands on her wrists and turned around into a kiss. He could feel her smiling through it.

Pan wrapped his arms around her waist too and, while still kissing her, pulled her off the floor. He carried her over to the bench and threw her in again.

Only then he saw she was just wearing his coat and a shirt of his. His smile became even bigger.

He took off the sword, hanging it sheathed on a hook on the bed frame. Then he followed Arkaniest onto the bed and was reciprocate with loving kisses and gentle touches.

For a good while, no words were necessary. They knew each other, knew how to make the other happy and how to be happy. It was the first time, Pan had a good night following the yearly ball. But it was also the first time someone participated that he actually loved.

And for a while he didn’t worry about the future or her decision. He didn’t think much at all in this time, while she was with him. She was maybe the only person he felt truly calm around.

But eventually Arkaniest fell asleep in Pan’s arms. He noticed her drifting away and kissed the top of her head, smiling. She fitted perfectly in to his arms and he enjoyed every night they fell asleep together like that. This was where he belonged.

It didn’t take long for him to follow her into sleep.

 

 

The next morning, Pan got woken by soft kisses on his shoulders. He kissed Arkaniest’s head: »Good morning, beautiful.«

She smiled up to him: »I could get used to this bed.«

»Should I make it even better?«, he asked and grinned.

»Is that even possible?«

He grinned even wider and rolled onto her, kissing her passionately. And while she was distracted, he reached over to the nightstand and rang the bell standing on it.

It didn’t have a clapper, so she couldn’t hear it and with her eyes closed she didn’t see his action either.

Pan counted the seconds in his head and eventually pushed up, sitting over Arkaniest.

She tried to wrap her arms around his shoulders and pull him back down but he was unmovable and just grinned at her.

Moments later, there was a knock on the door.

»Enter«, he called and sat back further.

Nathaniel entered with two trays in his arms: »Good morning, I bring you your breakfast.«

Arkaniest had an expression of confusion, surprise and overwhelm.

»Thank you, Nat«, Pan grinned and now moved to sit beside Arkaniest so Nathaniel could hand them both the trays.

When Arkaniest saw what was being serves, she swatted Pan lightly: »You are dumb.«

»Rest assured, that is no news to him«, Nathaniel teased Pan, causing Pan to laugh out.

»I heard that is my best quality.«

The other two both ignored him, Nathaniel stepped back and addressed Arkaniest: »Miss Karnista, Do you wish for your things to be retrieved from the tavern?«

»All of them?«, she asked uncertain.

»If you wish to.«

She looked over at Pan who gave a shrug and nod.

»Alright, yeah, sure.«

»I will bring everything here once it is retrieved.«

Arkaniest handed him the room key that was placed on the nightstand and told him the room number.

He bowed lightly: »Anything else I can do for you?«

»Leave please?«, Pan pushed.

Nathaniel smiled, bowed and left.

Arkaniest watched him go, then took the first bites: »It’s delicious!«

»I am glad.«

»I could really get used to all of this!«, she smiled widely. But Pan didn’t answer and when she glanced over she noticed the blank to stormy expression he bore.

»Pan?«

»To this… maybe...«, he answered slowly.

»You don’t want me to enter this life«, she deducted, speaking careful.

»No! No… That is not… it. Listen: I am the last person to try and sell you on a life in court. I know how it is – and I despise it. Yes, there are some upsides – for example, I wouldn’t want to exchange Nat for anything or anyone – but I still prefer to live like a nobody. Pan, the adventurer. Not Panagiotis-Thanassis Bert Jazrueltehd, eleventh Lord, second of the name and so on.

»If I were you, I would run should someone give me the opportunity I give you. The sacrifice you would need to make for a future with me… I don’t know if it’s worth it. I would hate myself if you would become miserable through a decision I made you take. But then again, I am not you. My father likes being a Duke. He likes the fancy balls and everything that I hate. He happily traded in his independence for all of this. If I had siblings...«, he sighed, »I can’t decide for you. And I shouldn’t influence you because I see both options as horrible. In my mind, I am forcing you to step into a pile of shit with either decision: the only question is just which foot do you want to get dirty?

»But you don’t need to decide. Neither today, nor tomorrow, not even in a week. With some luck, you have years to make up your mind… But one day I will put down my adventuring gear and be here. And on that day… on that day you need to decide...«

Arkaniest was silent for a while thinking about his words.

Eventually Pan couldn’t take the silence anymore and continued blathering: »I want you to stay with me forever, Arki. I love you like I haven’t loved anyone ever before. But I don’t want you to give up your freedom either. I am not sure love is worth a life full of regret and things you hate. But you could also regret letting me go the same way. I almost hope your feelings for me won’t-«

Arkaniest turned to him, grabbed his neck and pulled him into a kiss. He tasted sweet from his breakfast.

»Shut up, idiot. You are overthinking«, she told him when she pulled away. He had the eyes of a pleading puppy in that moment. »You just said: I don’t need to decide yet. We will see what will happen until the day I make up my mind, alright?«

»I love you, Arkaniest«, he smiled, seemingly melting after her kiss and words.

»I know. I have the key to your heart after all«, she teased and fingered the key around her neck.

Pan pulled a face: »It’s the key to the box I keep my signet ring in when traveling.«

»Oh...«

»Yeah. Oh. But you won’t need to carry that weight on you longer…«, he started to reach out to her to take it back.

But she put her hand on the key and held on to it: »Since you see it as a weight: I will keep it. Because for me, this is just a tiny key.«

He sat back: »Have I told you that I love you?«

»Not often enough«, she took another bite of bacon and grinned.
